#+TITLE: TEP-Rust Iteration Log
#+STARTUP: overview
#+OPTIONS: toc:2 num:nil

Append-only. One heading per iteration, newest at the bottom. Format and rules
are in =CLAUDE.md=. Record measured numbers, never bare verdicts.

* 2026-08-09 B-0000 Project plan and session protocol
:PROPERTIES:
:ITEM:    B-0000
:PHASE:   0
:TIER:    none
:COMMIT:  (pre-repository)
:STATUS:  DONE
:END:

** What changed
Reviewed the upstream Fortran (=teprob.f=, 1594 lines; =temain_mod.f=, 1413
lines) and the existing Python port, and wrote =PLAN.org=: architecture, a
ten-tier validation ladder, the quirk and delta register, and a nine-phase
roadmap. Added =tep-rust.bib=, =CLAUDE.md=, =BACKLOG.org= and this log. No code
yet; the directory is not a git repository.

** Validation numbers
Six citations verified: five DOIs against CrossRef and one against DataCite, all
with exactly matching titles, authors, years, volumes and pages. No code to
validate yet.

Environment probe: git 2.50.1, cargo/rustc 1.89.0 (Homebrew, *not* rustup),
gfortran 15.2.0 (Homebrew GCC), python3 3.13.0, maturin 1.12.4, cargo-deny
present. =rustup= missing, =trunk= missing, zero wasm targets installed.

** Surprises and decisions
Three findings drove the design and are worth not rediscovering.

=TEFUNC= is not a pure function of =(t, y)=: it advances the disturbance walks,
draws measurement noise, ticks the sampled analyzers, and latches valve sticking
on every call. Harmless under fixed-step Euler, silently wrong under any
multi-stage integrator. Hence the pure/impure split in the plan.

The RNG is bit-reproducible. =G = DMOD(G*9228907, 4294967296)= overflows 2^53, so
rounding is part of the algorithm, but IEEE fixes both the multiply and the
=fmod=. The existing Python port's noise divergence is therefore a *call-ordering*
defect, not an arithmetic one, which is why Tier 3 (RNG call-trace diff) exists
and is cheap.

Exact long-horizon trajectory matching against gfortran is impossible because
=exp= and =pow= differ by ULPs and the closed loop amplifies that. Tier 4 is
diagnostic only. The real gates are Tier 5 (equivalence testing on means *and
variances*, since the Python port's failure mode was variance collapse: reactor
pressure sigma 8.10 against Fortran's 61.48) and Tier 6 (cross-source detector
performance).

Four scoping decisions recorded in =BACKLOG.org= under Decisions.

** Next
B-0001, repository bootstrap. It needs one thing from the user: permission to
install =rustup=, which is required for toolchain pinning and the wasm32 target
and which Homebrew's Rust cannot provide. Ask first, then =git init=, license
with the original attribution preserved, and the toolchain file.

* 2026-08-09 B-0001a Repository bootstrap
:PROPERTIES:

** Preflight
Skipped per protocol: no model code exists, so there is no fidelity to protect.
Baseline was "not a git repository". Recorded gfortran 15.2.0 (Homebrew GCC) so
the first real Tier 1 numbers have a compiler to be attributed to.

** What changed
This is now a git repository on =main=. Added =.gitignore=, =LICENSE=
(BSD-3-Clause for new work), =LICENSE-NCSA= (verbatim upstream), =NOTICE.md=,
and =README.md=. B-0001 was split: the toolchain half is now B-0001b and is
blocked on permission to install =rustup=.

** Validation numbers
None applicable. Ten files tracked, working tree clean after commit.

** Surprises and decisions
The upstream license is *not* BSD-3-Clause, despite the upstream README saying
so. The actual text is the University of Illinois/NCSA Open Source License: an
MIT-style grant carrying three BSD-style conditions, copyright 1998-2002 The
Board of Trustees of the University of Illinois, developed by the Large Scale
Systems Research Laboratory under Prof. Richard D. Braatz.

This has a practical consequence that is easy to miss: the NCSA conditions
require the notice and disclaimers to be retained in redistributions in *binary*
form, not only source. So =LICENSE-NCSA= and =NOTICE.md= must be packaged into
the Python wheels and the wasm bundle in Phase 7 and Phase 8. Recorded here so
that is not discovered at publish time.

=LICENSE-NCSA= is verbatim including its typographical corruption (stray =%=
characters in the disclaimer, irregular whitespace). Deliberate: a license gets
carried as distributed, not tidied.

New-code license is BSD-3-Clause, matching the upstream Python project. The
crates.io norm is dual MIT OR Apache-2.0 for the patent grant. Left open in the
backlog; cheap to change before first publish.

Protocol bug, found by executing it: the close-out asked for *one* commit per
iteration *and* for =:COMMIT:= to record that commit's hash. Impossible, since a
commit cannot contain its own hash. Amending to fix the recorded value just
changes the hash again. =CLAUDE.md= now specifies two commits per iteration, the
work commit followed by a bookkeeping commit that names it, with =pending= as the
placeholder in between, and forbids =--amend= after a hash has been recorded.

** Next
B-0002, the workspace skeleton, xtask, and the CI gate. It does not depend on
B-0001b, so the rustup question does not block it. Two things in B-0002 matter
more than the boilerplate: decide the provenance annotation mechanism (a doc
attribute like =#[doc = "Ported from teprob.f:505-522"]= parsed by xtask), since
every later iteration depends on it, and make =xtask fidelity= a stub that exits
non-zero, so the preflight can never appear to pass vacuously before B-0004
fills it in.

* 2026-08-09 Relicense new work under MIT
:PROPERTIES:

** What changed
=LICENSE= is now verbatim MIT, superseding the BSD-3-Clause choice recorded in
the B-0001a entry above. Verbatim so that GitHub, crates.io and =cargo deny=
detect it correctly; the derived-work explanation stays in =NOTICE.md=.
Cross-references updated in =NOTICE.md=, =README.md= and =PLAN.org=.

** Validation numbers
None applicable.

** Surprises and decisions
The NCSA obligation is unchanged by this. NCSA grants the right to sublicense,
so shipping the combined work under MIT is permitted, but conditions 1 and 2
require the notice and disclaimers to survive into source *and* binary
redistribution, and condition 3 forbids using the University of Illinois or the
Large Scale Systems Research Laboratory names for endorsement. So the correct
SPDX expression for the combined work is =MIT AND NCSA=, not =MIT=, and
=LICENSE-NCSA= is not removable.

Added to B-0002: every crate declares =license = "MIT AND NCSA"= and =cargo deny=
is configured to accept exactly those two, so a stray third-party license shows
up as a build failure rather than as a surprise at publish time.

** Next
Unchanged: B-0002, the workspace skeleton, xtask, and the CI gate.

* 2026-08-09 B-0002a Workspace skeleton, xtask, and the local gate
:PROPERTIES:
:ITEM:     B-0002a

:COMMIT:   db62e40
:GFORTRAN: 15.2.0

** Preflight
Skipped per protocol: still no model code. Baseline was clean and green (no Rust
code at all). gfortran 15.2.0 present, so the oracle job in =xtask ci= runs
rather than skipping, and currently passes with zero tests.

** What changed
Nine crates on edition 2024, resolver 3: =tepsim-core= (=no_std= + =alloc=,
=#![forbid(unsafe_code)]=), =-control=, =-scenario=, =tepsim= (facade,
re-exporting its three layers), =-cli= (=tep= binary), =-py=, =-wasm=,
dev-only =-oracle=, plus =xtask=. The binding crates are deliberately
dependency-free until phases 7 and 8.

=xtask= is dependency-free and has three subcommands: =ci [--fast]=,
=provenance=, and =fidelity=. =ci= runs fmt, clippy =-D warnings=, test, doc
with =RUSTDOCFLAGS=-D warnings=, =cargo deny=, and an oracle-isolation check
that greps every shipped crate's manifest for =tepsim-oracle=.

=deny.toml= allows exactly MIT and NCSA and bans =rayon= outright. That ban is
not stylistic: data parallelism inside the core would reorder floating-point
reductions and break the cross-platform determinism invariant that Tier 9 tests.

=.github/workflows/ci.yml= has four jobs (gate on three platforms, oracle on
Linux and macOS only, wasm32 build, Miri on core). Unverified until B-0002b.

** Validation numbers
=cargo xtask ci= green, exit 0. 20 tests pass workspace-wide, all in =xtask=;
the other eight crates have no code yet. =cargo deny check=: advisories ok,
bans ok, licenses ok, sources ok.

Provenance verified end-to-end against a temporary copy of =teprob.f= (not
committed; B-0003 vendors it properly):
- nothing ported: 1594 total lines, 0 claimed (0.0%), 1 unclaimed span 1-1594
- two synthetic claims: 27 claimed (1.7%), spans 1-504, 523-1551, 1561-1594

27 = (1560-1552+1) + (522-505+1) = 9 + 18, and the carved spans are exactly
right, so the range algebra is correct on real input and not just in unit tests.

Both preflight tools fail closed with exit 2 and an actionable message:
=provenance= because the reference is not vendored, =fidelity= because no golden
trace exists.

** Surprises and decisions
The provenance scanner was wrong on its first real run. Scanning for the bare
=teprob.f:= tag anywhere in the text, it reported *2.6% of the file already
claimed across 2 files* when nothing had been ported: it was counting its own
documentation examples and its own test fixtures. A coverage tool that
overstates coverage is worse than no tool, and this is exactly the class of
error it exists to catch, so it is worth being blunt that unit tests did not
find it and running it against reality did.

A claim is now one whole comment line, anchored: =@port teprob.f:START-END=.
Prose mentioning the convention does not match, because after the comment prefix
the line starts with a backtick rather than the marker; string fixtures do not
match, because the line starts with code. Four regression tests pin this, and
=xtask= itself is no longer scanned. This is the mechanism every later iteration
depends on, so it is recorded in =CLAUDE.md= and =BACKLOG.org= too.

Second, smaller: the clippy =float_cmp= lint fired on my own test asserting
=percent(0, 0) == 0.0=. Rather than =#[allow]= it, the assertion now compares
=to_bits()=, which is exact and states the intent (positive zero, not NaN)
more precisely than the original.

Third: cleanup after the end-to-end check used =git checkout --= on a file that
was untracked, so it silently failed and left a synthetic annotation in the
source. Caught by inspecting rather than trusting the command. Worth remembering
that =git checkout= is not a cleanup mechanism for work that has never been
committed.

** Next
B-0003, vendor the reference material. It is the direct unblocker for
=xtask provenance= and for B-0004, and needs no user input: copy the three
Fortran files and the =d00=-=d21= datasets into =reference/=, record provenance
and SHA-256 for each, and add a checksum test so accidental edits fail the gate.
Note the upstream repo is already cloned in this session's scratchpad, but a
fresh clone pinned to a recorded commit is the better provenance story.

* 2026-08-09 B-0003 Vendor the reference material

** Preflight
Clean and green, =cargo xtask ci= exit 0, 20 tests. Fidelity preflight still
skipped: no model code. =xtask provenance= still failing closed, which was the
correct answer and is what this item fixes.

** What changed
=reference/= now holds 48 files, 25 MB, pinned to upstream commit
=9a6c8e5fcef4a2850778704e7793c87b0a187005=: three Fortran sources and the 44
published =d00=-=d21= datasets, verbatim and uncompressed. =CHECKSUMS.sha256=
covers all of them. =reference/README.org= records provenance, the canonical
Fortran decision, the seed table, and the dataset traps.

Four tests in =tepsim-oracle= (=sha2= as the only dev-dependency): checksums
match, the manifest covers every file present, =d00.dat= has its odd shape, and
all 42 other datasets have the conventional one.

** Validation numbers
=cargo xtask ci= green, exit 0. 24 tests workspace-wide, up from 20.

=xtask provenance= now works: 1589 total lines, 0 claimed (0.0%), one unclaimed
span 1-1589. That is the correct answer with nothing ported, and it is the
baseline every later iteration is measured against.

Checksum test proven to have teeth: changing =RG=998.9= to =RG=998.90= at
=teprob.f:475= flips the digest from =409975e0...= to =3ba7acd2...= and fails
the test. Restored and re-verified green.

All 44 dataset shapes measured, not assumed: =d00.dat= is 52 x 500, every
=dNN.dat= is 480 x 52, every =dNN_te.dat= is 960 x 52.

** Surprises and decisions
*Upstream ships two different copies of the plant model.* The repository root
has =teprob.f= at 1589 lines; =fortran/teprob.f= is 1594. Vendoring the wrong
one would have silently shifted every =@port= line number by up to five, so this
had to be settled before anything was copied.

The five-line delta hoists =ISD= into =COMMON/SHUTDN/=, declares it in =TEINIT=
with =ISD=0=, adds a local =IRAND=, and exposes =COMMON/RANDSD/ G=: all to let
f2py see the state. It is behaviourally identical. In the original, =ISD= is a
local in =TEFUNC= doing two unrelated jobs, set to =-1= at line 387 purely as
the =TESUB7= sign flag and used as the shutdown flag from line 702, but line 702
assigns =ISD=0= unconditionally before the eight shutdown tests and before both
reads, so the borrow cannot leak. Class A quirk: confusing, no numerical effect.

We vendor the root copies as canonical and B-0004 writes its own instrumentation
patch, so "unmodified" stays literally true and every deviation is reviewable.

*All 54 dataset seeds are documented in the source*, at =teprob.f:1187-1246=,
covering =d00_tr=-=d26_tr= and =d00_te=-=d26_te=. The plan and B-0005 both said
four; that was an artefact of my truncating a grep in the planning session. This
materially improves Tier 7: reproducing the published datasets is not a search
problem. B-0005 updated. Also noted: the "original" seed is =1431655765=, which
is =0x55555555=, and the =d19_te= entry ends in the letter =O= rather than a
zero, harmless in a comment but a syntax error if uncommented.

*=d00.dat= is transposed and has 500 samples*, contradicting upstream's own
=data/README.md= on both counts. It is the only file like this among the 44.
Upstream's Python loader carries a silent reshape for it. Rather than inherit a
silent fix, the shape is asserted in tests and documented, and
=reference/README.org= says explicitly to trust it over the vendored upstream
README.

Datasets are stored uncompressed. Gzip would take 25 MB to about 4.5 MB, but it
would add a decompression dependency to every consumer and put a transform
between us and the published bytes. 25 MB is a few seconds of clone time and
=reference/= is excluded from the packaged crates.

** Next
B-0004, the oracle harness, and it is the highest-leverage item in Phase 0:
everything from B-0005 onward is differential testing against it. Four things to
get right. Pin the gfortran flags in =build.rs= with no reassociation permitted
and assert them. Write our own minimal instrumentation to expose the =COMMON=
blocks, keeping it as a reviewable diff against the vendored original rather
than editing =reference/= (which the checksum test now forbids anyway). Prove a
Rust test can force a 50-state into =TEFUNC= and read back all 50 derivatives.
Then make =xtask fidelity= real by emitting and committing the golden 100-step
trace, which turns the preflight on for every session after this one.

* 2026-08-09 B-0004a Oracle harness: link and drive the Fortran
:PROPERTIES:
:ITEM:     B-0004a
:PHASE:    0
:TIER:     none
:COMMIT:   386c22c
:GFORTRAN: 15.2.0
:FFLAGS:   -c -O0 -fno-fast-math -fno-unsafe-math-optimizations -fPIC -std=legacy
:STATUS:   DONE
:END:

** Preflight
Clean and green. =xtask provenance= 1589 total, 0 claimed, one span 1-1589,
identical to the B-0003 baseline. gfortran 15.2.0, unchanged. Fidelity still
skipped; B-0004c turns it on.

** What changed
The original Fortran now compiles, links and runs from a Rust test. =build.rs=
compiles =reference/fortran/teprob.f= in place, never copying or editing it, and
finds =libgfortran= by asking gfortran rather than guessing at layouts. Flags are
pinned and asserted, and the gfortran version and flag set are compiled in as env
vars for B-0004c to record.

No C shim: gfortran's =tefunc_= and =teinit_= symbols and its COMMON blocks are
named directly from Rust. Since COMMON blocks are process-wide mutable globals
and the Fortran is not reentrant, access goes through a mutex-guarded singleton;
there is no way to reach the Fortran without holding the lock. B-0004 was split
into a/b/c after a feasibility probe.

** Validation numbers
=cargo xtask ci= green. 24 tests with default features, 37 with =oracle=, up
from 24. Both build paths verified: default features need no Fortran toolchain.

Nominal operating point from =TEINIT= plus one =TEFUNC=, against the published
values in parentheses:

| XMEAS(7)  reactor pressure | 2704.999984 kPa | (2705) |
| XMEAS(8)  reactor level    |   74.999997 %   |   (75) |
| XMEAS(9)  reactor temp     |  120.400000 C   |(120.4) |
| XMEAS(12) separator level  |   50.000001 %   |   (50) |
| XMEAS(15) stripper level   |   50.000000 %   |   (50) |

Derivatives at the nominal point: 38 of 50 non-zero, max magnitude 2.544261e-3.
Not a steady state to machine precision, which is expected and is why the
closed-loop controllers exist.

Determinism confirmed: with the same state and the same RNG word, two =TEFUNC=
calls are bit-identical and consume the same number of draws. Without that,
nothing downstream could mean anything.

RNG draw counts, which Tier 3 will have to reproduce exactly:
- at t=0: *0 draws*
- at the first step past zero (t = 1/3600 h): *exactly 264 draws*

** Surprises and decisions
*The original draws no random numbers at t=0.* Three guards line up to make it
so, and assuming otherwise is easy: I wrote a test asserting the generator
advances on the first call and it failed. =TEINIT= sets =TNEXT(I)=0.1= at
=teprob.f:1362=, so both walk loops at =teprob.f:359= and =teprob.f:372= are
skipped; the noise loop at =teprob.f:711= is gated on =TIME.GT.0.0=; and the
analyser blocks do not fire until t=0.1 and t=0.25. The measurement noise
sequence therefore begins on the *second* step. A port that starts drawing at
t=0 would diverge from the very first sample and look like an arithmetic problem.

*The first step past zero draws exactly 264 uniforms*, being the 22 continuous
measurements times the 12 uniforms =TESUB6= sums per measurement
(=teprob.f:1539-1546=). Predicted from reading the source, then confirmed by
walking the LCG forward from the seed to the observed word. This is a precise,
cheap Tier 3 checkpoint available before any model code exists.

*The oracle needs =unsafe=*, which =CLAUDE.md= previously allowed only in the
PyO3 and wasm glue. The rule now names three FFI crates and requires
=deny(unsafe_op_in_unsafe_fn)=, a safe wrapper, and a real =// SAFETY:= argument
on every block.

Smaller: rustdoc with =-D warnings= fails on intra-doc links to =Oracle= from the
crate-level docs, because the workspace doc build runs with default features and
the type does not exist there. Those two references are plain code spans now,
with a note saying why.

** Next
B-0004b, full state access: the =/TEPROC/=, =/WLK/= and =/CONST/= blocks plus
=TESUB1= through =TESUB8=. Two things to be careful about. =/TEPROC/= is a large
block ending in =IVST(12)= integers after a long run of doubles, so its Rust
layout needs asserting against the Fortran declaration at =teprob.f:243-271=
rather than eyeballing. And =ISD= is still unreachable because it is a local in
the original; exposing it means instrumenting at build time into =OUT_DIR= with
an explicit string replacement that asserts its own pre-image, since
=reference/= is checksummed and must stay pristine.

* 2026-08-09 B-0004b Oracle harness: full state access
:PROPERTIES:
:ITEM:     B-0004b
:PHASE:    0
:TIER:     none
:COMMIT:   d44e181
:GFORTRAN: 15.2.0
:FFLAGS:   -c -O0 -fno-fast-math -fno-unsafe-math-optimizations -fPIC -std=legacy
:STATUS:   DONE
:END:

** Preflight
Clean and green. Provenance 1589 total, 0 claimed, one span 1-1589, matching the
B-0004a baseline exactly. gfortran 15.2.0, unchanged. Fidelity still skipped;
B-0004c turns it on.

** What changed
All seven COMMON blocks are now reachable behind the safe wrapper, plus =TESUB1=
through =TESUB4= and =TESUB6= through =TESUB8=. =ISD= is exposed by rewriting the
source into =OUT_DIR= at build time, leaving =reference/= untouched.

** Validation numbers
=cargo xtask ci= green. 25 tests with default features, *51 with =oracle==, up
from 37.

Block sizes, asserted against the Fortran declarations: =/TEPROC/= 4688 bytes
(580 doubles + 12 integers), =/WLK/= 1104 (132 doubles + 12 integers),
=/CONST/= 896 (112 doubles).

Instrumentation equivalence: *all 50 derivatives and the final RNG word are
bit-identical* between the instrumented build and the pristine original.

Teeth checks on that test, both confirmed live: perturbing the state by 1e-6
relative makes 28 of 50 derivatives disagree; changing the seed leaves the
derivatives identical but fails the RNG-word assertion. That second result is
itself informative: =YP= does not depend on the measurement noise within a call,
because noise is added to =XMEAS= after the derivative-relevant quantities are
computed and =XMEAS= does not feed back without a controller. So the derivative
comparison is insensitive to RNG differences, and the separate word comparison is
what covers them.

Shutdown flag verified both ways: clear at nominal, and tripping on reactor
overtemperature (=teprob.f:706=) with all 50 derivatives zeroed, which is the
Class C freeze-rather-than-shutdown quirk the delta register must decide about.

** Surprises and decisions
*182 of the constant assignments in =TEINIT= are single-precision literals.*
Fixed-form Fortran treats a literal with no =D= suffix as =REAL=, so assigning
it to a =DOUBLE PRECISION= variable widens a value already rounded to 24 bits.
=XMW(2)=25.4= at =teprob.f:942= stores 25.399999618530273, not the nearest
double to 25.4. =CPPRMX=1.3= stores 1.2999999523162842. 166 assignments do carry
the suffix and are full precision, so it is genuinely per-constant.

The relative error is about 1.5e-8 against a Tier 1 tolerance of 1e-13: five
orders of magnitude. A port writing =25.4_f64= would fail in a way that looks
like a deep numerical problem and is actually clerical. Found because three
layout tests failed on values I had assumed were exact, so the tests earned
their keep before any model code exists. B-0006 now requires per-literal
transcription plus a test that enumerates =D=-suffix status from the source
rather than trusting the eye.

Downstream of the same cause: the feed compositions at =teprob.f:1134-1159= are
=0.9999= and =0.0001= without suffixes, so they sum to 1 only to about 1.7e-8.
The composition test uses 1e-7, and that is not slack: a tighter bound would
assert something the original does not do.

*The uniqueness assertion in the instrumentation paid for itself immediately.*
The obvious anchor for initialising =ISD= in =TEINIT= is =TNEXT(I)=0.1D0=, which
also appears in =TEFUNC='s t=0 reset block. Matching twice would have edited both
sites. Anchoring on the =DO 550= terminator instead is unique.

*A badly chosen teeth check.* My first attempt perturbed =TIME=, and the test
still passed. Not a broken test: with no disturbances active, =TESUB8= returns
zero regardless of time and the analysers do not fire until t=0.1, so =TEFUNC=
genuinely has no time dependence at small t. Worth knowing before Tier 2 picks
sampling points, since varying time alone explores nothing.

** Next
B-0004c, the golden trace and a real fidelity preflight. Emit 100 Euler steps
from the nominal state recording all 50 states, all 50 derivatives, the RNG word
and the 41 measurements per step; stamp it with gfortran 15.2.0 and the flag set;
and make =xtask fidelity= diff against it without needing gfortran. That turns
the preflight on for every session afterwards and closes Phase 0. Store the trace
as raw bit patterns, as the pristine probe already does, so the comparison is
exact rather than decimal.

* 2026-08-09 B-0004c Golden trace and a real fidelity preflight
:PROPERTIES:
:ITEM:     B-0004c
:PHASE:    0
:TIER:     none
:COMMIT:   5426691
:GFORTRAN: 15.2.0
:FFLAGS:   -c -O0 -fno-fast-math -fno-unsafe-math-optimizations -fPIC -std=legacy

** Preflight
Clean and green. Provenance 1589 total, 0 claimed, unchanged from B-0004b.
gfortran 15.2.0, unchanged. This is the last iteration where the fidelity step
is skipped: it works from here on.

** What changed
=golden/nominal-100-steps.trace= holds 100 Euler steps of the original from the
nominal state, as hexadecimal IEEE-754 bit patterns, with the gfortran version,
flag set, seed and step size that produced it. A =gen-golden-trace= binary
behind the =oracle= feature regenerates it in one command, and =CLAUDE.md= now
describes what each half of the preflight actually covers.

** Validation numbers
=cargo xtask ci= green. *34 tests default, 63 with =oracle==, up from 25 and 51.

Trace: 100 steps, 142 values per step, 14,200 values, 240 KB, 118 lines.
Reactor temperature 120.400000 C at step 0 and 120.384504 C at step 99, drifting
gently as expected for 100 seconds open loop with no control and no disturbances.

Teeth checks on both halves, each confirmed:
- corrupting one value fails the oracle test, naming the field:
  "step 1: state[3] 0.42300437096630084 vs 7.604320537028714e307"
- corrupting one to NaN fails =xtask fidelity= with exit 2 and
  "step 0, value 4 is not finite"
- restored, both pass

Nine parser unit tests cover missing headers, truncated lines, corrupted hex,
out-of-order steps, non-finite values, short traces, and exact round-tripping of
awkward values including 1/3, =f64::MIN_POSITIVE= and negative zero.

** Surprises and decisions
*The item as written could not be done honestly.* It asked for =xtask fidelity=
to diff the trace against the Rust port, but there is no port yet, so the
comparison would have been vacuous while looking like a passing gate. The
responsibility splits in two, and both halves are real now: the oracle test
catches toolchain drift by rerunning the Fortran, and =xtask fidelity= validates
the anchor itself without needing gfortran. The command prints "0 of 100
recorded steps diffed" so the gap is visible rather than implied.

*My first teeth check tested the wrong half.* I corrupted a value in the trace
and expected =xtask fidelity= to fail; it passed, correctly, because the
corruption produced another valid finite number and that command checks
structure, not values. Re-tested against the half that owns values. Twice now a
teeth check has probed something insensitive, which is a good argument for
always running them rather than assuming a test would have caught the thing.

*Also: my exit-code check was measuring the wrong process.* =cmd | tail; echo 0=
reports =tail='s status. The real check is running without a pipe. Worth
remembering, since a gate that silently exits 0 is exactly the failure this
protocol exists to prevent.

=cargo deny= caught a wildcard path dependency the moment xtask picked up the
trace format, which is the ban config earning its keep on the first real change.

*Phase 0 is complete.* The workspace, the gate, the vendored reference, the
oracle harness and the fidelity anchor all exist before a single line of the
model has been ported. Nothing downstream has to be taken on trust.

** Next
B-0005, port =TESUB7= and prove bit-exactness: the first actual model code, and
the first =@port= claim, so provenance moves off 0%. The arithmetic is one line,
=G = DMOD(G*9228907, 4294967296)=, and the whole difficulty is resisting the
urge to tidy it: the product exceeds 2^53 so the rounding is part of the
algorithm, and integer arithmetic would give different numbers. Prove bit
equality over 1e7 draws against the oracle, from the compiled-in seed and a
sample of the 54 documented dataset seeds, and commit a test-vector file so the
check survives on machines without gfortran.

* 2026-08-10 B-0005 Port TESUB7 and prove bit-exactness

:ITEM:     B-0005
:PHASE:    1
:TIER:     1
:COMMIT:   9b4960b
:GFORTRAN: 15.2.0
:FFLAGS:   -c -O0 -fno-fast-math -fno-unsafe-math-optimizations -fPIC -std=legacy
:STATUS:   DONE
:END:

** Preflight
First session with the preflight actually running. =xtask fidelity= green: 100
steps, 142 values each, recorded with gfortran 15.2.0, local gfortran matches,
"0 of 100 steps diffed" as expected with no model code. Provenance 1589 total,
0 claimed. =xtask ci --fast= green. Baseline identical to the B-0004c close.

** What changed
=TepRng= in =tepsim-core=, the first ported model code. Two committed check
layers, =golden/tesub7-vectors.txt= for machines without gfortran and a
draw-by-draw differential test for machines with it. Provenance claims now cover
whole subroutines; =CLAUDE.md= records the convention.

** Validation numbers
=cargo xtask ci= green. *44 tests default, 76 with =oracle==, up from 34 and 63.

*Provenance: 9 of 1589 lines claimed, 0.6%.* Two unclaimed spans, 1-1546 and
1556-1589. First non-zero reading.

Bit-exactness against the Fortran:
- compiled-in seed 4651207995: 10^7 draws, XOR fold and final state both match
- five dataset seeds (original, d00_tr, d00_tr_new, d01_tr, d00_te): 10^6 draws
  each, folds and final states all match
- draw-by-draw over 200,000 draws for four seeds: every draw and every
  intermediate state bit-identical
- 16 raw outputs of each output mode, per seed, all bit-identical
- interleaved modes over 50,000 draws on an irregular pattern: all match

Supporting measurements:
- exact integer arithmetic diverges at *draw 0*: 714943912.0 against 714943913
- products exceed 2^53 on 0.7716 of draws, against 0.7728 predicted from
  2^53/9228907 over 2^32
- state never reaches zero in 10^6 draws from any of four seeds

Teeth check: substituting the u128 integer recurrence fails both layers, the
differential test on draw 0 with "Rust gives 0.16646085144020617, Fortran gives
0.16646085120737553".

Vector generation cost 5.8 s wall for 1.5 x 10^7 FFI calls.

** Surprises and decisions
*Nothing surprising about the port itself*, which is the point: the analysis in
the planning session held up exactly. The generator is bit-reproducible, and
writing it the way the Fortran writes it was sufficient.

*My guess about the overflow rate was wrong and the test caught it.* I asserted
that over 90% of products exceed 2^53; the real figure is 77%, because the
threshold is 2^53/9228907 which is about 22.7% of the way through the state
range. Rather than loosen the bound I derived it: the test now computes the
predicted fraction from the arithmetic and checks the observed one against it,
so it explains itself instead of encoding a number I once measured.

*Provenance granularity needed deciding.* Claiming only the three arithmetic
lines gave 3/1589 and left =RETURN=, =END= and the declarations permanently
unclaimed, which would make the coverage number unreadable by the time
=TEFUNC='s 600 lines are ported. Claims now cover the whole subroutine on the
type that ports it, with finer claims inside pinning individual lines.

Two small lint interactions worth noting for later sessions: clippy's
=float_cmp= fires on =assert_ne!= over floats as well as =assert_eq!=, and
rustdoc with =-D warnings= rejects a public doc linking to a private constant.
Both resolved by comparing =to_bits()= and by naming the value in prose.

** Next
B-0006, the constants table. This is the item B-0004b's finding was aimed at:
182 of the assignments in =TEINIT= are single-precision literals, so each
constant must be transcribed according to whether its Fortran literal carries a
=D= suffix. Write the test that enumerates suffix status from
=reference/fortran/teprob.f= *first*, then the table, so the transcription is
checked by machine rather than by eye. =XMW(2)= is the canary: it must come out
25.399999618530273 and not 25.4.

*rustc 1.89.0 to 1.97.1, an eight-version jump, and the gate is green with no
code changes.* 50 tests default, 82 with =oracle=, up from 44 and 76; the six new
ones are the toolchain parsers.

Crucially, the RNG vectors still pass bit-exact under the new compiler: 10^7
draws on the compiled-in seed and 10^6 on five others, all folds and final states
matching the Fortran. IEEE f64 arithmetic is stable across the jump, which is the
first real evidence for the Tier 9 determinism claim.

wasm32 builds =tepsim-core= and =tepsim-wasm= cleanly: 17,082 and 3,228 byte
rlibs.

Disk: two toolchains now, =stable= at 1.4 GB and the pinned =1.97.1= at 669 MB.
Both are 1.97.1 today and will diverge when stable moves. Left alone; removing
=stable= is the user's call since other projects may want it.

** Surprises and decisions
*The eight-version jump was a non-event*, which is worth recording precisely
because it could have eaten a session. No new clippy lints fired, no rustdoc
complaints, no behaviour change. The codebase being lint-clean at =-D warnings=
from the start is what made that cheap.

*The toolchain guard matters more than it first appears.* =rust-toolchain.toml=
only takes effect when =cargo= is rustup's; with any other cargo first on PATH it
is ignored *silently*. Homebrew's Rust 1.89 is still installed here and loses
only on PATH ordering, so a single PATH edit would switch compilers with no
signal at all, and every validation number after that would come from an
unrecorded compiler. The check turns that into an immediate, explicit failure.

*My first teeth check on that guard was wrong again.* Pinning a bogus 1.42.0
made rustup try to download it and fail on target availability, before my check
ever ran. That is a third instance of a teeth check probing the wrong layer. The
comparison logic is now two pure functions with six unit tests, which is both
faster and actually exercises the code in question, and no toolchain download is
involved.

*=cargo +nightly= is required in the miri job.* The pin would otherwise override
the nightly that job installs, and miri is not in the pinned stable. Easy to miss
and would have shown up as a confusing CI failure at B-0002b.

Installed with =--no-modify-path=, since =~/.cargo/bin= was already ahead of
=/opt/homebrew/bin=. No shell profile was modified.

** Next
Back to B-0006, the constants table, unchanged by this detour. Write the test
that enumerates =D=-suffix status straight from
=reference/fortran/teprob.f= first, then the table, so transcription is checked
by machine. =XMW(2)= must come out 25.399999618530273 rather than 25.4.

The only remaining blocked item is B-0002b, which needs a GitHub remote. Worth
noting that the CI workflow has now been edited twice without ever running, so
its first execution will likely need a fixup pass.

*Provenance 83/1589 lines claimed, 5.2%*, up from 9 (0.6%). Four unclaimed

** Surprises and decisions
*The backlog had a dependency inversion.* B-0006 required constants "indexed by
=Component=" while =Component= was defined in B-0007, which depended on B-0006.
Caught at the pick step, which is what that step is for. Swapped, with the
reason recorded in the item.

*The stream mapping was re-derived rather than trusted.* =PLAN.org= already had
the table from the planning session, but CLAUDE.md says to read the Fortran
rather than the port, so I matched each =FTM= index against the manipulated
variable that drives it and the measurement that reports it. It agreed, and the
specific lines are now cited in the module docs so nobody has to redo it. Worth
stating plainly what the trap is: only the A and C feed has an internal index
equal to its paper number, and the A and D feeds are swapped relative to naive
expectation.

*The =Composition= tolerance is set by the original, not by taste.* 1e-6 rather
than something tighter, because the feed compositions are single-precision
literals that sum to 1 only to 1.7e-8. A test asserts the tolerance admits the
original's data *and* that the data is genuinely off by more than a rounding
error, so the tolerance cannot be quietly tightened later without failing.

*=clippy::float_cmp= has now cost four separate detours.* Fixed systemically
with a shared =assert_exact= helper rather than a fifth case-by-case patch.
Suppressing the lint would be wrong, since it catches real mistakes, and an
approximate comparison would be wrong too, since these values are meant to be
exact.

* 2026-08-10 B-0002b CI matrix verified green
:PROPERTIES:
:ITEM:     B-0002b
:PHASE:    0
:TIER:     none

:GFORTRAN: 15.2.0 local; 13.3.0 Ubuntu runner; 16.1.0 macOS runner

** Preflight
Clean and green locally. Unblocked by the user asking for the repository.

** What changed
Private =jkitchin/tep-rust= created and pushed. CI green on all seven jobs at
run 31388180377: gate on Linux, macOS and Windows; oracle on Linux and macOS;
wasm32 build; miri.

** Validation numbers
Four CI runs. The first three failed, each on a different real defect.

*Cross-compiler agreement, the useful result.* The golden trace was recorded
with gfortran 15.2.0 on macOS. Measured against it over 100 Euler steps, 14,200
values:

| Running                | Platform libm | Values differing | Worst deviation     |
|------------------------+---------------+------------------+---------------------|
| 15.2.0 (local)         | Apple         | 0                | 0, bit exact        |
| 16.1.0 (macOS runner)  | Apple         | under bound      | below 1e-9          |
| 13.3.0 (Ubuntu runner) | glibc         | 16               | 3.307e-8 at step 91 |

A different compiler on the same platform barely moves anything; a different
platform's =libm= moves the last bits of =exp= and =pow=, and 91 Euler steps
amplify that to 3.3e-8. This is the first direct measurement of the mechanism
=PLAN.org= predicts will limit cross-platform agreement, and it arrived for free
from runner variation. Tolerance set to 1e-7, about three times the largest
observed figure.

*The generator matched bit-exactly on all three compilers.* Asserted separately
and unconditionally, because it is pure f64 arithmetic with no library calls and
has no excuse to differ. It did not. That cleanly separates "arithmetic we
control" from "arithmetic the platform controls".

** Surprises and decisions
*=cargo xtask= had never worked.* There was no =.cargo/config.toml= alias, so the
command =CLAUDE.md= documents as *the* gate did not exist. Every session,
including all of mine, had been running =cargo run -p xtask --= instead. Nobody
noticed until a machine executed the documented command for the first time.
Worth generalising: a command that only ever appears in documentation is
untested documentation.

*Demanding bit equality across compilers was a design error, not a CI problem.*
The trace is a record of one (compiler, flags) pair, and CI legitimately has
three. The fix splits the check: bit equality against the recording compiler,
bounded agreement otherwise, with the observed deviation always printed. That
turned an obstacle into the measurement above.

*Windows failed the vendored checksums* because Git translates LF to CRLF on
checkout, changing the bytes and so the SHA-256 of every reference file. The
checksum test was right and Git was wrong to be helpful.

*Two of my own fixes silently did nothing, and I claimed otherwise in a commit
message.* A scripted string replacement for the tolerance constant failed to
match, and I did not check, so 1e-9 shipped while the commit said 1e-7; Ubuntu
failed again quoting the old bound. Separately, =.gitattributes= had correct
content and no effect, because in gitattributes the *last* matching pattern
wins and I had put the catch-all after the specific override. Both are the same
error: asserting a change happened without verifying it. Replacements now assert
they matched, and the following commit corrected the record.

*The "gate has teeth" criterion was satisfied by real failures*, runs
31385781606, 31386640152 and 31387437653, rather than by a contrived breaking
commit. Three genuine defects caught with actionable messages is better evidence
than a deliberate break, and it keeps main's history clean.

** Next
B-0006, the constants table, now the only item in the queue and unblocked.
=Component= exists to index it, and the discipline is established twice over by
the header-table test: write the machine check against
=reference/fortran/teprob.f= *before* the table. 182 assignments in =TEINIT= are
single-precision literals; =XMW(2)= must come out 25.399999618530273.

Also worth doing soon, though not yet an item: the workspace declares
=license = "MIT AND NCSA"= for every crate, which is broader than the facts.
Only crates containing ported Fortran are derivative. =tepsim-scenario=,
=xtask=, =tepsim-py=, =tepsim-wasm= and =tepsim-cli= are original work and could
declare plain MIT.

* 2026-08-10 B-0006 Thermodynamic constants table
:PROPERTIES:
:ITEM:     B-0006
:PHASE:    1
:TIER:     1
:COMMIT:   8065b4d
:GFORTRAN: 15.2.0
:RUSTC:    1.97.1
:STATUS:   DONE
:END:

** Preflight
Clean and green. Fidelity green, local gfortran 15.2.0 matches the trace.
Provenance 83/1589 (5.2%), matching the B-0007 baseline.

** What changed
=crates/tepsim-core/src/constants.rs=: all 112 entries of =COMMON/CONST/=, with
per-literal precision. Two independent verification tests.

** Validation numbers
=cargo xtask ci= green. *78 tests default, 112 with =oracle==, up from 74 and 106.

*All 112 constants bit-exact against gfortran's stored =COMMON/CONST/=.*

Precision split, parsed from the source and asserted: *62 single precision, 50
double*. Per block, all 8 single: =AVP=, =BVP=, =CVP=, =AV=, =XMW=. All 8
double: =AH=, =BH=, =CH=, =AG=, =BG=, =CG=. Mixed: =AD=, =BD=, =CD=.

*Provenance 195/1589 lines, 12.3%*, up from 83 (5.2%). Five unclaimed spans, the
largest being 169-940 and 1053-1546.

Teeth checks, both caught:
- dropping =single()= from =XMW(2)= fails both tests, the oracle one reporting
  "Rust 25.4 vs Fortran 25.399999618530273 (differ by 1.502e-8 relative)",
  matching the B-0004b prediction to three digits
- substituting a wrong value into =AH(4)= fails at 3.133e1 relative. Note this
  second probe changed the value as well as the precision, so it demonstrates
  gross-error detection rather than the exact inverse of the first.

** Surprises and decisions
*All 24 Antoine coefficients are single precision.* Not just the odd one:
=AVP=, =BVP= and =CVP= carry no =D= suffix anywhere. They are the worst place
for it, because =teprob.f:484= and =487= evaluate
=DEXP(AVP(I)+BVP(I)/(TCR+CVP(I)))=, so a 1e-8 error in an exponent argument is
amplified rather than merely carried. Anyone porting TEP who assumes "the
constants are just numbers" gets vapour pressures wrong in the eighth digit
everywhere, which then feeds every flash calculation.

*The table was generated, not typed.* 112 hand-transcribed values is past what
anyone checks reliably, so a script converted them and two tests verify the
result. The generation and the re-derivation share an assumption about Fortran
literal semantics, which is exactly why the oracle comparison exists: it asks
the compiler instead, so it would catch a misunderstanding common to both.

*Every no-suffix literal is wrapped, even the exact ones.* =single(2.0)= is
identical to =2.0=, but writing it uniformly means the wrapper records what the
*source* says rather than what rounding happens to occur. A reader checking
=XMW(1)= against =teprob.f:941= sees the correspondence without doing any
arithmetic, and nobody has to decide case by case whether a wrapper is needed.

*The assert-your-replacements discipline paid off immediately.* A scripted edit
failed to match because =cargo fmt= had rewrapped the target, and the assertion
stopped it rather than silently doing nothing, which is precisely the failure
that produced a false commit message in B-0002b.

** Next
B-0008, the Tier 1 test harness: sweep generators and ULP reporting, built
before the routines that need it. Composition sweeps over a simplex grid plus
random Dirichlet samples, temperature sweeps per =ITY= mode, and a comparison
helper that reports a ULP histogram rather than a bare pass or fail. The
constants are now in place to feed it, and B-0009 through B-0011 all depend on
it existing first.

* 2026-08-11 B-0008 Tier 1 harness: sweep pools and ULP reporting
:PROPERTIES:
:ITEM:     B-0008
:PHASE:    1
:TIER:     1
:COMMIT:   12d37c3
:GFORTRAN: 15.2.0
:STATUS:   DONE
:END:
** Preflight
Clean and green. =cargo xtask ci= green at 78 tests default, 112 with
=oracle=. Fidelity green: golden trace intact, 100 steps, 142 values/step,
recorded with gfortran 15.2.0, local gfortran 15.2.0 matches, 0 of 100 steps
diffed as expected in phase 1. Provenance 195/1589 (12.3%), five unclaimed
spans, *identical to the B-0006 closing number*: no drift. No Tier 1/2/3
numbers exist yet to compare against. =book/src/deltas.md= does not exist yet,
so there were no open quirk decisions to re-read.

** What changed
=crates/tepsim-oracle/src/tier1/= now holds the Tier 1 measuring apparatus:
three composition pools, a temperature ladder, and a =Comparison= accumulator
whose =Display= is the report format for this log. It is *not* gated on the
=oracle= feature, because it generates inputs and compares numbers and never
touches the Fortran, so it can be proved correct on a machine with no
gfortran. =tepsim-core= became a real dependency of =tepsim-oracle= rather
than a dev-dependency; the isolation check still passes, the arrow only ever
points this way.

** Validation numbers
=cargo xtask ci= green. *106 tests default, 145 with =oracle==, up from 78 and
112. Provenance *unchanged at 195/1589 (12.3%)*, correct: this iteration
ported nothing.

*Pool sizes.* =Sweep::SMOKE= is 7,950 cases (grid 4 divisions x 15
temperatures = 4,950, plus 2,000 Dirichlet and 1,000 face). =Sweep::FULL= is
*9,987,490 cases*, the ten million =PLAN.org= asks for.

*Throughput, the number B-0009 needs*: 111,300 evaluations in 0.07 s, about
*1.58e6 evaluations/s in a debug build*, case generation included. =FULL= is
therefore roughly 6.3 s per routine per pass, about 45 s for all seven
(routine, ITY) pairs. It fits in a test.

*The Fortran agrees with itself* over the whole SMOKE sweep for all seven
pairs: 7,950 cases each, max ulp 0, max rel err 0.000e0, histogram 0:7950, no
non-finite values anywhere. That is the plumbing check, not a validation.

*Teeth, one ULP injected into the temperature.* Every routine saw it:

| routine     | max ulp | max rel err | percentiles                |
|-------------+---------+-------------+----------------------------|
| TESUB1 ity0 |       5 |    6.535e-16 | p50=1 p90=2 p99=4 p100=5   |
| TESUB1 ity1 |       4 |    5.643e-16 | p50=0 p90=1 p99=2 p100=4   |
| TESUB1 ity2 |    1024 |    1.773e-13 | p50=0 p90=2 p99=8 p100>=16 |
| TESUB3 ity0 |       4 |    5.043e-16 | p50=0 p90=1 p99=2 p100=4   |
| TESUB3 ity1 |       3 |    4.245e-16 | p50=0 p90=1 p99=2 p100=3   |
| TESUB3 ity2 |       4 |    5.471e-16 | p50=0 p90=1 p99=2 p100=4   |
| TESUB4      |       7 |    9.350e-16 | p50=0 p90=1 p99=3 p100=7   |

Worst cases: TESUB1 ity2 at =grid#704 T=21.875=, TESUB4 at =face#815
T=159.27517615845147=. The =face= pool produced two of the seven worst cases
despite being an eighth of the sweep, which is the argument for keeping it.

*Metric self-checks.* An injected relative offset of 1e-9 is reported as 1e-9
to within 1e-15. A composition round-trips through the FFI in Fortran order,
checked by exact linearity of =TESUB1 ity=0= in z: 2,000 cases, max rel err
3.383e-16, max ulp 2, at =grid#644 T=5.292=.

** Surprises and decisions
*TESUB1 with ITY=2 is ill-conditioned, and this is the headline for B-0009.*
=teprob.f:1410-1411= subtracts =R*(T+273.15)= with =R=3.57696e-6=. At an
equimolar A/B/C/D mixture at 21.875 C, H is 1.0566592904652041e-3 and the
correction is 1.0552926021679687e-3: the subtraction discards about ten bits.
One ULP in gives 1024 ULP out, and a relative error of 1.773e-13, *already
past the Tier 1 gate of 1e-13*. This does not doom B-0009. Identical
arithmetic in an identical order gives identical bits however bad the
conditioning, so a correct port still reports zero. It does mean ITY=2 has no
margin: any difference at all, from any cause, arrives multiplied by a
thousand. Suspect it first when Tier 1 moves.

*The =273.15= at =teprob.f:1411= is single precision.* No =D= suffix, so
gfortran stores 273.14999389648438. Writing =273.15_f64= makes the correction
wrong by 2.1e-8 relative, which the cancellation above turns into about 1.6e-5
in the returned enthalpy: eight orders past the gate. This is the B-0006
hazard in the one place a constants test cannot see it, because the literal is
not in =COMMON/CONST/= at all. *There may be more of these outside the COMMON
blocks; B-0009 should grep its line range for suffix-free literals before
writing any Rust.* Asserted bit-exactly rather than approximately: at a
cancelling case the subtraction is exact by Sterbenz's lemma, so
=H(ity=1) - H(ity=2)= recovers the correction term to the last bit.

*Buckets 0-15 are counted individually and the rest lumped.* The ITY=2 result
is what justifies the design rather than refuting it: the overflow bucket
reported =>=16:39= while the exact worst, 1024, was still reported in full.
A histogram that stopped at its last bucket would have hidden the finding.

*The sampler is SplitMix64, not =TepRng=.* The plant's generator is itself
under test, so drawing Tier 1 inputs from it would let a bug in the =TESUB7=
port silently change which cases the sweep visits. Integer arithmetic only, so
the stream is identical on x86-64, aarch64 and wasm32.

*Dirichlet is sampled by spacings, with concentration fixed at 1.* Sorted
uniforms, gaps taken. No rejection, so the words consumed per sample are
fixed; no transcendental function, so no cross-platform ULP risk; fractions
non-negative by construction. A general Dirichlet(alpha) would need a gamma
sampler and give up all three. The near-degenerate compositions a small alpha
would reach are covered instead by the third pool, which draws a random
support of 1 to 8 species and is uniform on that face, so vertices and edges
are hit exactly rather than approached.

*The temperature domain is one band for all three ITY modes, and that is not
an approximation.* Every =TESUB1= call site passes some =TST(i)=
(=teprob.f:555-564=, =654-655=), and every =TST(i)= is either the 45 C feed
temperature or one of TCV, TCR, TCS, TCC (=teprob.f:411-412=, =549-554=,
=652-653=). The vapour correlations are evaluated at exactly the temperatures
the liquid ones are. Splitting the domain by mode would describe a distinction
the source does not make. Bounds are 0 to 175 C: the ceiling is the reactor
shutdown limit at =teprob.f:706=, the floor is below the =TCC < 5.292= branch
at =teprob.f:617= so that branch is actually exercised. A test computes the
=TESUB4= density poles from the constants and asserts the ceiling clears the
nearest one; it is *208.57 C, for component D*, so the sweep stays 33 degrees
clear of the singularity.

*Eight breakpoints go in the ladder by hand*, each carrying the =teprob.f=
line that makes it special, because a uniform grid steps over a branch
boundary with probability one.

*No =xtask validate= yet.* =CLAUDE.md= says it arrives "incrementally from
B-0008", but with no routine ported there is nothing for it to report on, and
scaffolding an empty report now would fix its shape before the first real
measurement exists. It belongs in B-0009, where the first Tier 1 number is
produced.

** Next
B-0009: port =TESUB1= and =TESUB3=. The harness is ready, so the iteration is
read =teprob.f:1375-1414= and =1443-1480=, write the two functions, and run
=Sweep::FULL= through all three ITY modes.

Before writing any Rust, *grep that line range for literals without a =D=
suffix*. This iteration found one, the =273.15= at =teprob.f:1411=, and it is
worth 1.6e-5 relative on its own. =3.57696D0= and =1.D6= on the line above are
double, and the =2.D0= and =3.D0= divisors in the polynomials are too, but
=1.8D0= should be confirmed rather than assumed.

Expect ITY=0 and ITY=1 to come out at 0 ULP and ITY=2 to be the one that
argues. If ITY=2 does not reach zero, the cause is upstream of the
cancellation, not in it: compare =TESUB1(z,T,1)= first, since ITY=2 differs
from it by exactly two lines.

=xtask validate --tiers 1= should land with it, since B-0009 is the first
iteration with a Tier 1 number to report.

* 2026-08-11 B-0009 TESUB1 and TESUB3: enthalpy and heat capacity, bit-exact

:ITEM:     B-0009

** Preflight
Clean and green. Fidelity green, local gfortran 15.2.0 matches the trace.
Provenance 195/1589 (12.3%), *identical to the B-0008 closing number*: no
drift. No Tier 1 numbers existed yet to compare against, since B-0008 built
the instrument and this is the first iteration to use it.

Did what the B-0008 entry said to do first: extracted every numeric literal in
=teprob.f:1375-1414= and =1443-1480=. Seven distinct literals, six carrying a
=D= suffix (=0.0D0=, =1.8D0=, =2.D0=, =3.D0=, =3.57696D0=, =1.D6=) and exactly
one without: the =273.15= at =teprob.f:1411=. That confirmed the B-0008
prediction and was the only precision hazard in either routine.

** What changed
=crates/tepsim-core/src/thermo.rs=: =enthalpy= and =heat_capacity=, with
=EnergyBasis= replacing the bare =ITY= integer. =cargo xtask validate --tiers
1= now exists and runs the full-volume gate in release.

** Validation numbers
=cargo xtask ci= green in *29 s*. *116 tests default, 159 with =oracle==, up
from 106 and 145. Provenance *273/1589 (17.2%)*, up from 195 (12.3%): the two
subroutines are 78 lines and all 78 are claimed.

*Tier 1, the gate. Bit-exact, everywhere, with no exceptions:*

| routine | ITY | cases | max rel err | max ulp | histogram |
|---------+-----+-----------+-------------+---------+---------------|
| TESUB1  |   0 | 9,987,490 | 0.000e0     |       0 | 0:9987490     |
| TESUB1  |   1 | 9,987,490 | 0.000e0     |       0 | 0:9987490     |
| TESUB1  |   2 | 9,987,490 | 0.000e0     |       0 | 0:9987490     |
| TESUB3  |   0 | 9,987,490 | 0.000e0     |       0 | 0:9987490     |
| TESUB3  |   1 | 9,987,490 | 0.000e0     |       0 | 0:9987490     |
| TESUB3  |   2 | 9,987,490 | 0.000e0     |       0 | 0:9987490     |

*59,924,940 evaluations, zero differing bits.* p50 = p90 = p99 = p100 = 0 in
all six. The 1e-13 gate was never approached; a separate test asserts bit
equality directly, because a 1e-13 threshold would let a drift to 1e-15 pass
unnoticed forever.

*Timing*: 1.5 to 1.9 s per mode in release, about 190 s per routine in debug.

*The sabotage check.* Substituting =273.15_f64= for the widened =f32=, and
changing nothing else: *max relative error 1.597e-5 at =grid#704 T=21.875=,
103,098,852,352 ULP*, every one of the 7,950 smoke cases in the overflow
bucket. That matches the 1.6e-5 B-0008 predicted from the cancellation ratio,
to three digits.

** Surprises and decisions
*The result is bit equality, not "within tolerance", and that should now be
the standard.* Both routines are straight-line arithmetic over constants
B-0006 already proved bit-identical, so once the association and the literal
precisions are right there is nothing left to differ by. Any future Tier 1
routine that lands at 1e-15 instead of zero has something wrong with it that a
tolerance would hide. B-0010 should expect zero from =TESUB4= too.

*Three transcription traps, all of which would have passed a casual reading:*
=CH(I)*T**2/3.D0= associates as =(CH * (T*T)) / 3=, not =((CH*T)*T)/3=;
=BH(I)*T/2.D0= as =(BH*T)/2=; and the three-term sum left to right. Writing
=T*T*CH/3.0= is the natural Rust and is wrong in the last bits.

*Clippy actively pushes toward breaking this port.* =suboptimal_flops= wants
=mul_add= for every polynomial term, which would fuse two roundings into one
and produce a *more accurate* answer that no longer matches the oracle.
=assign_op_pattern= wants =hi *= 1.8= where the Fortran writes =HI=1.8D0*HI=;
that one is genuinely bit-identical since IEEE multiplication commutes, but it
hides the line-for-line correspondence. Both are allowed at module scope with
the reason recorded. *Expect this in every ported module and do not take the
suggestion without checking Tier 1 afterwards.*

*ITY=2 is internal energy, and the arithmetic proves it rather than the
comments.* =3.57696 = 1.8 x 1.9872= exactly in decimal: the Rankine-per-Celsius
ratio times the gas constant in Btu/(lbmol R), the same two numbers
=teprob.f:594= uses separately. So =H - R(T+273.15)= is =U = H - RT= with the
temperature made absolute, and the one call site (=teprob.f:465=) solves for
=TCV= from =ESV=, an energy state. A test asserts the factorisation so the
claim is checkable.

*The original is inconsistent about the offset.* =teprob.f:1411= writes
=273.15=, single. =teprob.f:594= writes =273.15D0=, double. The precision
cannot be inferred from elsewhere in the file; each occurrence has to be read
off its own line. Assume nothing about a literal seen in one place from having
seen it in another.

*=TESUB3= is exactly =d(TESUB1)/dT=*, which is what makes =TESUB2='s Newton
iteration converge. Asserted by finite difference across all three modes
rather than left as an observation, since B-0011 depends on it.

*Split the gate from the commit path, deliberately.* The full sweep is 190 s
per routine in debug, a 38x increase on the oracle job, and =TESUB2= in B-0011
runs up to 100 Newton iterations per call. Leaving it in =cargo test= would
have made the gate something a future session quietly deletes. So =cargo test=
runs =Sweep::SMOKE= and =cargo xtask validate --tiers 1= runs =Sweep::FULL= in
release, selected by =TEP_TIER1_SWEEP=. *Both run the same assertions at the
same tolerance*, and the session preflight already invokes =validate=, so the
gate runs every session. The smoke sweep still contains =grid#704=, the
worst-case cell for every failure mode found so far. Tiers 2-10 print that they
are unimplemented rather than passing silently.

*No =Celsius= newtype yet.* =PLAN.org= calls for one at public API boundaries.
These two functions are called from the innermost part of the derivative
evaluation, which the plan exempts, and introducing the newtype now would
churn the Tier 1 harness built one iteration ago. Deferred to the iteration
that builds the public =Simulation= surface. Recorded here so it is a decision
rather than an omission.

** Next
B-0010, =TESUB4=, liquid density, =teprob.f:1481-1505=. It is the shortest of
the four and the harness already drives it, so the iteration is mostly reading
the line carefully.

Two things to check before writing it. First, =V=0.0= at =teprob.f:1498= is a
*single-precision zero assigned to a double*, which is harmless for zero but
shows the file's habit; grep the range for suffix-free literals as usual, and
note =AD=, =BD=, =CD= are already in the constants table. Second, the
denominator =AD(I)+(BD(I)+CD(I)*T)*T= is Horner form, not the expanded
polynomial, so it must be transcribed as written: expanding it changes the
rounding and =TESUB4= is the one routine with a genuine singularity nearby,
at 208.57 C for component D, which B-0008 measured.

Expect bit equality. If it comes out at 1e-16 instead of 0, the cause is
almost certainly the Horner association.

* 2026-08-11 B-0010 TESUB4: liquid density, bit-exact
:PROPERTIES:
:ITEM:     B-0010
:PHASE:    1
:TIER:     1
:COMMIT:   dafa54a
:GFORTRAN: 15.2.0
:STATUS:   DONE
:END:
** Preflight
Clean and green. Fidelity green, local gfortran 15.2.0 matches. Provenance
273/1589 (17.2%), identical to the B-0009 close: no drift.

*Tier 1 re-run at full volume and unchanged*: all six reports from B-0009 still
read max rel err 0.000e0, max ulp 0, histogram 0:9987490. The sabotage probe
still reads 1.597e-5 at =grid#704=. Nothing moved.

Literal extraction over =teprob.f:1481-1505=, as usual: two literals, =0.0= and
=1.0=, both without a =D= suffix and both *exactly representable in f32*, so
for once the missing suffix costs nothing. Worth recording that the check was
run and came back clean rather than leaving it silent.

** What changed
=thermo::liquid_density=, and the Tier 1 differential extended to cover it.
The module now covers three of the four Phase 1 utility routines.

** Validation numbers
=cargo xtask ci= green. *120 tests default, 164 with =oracle==, up from 116 and
159. Provenance *298/1589 (18.8%)*, up from 273: =TESUB4= is 25 lines, all
claimed.

*Tier 1, TESUB4*: 9,987,490 cases, *max relative error 0.000e0, max ulp 0*,
histogram 0:9987490, p50 = p90 = p99 = p100 = 0, 0 non-finite. 1.4 s in
release.

Seven Tier 1 reports now exist and every one of them reads zero. The bit
equality test covers all three routines across all three modes, 89.9 million
evaluations per full run, with no differing bits.

** Surprises and decisions
*No surprises, which is itself the result worth recording.* The B-0009 entry
predicted the single hazard, the Horner denominator =AD(I)+(BD(I)+CD(I)*T)*T=,
and predicted that getting it wrong would show up as 1e-16 rather than zero.
Transcribed as written, it came out at zero on the first run. The habit of
naming the specific expected failure in the previous entry is doing real work;
keep doing it.

*The units are recoverable from the call sites, not guessable.*
=teprob.f:467-469= divides a molar holdup by this to get a volume and
=teprob.f:704= divides that volume by 35.3145, the cubic feet in a cubic
metre, before a level comparison. So the denominator is cubic feet, the
=AD/BD/CD= coefficients are mass densities in lb/ft^3, and the return is
*lbmol/ft^3*. Recorded because Phase 2 will need it for the level equations
and re-deriving it is ten minutes each time.

*A, B and C have no real density.* =AD= is 1.0 with =BD=CD=0= for all three
(=teprob.f:973=, =983=, =993=), so they contribute =x*M= flat and
temperature-independent. They are the non-condensibles and the model never puts
them in a liquid phase in quantity. It is a placeholder that keeps the sum
finite, not a fitted number, and a test pins it so nobody later "fixes" it.

*Volumes add, densities do not.* The correlation is a reciprocal sum precisely
because ideal volume additivity is the mixing rule. A unit test asserts a 50/50
G/H mixture has the mean of the two pure specific volumes, which is the
property that would break first if the expression were ever rewritten as a
weighted average of densities.

*No pole inside the operating range, checked rather than assumed.* Every
species density stays positive at every integer degree from 0 to 175. The
nearest root is component D at 208.57 C, which B-0008 measured and the harness
already asserts the sweep ceiling clears.

** Next
B-0011, =TESUB2=, =teprob.f:1415-1442=, and it is a different kind of item
from the last three: the first *Class B quirk fix* and the first
=book/src/deltas.md= entry, not a transcription.

The quirk, read off the listing: the loop runs =DO 250 J=1,100= and the
convergence test =IF(DABS(DT).LT.1.D-12)GO TO 300= is *on the loop-terminal
line*, so it is evaluated after each update. If the loop runs to exhaustion,
control falls through to =T=TIN=, which *silently restores the input guess and
returns as if nothing happened*. The caller cannot tell a converged answer from
a failed one.

Three things to get right. First, the port returns =Result= and
non-convergence is an error, per the backlog. Second, *Tier 1 must be compared
against the Fortran on the converging domain only*, so the sweep has to be
partitioned rather than gated wholesale, and the count of non-converging cases
is itself the delta measurement the item asks for. Third, =TESUB2= calls
=TESUB1= and =TESUB3= internally, both now bit-exact, so any Tier 1 failure is
in the iteration logic and nowhere else, which is a much easier debugging
position than the last three items had.

Expect the non-convergence count to be zero across the physical range. If it
is not, that is a genuine finding about the original and belongs in the delta
register with the exact cases that produced it.

Note on cost: =TESUB2= runs up to 100 Newton iterations per call, each calling
=TESUB1= and =TESUB3=. At =Sweep::FULL= that is up to 2x10^9 evaluations. The
=validate=/=ci= split from B-0009 was put in for exactly this, but B-0011
should measure before assuming =FULL= is affordable even in release, and say so
in its entry.

** Preflight
Clean and green. Fidelity green, local gfortran 15.2.0 matches. Provenance

Literal extraction over =teprob.f:1415-1442=: exactly one, =1.D-12=, and it
carries the =D= suffix. No precision hazard in this routine.

** What changed
=thermo::temperature_from_enthalpy=, the Newton solve, returning
=Result<f64, TemperatureError>=. =book/src/deltas.md= now exists, with the
class table, the =@delta= annotation convention and D-001 as its first entry.
=xtask validate= runs each Tier 1 test target separately.

*Phase 1 is complete.* =teprob.f:1375-1505= is claimed end to end.

** Validation numbers
=cargo xtask ci= green. *123 tests default, 169 with =oracle==, up from 120 and
164. Provenance *326/1589 (20.5%)*, up from 298. =xtask validate --tiers 1=
green in *78 s* wall clock, thirteen reports, every one 0 ULP.

*Tier 1, TESUB2. Bit-exact in all six combinations:*

| ITY | start | cases     | max rel err | max ulp | abandoned | round trip  |
|-----+-------+-----------+-------------+---------+-----------+-------------|
|   0 | warm  | 9,987,490 | 0.000e0     |       0 |         0 | 0e0         |
|   0 | cold  | 9,987,490 | 0.000e0     |       0 |         0 | 8.53e-14 C  |
|   1 | warm  | 9,987,490 | 0.000e0     |       0 |         0 | 0e0         |
|   1 | cold  | 9,987,490 | 0.000e0     |       0 |         0 | 3.69e-13 C  |
|   2 | warm  | 9,987,490 | 0.000e0     |       0 |         0 | 0e0         |
|   2 | cold  | 9,987,490 | 0.000e0     |       0 |         0 | 5.12e-13 C  |

*59,924,940 solves, zero differing bits, zero abandoned.* Worst cold-start
round trips at =dirichlet#720561 T=171.86=, =face#1309309 T=146.56= and
=face#96 T=144.14=; the boundary pool again produced two of the three.

*Delta D-001 measured effect: zero.* The Class B claim holds on the physical
domain.

** Surprises and decisions
*Two start strategies, and only one of them is a test.* A warm start, which is
what every call site does (=teprob.f:460-465= passes the previous step's
temperature), converges in one step and recovers the answer *exactly*: round
trip 0e0, not merely small. That is a realistic measurement and a useless
test, because the iteration never has to work. The cold start, from the far
end of the range, is what actually exercises Newton, and it costs 4x the wall
clock (12.1 s against 3.1 s) precisely because it is doing the iterating.
Measuring only the realistic case would have proved nothing about convergence.

*The cost fear from the B-0010 entry was wrong, and worth recording as wrong.*
That entry warned =TESUB2= could reach 2x10^9 evaluations at =Sweep::FULL= and
told this session to measure before assuming =FULL= was affordable. Measured:
the whole Tier 1 ladder is 78 s in release, of which =TESUB2= is about 46 s.
Newton converges in a handful of iterations, not the hundred the cap allows,
so the worst case never happens. The advice to measure was right; the estimate
behind it was pessimistic by two orders of magnitude.

*The delta is an assertion, not a paragraph.* =total_abandoned == 0= is checked
in the Tier 1 test, so if a future change to the sweep, the constants or the
iteration ever reaches the non-convergence path, it fails there. A measured
delta recorded only in prose decays into a claim about the past.

*The quirk is demonstrated, not just described.* A second test drives an
unreachable target through both implementations and asserts that the Fortran
returns the guess *bit for bit* while the port returns an error. That is what
makes the register entry checkable: the reconciliation rule the sweep uses
("where the port errors, the Fortran must have returned exactly the guess") is
itself verified against a case that actually takes the path.

*Fortran binds unary minus below division.* =DT=-ERR/DH= at =teprob.f:1437= is
=-(ERR/DH)=, not =(-ERR)/DH=. The two agree bit for bit because IEEE negation
is exact and round-to-nearest is symmetric, so this one is harmless, but the
parse differs from Rust's and the next routine's might not be so forgiving.
*Check operator precedence against Fortran's rules, not Rust's, every time.*

*=Result<f64, ...>= rather than =Result<Celsius, ...>=* as the backlog wrote
it. The =Celsius= newtype is still deferred, per the B-0009 decision; changing
that here would have meant touching all four routines and the harness. The
backlog item was ticked with the substitution noted in place.

** Next
*Phase 2, and its first action is decomposition, not porting.* =BACKLOG.org=
says to decompose a phase as the first action of its first iteration, and
=PLAN.org= is explicit that the load-bearing decision is "Splitting the right
-hand side": =TEFUNC= is not a pure function, since it advances the
disturbance walks, draws measurement noise and ticks the sampled analysers, so
it has to become a pure =derivatives()= plus an impure =advance_discrete()=.
Get that split right before porting a single balance equation, because every
subsequent item inherits it and Tier 2 depends on being able to evaluate the
derivative twice with the same answer.

*Make the decomposition its own iteration.* It should produce backlog items,
not code. The intended shape is already listed under "Phase 2" in
=BACKLOG.org=; the work is turning those ten bullets into sized items with
=:DEPS:= and picking the order.

Three things the decomposition should account for that this phase learned:

1. *Tier 2 needs the adversarial state catalogue before it needs volume.*
   Tier 1's =face= pool, an eighth of the sweep, produced most of the worst
   cases. The Tier 2 equivalent is the enumerable list already in =PLAN.org=,
   and it should be built by hand early rather than bolted on.
2. *The precision audit is per line range, not per file.* Three of the four
   routines had no suffix-free literal; the one that did cost 1.6e-5. Every
   Phase 2 item that touches =teprob.f= should extract its own range's literals
   as its first action, and =TEFUNC= at 169-940 is 772 lines of them.
3. *Expect bit equality and treat anything less as a finding.* Thirteen Tier 1
   reports read zero. Phase 2 will not manage that everywhere, since =exp= and
   =pow= enter through =libm=, but the burden of proof is on the difference.

* 2026-08-11 B-0012 Phase 2 decomposition and the RHS split decision
:PROPERTIES:
:ITEM:     B-0012
:PHASE:    2
:TIER:     none
:COMMIT:   3024a89
:GFORTRAN: 15.2.0
:STATUS:   DONE
:END:
** Preflight
Clean and green. Fidelity green, gfortran 15.2.0 matches. Provenance 326/1589
(20.5%), identical to the B-0011 close. Tier 1 re-run at full volume, *all
thirteen reports still 0 ULP*. No drift.

** What changed
No code. =BACKLOG.org= gained fourteen items, B-0013 to B-0026, each naming
its =teprob.f= range. =PLAN.org='s "Splitting the right-hand side" section was
rewritten, with a decision entry recording why.

** Validation numbers
=cargo xtask ci= green, unchanged at 123 tests default and 169 with =oracle=.
Provenance unchanged at 326/1589: nothing was ported.

** Surprises and decisions
*The plan's two-way split cannot work as written, and the reason is one line
number.* =PLAN.org= had =advance_discrete(t, y, u) -> Measurements= as a single
impure call. But =XMEAS= is assembled at =teprob.f:679-701= out of =FTM=,
=PTR=, =VLR= and the rest, all of which the *pure* evaluation produces. So the
noise addition at =711-716= and the analyzer latching at =717-761= cannot run
before the derivative, while the walk advance at =359-396= cannot run after
it. One impure entry point cannot be on both sides of the pure one.

Three phases: =advance_discrete= (pre), =derivatives= (pure, returning
=(Derivative, Signals)=), =sample_measurements= (post). Returning the
noise-free signals from the pure call is what stops the post-phase from having
to recompute the model, which would have doubled the cost of every Euler step.

This is a refinement, not a pivot: the pure/impure boundary and the reason for
it are exactly as the plan had them. *Recorded as a decision and applied to
=PLAN.org= rather than worked around, and it is the thing to review before
B-0014 builds on it.*

*The valve latch hoist is free, and is a claim to be proved, not assumed.*
=teprob.f:798-806= latches =VCV= from =XMV= at the very end of =TEFUNC=, just
before the valve derivatives at =807=. It reads only =XMV=, =VST=, =IVST= and
=TIME=. Nothing between =341= and =798= writes any of those, so hoisting it
into the pre-phase changes no number. B-0014 asserts this against the oracle;
reasoning about 450 lines of Fortran by eye is exactly how a silent divergence
gets in.

*=COMMON/TEPROC/= exposes every intermediate, which changes the shape of the
whole phase.* The oracle's =Teproc= mirror already carries =PPR=, =RR=,
=CRXR=, =FTM=, =FCM=, =HST=, =TST=, =SFR=, =QUR=, =QUS=, =QUC=, =UAR=, =VCV=,
=XDEL=, =TGAS=, =TPROD= and the rest. Tier 2 was written up as a
fifty-component derivative comparison, which is all-or-nothing and would have
meant nine items of unverified work before the first signal. It does not have
to be: *each item can diff its own =COMMON= field the moment it is written.*
That is the single most useful thing found this iteration, and it is what makes
a fourteen-item decomposition sized rather than reckless.

*The precision hazard is much denser in Phase 2 than in Phase 1.* Over
=teprob.f:473-520= there are twelve distinct numeric literals and *exactly one*
carries a =D= suffix, =0.767488334D0=. Single precision: =RG=998.9= (which
multiplies every ideal-gas partial pressure), the three Arrhenius
pre-exponentials =31.5859536=, =3.00094014=, =53.4060443=, the three
activation energies =40000.0=, =20000.0=, =60000.0=, the gas constant
=1.987=, and the fractional pressure orders =1.1544= and =0.3735=.

Six of those sit *inside a =DEXP= argument*. An =f32= ULP at 31.6 is about
1.9e-6 absolute, and =exp= turns an absolute error in its argument into a
relative error in its result, so reading =31.5859536= as =f64= would put the
first rate constant out by roughly 1e-6 relative: six orders past Tier 2's
1e-12 gate. Phase 1 found one such literal in four routines; Phase 2 has
eleven in forty-eight lines. The per-range literal audit is now written into
every item as its first action.

*Where bit equality will end.* =DEXP= first appears at =teprob.f:485=, in the
Antoine vapour pressures, and =**= with a fractional exponent at =507=. Those
go through the vendored =libm=, which is not gfortran's. B-0018 is therefore
the item where the thirteen-consecutive-zeros run most likely stops, and it is
written up to *quantify* that difference rather than to be surprised by it,
because every later item inherits whatever it turns out to be.

** Next
B-0013, the =State= and =Derivative= types. Small, structural, no oracle
needed for most of it.

The one thing worth care: the flat =[f64; 50]= view exists only for oracle
interop, and its index mapping must be pinned *against the golden trace*,
not transcribed from =teprob.f:417-440= by eye. The trace already records 50
states per step, so a round-trip test has ground truth available with no
Fortran toolchain required. Getting this mapping wrong would silently
mis-attribute every Tier 2 component for the rest of the phase.

After that B-0014 and B-0015 are both structural, and B-0016 is the
adversarial catalogue. *Resist starting the physics until B-0015 exists.* That
ordering is what made Phase 1 land thirteen bit-exact reports in four
iterations, and the temptation to port a balance equation first will be
strongest exactly when the harness is the thing that would have caught it.

* 2026-08-11 B-0013 State and Derivative, layout pinned against COMMON
:PROPERTIES:
