:END:
** Preflight
Clean and green. Fidelity green, gfortran 15.2.0. Provenance 326/1589 (20.5%),
identical to B-0012's close. Tier 1 full volume, all thirteen reports 0 ULP.

** What changed
=tepsim-core::state=: =Holdup=, =State=, =Derivative=, =Vessel=, and a
=VectorSpace= trait. Plus =crates/tepsim-oracle/tests/state_layout.rs=, which
pins the flat layout against the Fortran.

** Validation numbers
=cargo xtask ci= green. *131 tests default, 180 with =oracle==, up from 123 and
169. Provenance *350/1589 (22.0%)*, up from 326: =teprob.f:417-440= claimed.
Tier 1 re-run after the change, still thirteen reports at 0 ULP.

*Layout pin: 38 of 38 directly-unpacked slots match their =COMMON/TEPROC/=
variable bit for bit.* 32 mole slots, 4 energies, 2 cooling-water temperatures.

*Valve pin: 12 of 12.* Each valve perturbed by 1% in isolation moves exactly
one derivative, at exactly =1/VTAU= to within 1e-12 relative, and leaves the
other eleven at exactly zero.

Nominal vessel energies, for the record, since the pin depends on them being
distinguishable: reactor 2.8085227012634277, separator 0.5699318051338196,
stripper 0.3755297362804413, mixing 0.9218489527702332. Minimum pairwise
separation is well over the 1e-3 the test demands. *All four are f32 values
widened*, which is TEINIT's single-precision habit showing up in the state
vector itself.

** Surprises and decisions
*A round-trip test would have proved almost nothing here, and that is the main
lesson of this item.* Fifty in, fifty distinct out, is satisfied by any
permutation. Swapping the stripper and the mixing zone passes it, because both
are eight moles and an energy, and the mistake would then mis-attribute every
Tier 2 component for nine items without ever failing a test. The fix was to ask
the Fortran which slot is which: =TEINIT= calls =TEFUNC= at =teprob.f:1369=, so
=COMMON/TEPROC/= is already populated from =YY= by the time =init()= returns.

*=VPOS= is not in =COMMON/TEPROC/=*, so the last twelve slots could not be
pinned that way. They are pinned dynamically instead, through
=teprob.f:806=: =YP(I+38)=(VCV(I)-VPOS(I))/VTAU(I)=. Perturbing one valve must
move exactly one derivative by exactly =1/VTAU= and leave the rest at zero,
which identifies each slot individually rather than as a block. Worth
remembering as a technique: *when a variable is not observable, find the
equation it appears in and observe that instead.*

*The eight slots do not mean the same thing in every vessel.* Reactor and
separator split by phase, slots 1-3 being vapour A/B/C and 4-8 liquid D-H.
Stripper is all liquid, mixing zone all vapour. The field is therefore named
=moles= rather than any phase word, since no phase word is true of all four.
More consequentially, =UCVR(4..8)= is *not* a state at all: it is recomputed
from the equilibrium at =teprob.f:500-501= every call, and =UCLR(1..3)= is
forced to zero at =420-421=. B-0017 and B-0018 will need this.

*=Derivative= wraps =State= rather than repeating the fields.* Two
hand-maintained fifty-slot layouts would drift, and drift between a state and
its own derivative is the kind of bug that produces a plausible wrong answer
rather than a crash.

** Next
B-0014, the three-phase RHS skeleton. Structure only, no physics, and two
tests that are worth more than they look:

1. =derivatives= called twice with identical arguments must return
   bit-identical results. That is the entire point of the split, it is what
   Tier 2 rests on, and it is trivial to assert now and expensive to retrofit.
2. *The valve-latch hoist must be proved, not argued.* B-0012 claims nothing
   between =teprob.f:341= and =798= writes =XMV=, =VCV=, =VST= or =IVST=, so
   running the latch early changes no number. That is a claim about 450 lines
   of Fortran read by eye. Prove it against the oracle: drive a sequence of
   steps with the latch in each position and diff the state trajectories.

Note the skeleton has no physics, so =derivatives= should return an explicit
"not implemented" error rather than zeros. Zeros are a valid derivative and
would let a later item accidentally pass a test against a frozen plant.

* 2026-08-11 B-0014 The three-phase RHS skeleton
:PROPERTIES:
:ITEM:     B-0014
:PHASE:    2
:TIER:     none
:COMMIT:   1b01155
:GFORTRAN: 15.2.0
:STATUS:   DONE
:END:
** Preflight
Clean and green. Fidelity green, gfortran 15.2.0. Provenance 350/1589 (22.0%),
identical to B-0013's close. Tier 1 thirteen reports at 0 ULP.

** What changed
=tepsim-core::plant= with the three phases and =euler_step=. Plus
=crates/tepsim-oracle/tests/hoist_valve_latch.rs=, which proves the latch may
be moved.

** Validation numbers
=cargo xtask ci= green. *139 tests default, 188 with =oracle==, up from 131 and
180. Provenance unchanged at 350/1589: nothing new was ported.

*Hoist scan: 448 statements over =teprob.f:345-792=, zero writes* to =IDV=,
=XMV=, =VCV=, =VST= or =IVST=. The detector finds all nine writes inside the
hoisted block =793-804= when pointed at it, so the clean result is not a
broken scan.

** Surprises and decisions
*The scan found an off-by-one in my own B-0012 decision entry, on its first
run.* That entry said the hoisted block was =teprob.f:794-806= and that nothing
between =341= and =798= wrote what it reads. The test failed immediately on
=IVST(10)=IDV(14)= at line *793*. The block starts one line earlier than I
recorded it, having read the listing carefully the iteration before.

This is the entire argument for mechanical checks, and it is worth stating
plainly: *a line range read off a listing by eye and written into the design of
record was wrong, and the only reason it did not stay wrong is that something
counted.* Corrected in =PLAN.org=, =BACKLOG.org= and the module docs.

*The proof is static, not dynamic, and that was forced.* The obvious way to
prove a reordering is free is to run both orders and diff. That is not
available here: =reference/= may never be edited, so the reordered Fortran
cannot be built. The static proof is arguably stronger anyway, since it covers
every state rather than the ones a test happens to visit, but it depends on the
parser being right, so two companion tests check that the scan can see a write
and can tell an assignment from a comparison.

*The latch shares a loop with the derivative.* =DO 9020= at =teprob.f:799= runs
from the latch through =YP(I+38)= at =805=. The latch is impure and the
derivative is pure, so the port splits the loop in two. B-0025 needs to know
this; it is written into that item.

*=derivatives= returns =NotImplemented=, deliberately not zeros.* An all-zero
derivative is a valid answer, describing a plant that is not moving. A stub
returning it would let a later item pass a Tier 2 comparison against a state
where the real derivative happens to be small, and the failure would surface
several items downstream of its cause.

*=assert_derivatives_are_pure= lives in the library, not in a test file.*
Purity is a contract the plant offers rather than a property of one test, so it
should be as callable from =tepsim= or a user's crate as from here. It is
currently weak, since both evaluations fail identically, and it strengthens
without anyone having to remember to strengthen it.

*Purity is enforced by the signature.* =derivatives= takes =&self=; the crate
is =#![forbid(unsafe_code)]= and holds no statics, so there is nowhere for
interior mutability or hidden global state to live. The runtime assertion is a
belt on top of that, not the mechanism.

** Next
B-0015, the Tier 2 harness: state forcing and the =COMMON/TEPROC/= differ.

*Build it before any physics.* That ordering is what made Phase 1 land thirteen
bit-exact reports in four iterations, and the temptation to port a balance
equation first will be strongest exactly when the harness is the thing that
would have caught the mistake.

Three things it needs. First, forcing: set all 50 states, 12 MVs, 20 IDV flags,
the walk state and the RNG word, then call =TEFUNC= and snapshot. The oracle
already has every setter for this. Second, a differ that names a
=COMMON/TEPROC/= field and compares it, reusing =tier1::Comparison= unchanged,
which is the payoff for having made it generic over the case type. Third, two
of the three pools: nominal-trajectory states and scaled random perturbations.

One hazard to design around: =TEFUNC= advances the walks and draws noise, so
calling it twice on the same state does *not* give the same answer. Every
comparison must reset the RNG word and the walk state first, or the harness
will report differences that are its own fault. =Oracle::set_rng= and
=Oracle::set_wlk= exist for this. Get it wrong and Tier 2 becomes noise.

* 2026-08-11 B-0015 Tier 2 harness, and TEFUNC's hidden state
:PROPERTIES:
:ITEM:     B-0015
:PHASE:    2
:TIER:     2
:COMMIT:   336b8e8
:GFORTRAN: 15.2.0
:STATUS:   DONE
:END:
** Preflight
Clean and green. Fidelity green, gfortran 15.2.0. Provenance 350/1589 (22.0%),
identical to B-0014's close. Tier 1 thirteen reports at 0 ULP.

** What changed
=tepsim-oracle::tier2=: =Scenario=, =Snapshot=, =Pools=, =compare_field=,
=reproducible=. Fourteen harness tests.

** Validation numbers
=cargo xtask ci= green. *139 tests default, 202 with =oracle==, up from 139 and
188. Provenance unchanged at 350/1589: nothing ported. Tier 1 unchanged.

*The measurement that matters this iteration*: seeding =TCR/TCS/TCC/TCV= from
another point on the nominal trajectory, *changing nothing else*, moves this
many of the fifty derivatives:

| seed taken from | YP components moved |
|-----------------+---------------------|
| step 1          | none                |
| step 10         | 21                  |
| step 50         | 16                  |
| step 100        | 14                  |
| step 199        | 4                   |

Worst-affected components across the sweep: 9-18 (separator holdups and
energy), 28-38 (mixing zone and cooling water), and 22, 27, 36.

Other numbers: the generator word moves *22 of 41 measurements*; a one-degree
shift in =ADIST(5)= moves =TCWR= from 35 to 36 and *1 of 50 derivatives*; the
nominal trajectory moves *38 of 50 states* over 100 steps and *never trips*;
the perturbed pool spans *1.0e-9 to 9.96e-2* relative with *0 of 200 tripping*.

** Surprises and decisions
*=TEFUNC= has hidden state, and the harness found it by failing.* =TESUB2=
takes its temperature argument as both the initial guess and the result
(=teprob.f:1432=, =1438=), and the four call sites at =460-465= pass =TCR=,
=TCS=, =TCC= and =TCV= straight out of =COMMON/TEPROC/=. Every evaluation
warm-starts its Newton solves from the previous evaluation's answers. Since
Newton stops when the *step* falls below 1e-12, the converged value depends on
where it started, so the derivative is path-dependent at the 1e-13 level even
with the walks and the generator pinned.

The first failure was =TST(6)=, which is =TCV=, moving from 86.12011310855937
to 86.12011310855931 between two evaluations of the same state. That
propagated through =PTV= into =FTM(6)= and on into =YP(27)=.

*This is a finding about the port, not only about the harness.* The four
temperatures are genuinely part of the plant's persistent state, alongside the
latched valve commands. B-0017 must carry them, and the item now says so. A
port that recomputed them from a fixed initial guess would be *more* principled
and would not be bit-exact.

*A =Scenario= carries the whole =COMMON/TEPROC/= block, not a chosen subset.*
Deciding which fields carry over is precisely the judgement that was wrong the
first time. Restoring all of it cannot be wrong, costs one memcpy, and picks up
=VCV= and the analyser state without anyone having to think about them.

*Second bug, same shape: the pool evaluated every state at t=0.*
=teprob.f:397-406= resets the entire walk state whenever =TIME.EQ.0.D0=, so a
pool built that way would have exercised the disturbance machinery *not at
all*, and Tier 2 would have looked healthy while covering none of it. Each
recorded state now carries the time it was visited at.

*Two tests were testing false premises, and were replaced rather than
weakened.* "Evaluating twice without restoring the walks changes the answer"
is false on a short run: =TNEXT= starts at 0.1 hours (=teprob.f:404=), which is
360 one-second steps, so =TESUB5= never fires and the coefficients never move.
And "a differently seeded temperature changes a derivative" is false for a seed
a hundredth of a degree away, which converges to the same bits. Both
replacements prove the property directly instead: perturb =ADIST(5)= and watch
=TCWR= move, and walk the trajectory until a seed distance propagates.

Worth naming the pattern, because it recurred three times this iteration: *a
test that fails because its premise is wrong is not a test to relax.* Each time
the right move was to find what the property actually depends on and assert
that.

*Tripped states are counted, never hidden.* =teprob.f:807-811= zeroes all fifty
derivatives on a trip, so a tripped state is reproduced by any port, correct or
not. A Tier 2 result that did not report how many of its cases were tripped
would be overstating itself by exactly that many.

** Next
B-0016, the adversarial state catalogue: the third pool, built by hand from the
source.

Two things make it worth its own iteration rather than a paragraph in B-0017.
Tier 1 showed the boundary pool found most of the worst cases despite being an
eighth of the sweep, and every branch in Phase 2 is enumerable from the
listing, so this is a transcription job with a checklist rather than a design
job.

The checklist is already in the item. One addition from this iteration:
*construct each adversarial state and assert it actually reaches the branch it
was built for*, by reading the =COMMON= field that the branch writes. A state
intended to sit at =TCC = 5.292= that misses by a degree is worse than no state
at all, because it looks like coverage in the report. The harness can already
observe every one of those fields.

Note also that several adversarial states will trip the shutdown by
construction, especially the eight built to trip it. Those belong in the
catalogue and must be *excluded from the derivative comparison*, since a
tripped plant's derivative is all zeros. Count them and say so.

* 2026-08-11 B-0016 The adversarial state catalogue

** Preflight

** What changed
=tepsim-oracle::tier2::adversarial=: =Knob=, =Target=, =Boundary=, =seek=,
=catalogue=, =build=. Seven tests.

** Validation numbers
=cargo xtask ci= green. *139 tests default, 209 with =oracle==, up from 202.
Provenance unchanged: nothing ported. Tier 1 unchanged.

*17 of 17 boundaries constructed and verified.* Worst placement miss across the
whole catalogue is *1.7e-16 relative*; most are exactly 0.

| boundary                                   | knob setting | reached      | trips |
|--------------------------------------------+--------------+--------------+-------|
| VLR at the 10% heat-transfer breakpoint     |     0.133419 |       78.000 |       |
| VLR at the 50% heat-transfer breakpoint     |     0.667094 |      390.000 |       |
| TCC at the lower stripping-factor branch    |     0.076417 |        5.292 |       |
| TCC at the upper stripping-factor branch    |     2.906493 |      170.000 |       |
| TCC approaching the 177 C pole              |     3.031874 |      176.000 |       |
| TCR at the shutdown limit                   |     1.567177 |      175.000 | yes   |
| FTM(11) at the stripping-factor threshold   |     0.006667 |        0.100 |       |
| VLR upper / lower shutdown limit            | 1.449729 / 0.120811 | 847.548 / 70.629 | yes / |
| VLS upper / lower shutdown limit            | 2.456661 / 0.204722 | 423.774 / 35.315 | yes / |
| VLC upper / lower shutdown limit            | 1.805214 / 0.225652 | 282.516 / 35.315 | yes / |
| PTR at the pressure shutdown limit          |     1.180247 |    23261.850 |       |
| PTV = PTR, mixing-to-reactor clamp          |     0.876008 |        0.000 |       |
| PTR = PTS, reactor-to-separator clamp       |     1.036828 |        0.000 |       |
| PTV = PTS, recycle clamp                    |     0.853760 |       -0.000 |       |

*4 of 17 trip the shutdown*, reported separately. *37 of 50 state slots* are
reachable by some knob.

Nominal pressures, for reference: PTV 24028.5, PTR 21049.2, PTS 20514.6 mmHg.

** Surprises and decisions
*Bisecting the oracle beats inverting the model, and it is not close.* Placing
a state at =VLR/7.8 = 50= analytically means reimplementing the liquid volume,
density and temperature chain: a second copy of the code under test, whose
errors would place states *near* boundaries rather than on them. Near is the
worst possible outcome, because it exercises the ordinary branch while
appearing in the report as boundary coverage. Sixty bisections of the real
model land within 1.7e-16 every time.

*=seek= refuses rather than approximates.* If the bracket does not straddle the
target it returns =None= and the boundary is reported by name. That refusal
found the one real gap immediately, rather than silently producing a state
somewhere in the bracket.

*The purge-flow clamp is unreachable, and the number is worth keeping.*
=teprob.f:585-586= clamps =DLP = PTS - 760= at zero. Driving =PTS= as low as
the state allows, by removing the entire separator vapour inventory and cooling
to 0 C, floors it at *811.49 mmHg*: ideal-gas contribution 0.00, Antoine
contribution 811.49. Two reasons it cannot go lower: the ideal-gas term is
=UCVS*RG*TKS/VVS= and =TKS = TCS + 273.15= floors at 273.15 rather than at
zero, and the Antoine terms depend only on =TCS= and the *liquid* composition,
which the vapour inventory does not touch.

So the branch is 7% away and unreachable from that side. It is out of the
catalogue, the floor is pinned by its own test with a 1 mmHg tolerance, and
B-0021 carries a note to cover the branch with a unit test at a chosen
composition. *A named, measured gap is a finding; an unnamed one is a hole.*

*The compressor clamps are deferred to B-0021, not dropped.* They are branches
on =PR= against =CPPRMX=, and the catalogue can only aim at something it can
observe. There is nothing to observe until the compressor curve is ported. The
item now says so.

*Knobs scale holdups and energy together.* Scaling a vessel's liquid inventory
alone would change =ESR = ETR/UTLR= (=teprob.f:456=) and move the temperature
as a side effect, so a state built for a volume boundary would also be at an
unintended temperature. Scaling both leaves the specific energy fixed. The
temperature knobs move the energy alone, for the same reason in reverse.

** Next
B-0017, and it is the *first physics item in the phase*: unpack the state into
holdups, mole fractions, temperatures, densities and volumes,
=teprob.f:417-472=.

Three things it must do that the item now records:

1. *Carry the four solved temperatures in =Plant= as warm-start seeds.* B-0015
   measured what happens otherwise: seeding them from a different trajectory
   point moves up to 21 of the 50 derivatives. A port that recomputed them from
   a fixed guess would be more principled and would not be bit-exact.
2. *Extract the literals in =417-472= first.* Note =TKR=TCR+273.15= at =461=
   in particular: D-001 showed the same constant is single precision in one
   place and double in another, so it has to be read off this line.
3. *Use the intermediate differ.* =UCLR=, =XLR=, =ESR=, =TCR=, =DLR=, =VLR= and
   their separator and stripper counterparts are all in =COMMON/TEPROC/=, so
   the item can be verified field by field the moment it is written, against
   all three pools including the seventeen boundaries built here.

It calls =TESUB2= four times and =TESUB4= three times, all of which are already
bit-exact, so any Tier 2 failure is in the unpacking and nowhere else.

=unpack=. Plus =tier2_unpack.rs=, the first Tier 2 differential.

*Tier 2, all fourteen field groups, all three pools, bit-exact:*

Pools: 400 nominal trajectory states, 2,000 perturbations, 17 adversarial
boundaries = *2,417 states*. Per eight-component field that is *19,336 cases*;
per four-component group, 9,668; per three, 7,251.

| UCLR        | 19,336 | 0.000e0     |       0 |
| UCLS        | 19,336 | 0.000e0     |       0 |

| XLS         | 19,336 | 0.000e0     |       0 |

| totals      |  9,668 | 0.000e0     |       0 |

| TCR/TCS/TCC/TCV |  9,668 | 0.000e0 |       0 |
| TKR/TKS/TKV |  7,251 | 0.000e0     |       0 |

*Zero states failed to converge*, so delta D-001 still has not fired, now
including on the seventeen adversarial boundaries and 2,000 perturbations.

** Surprises and decisions
*No surprises, and that is the point.* Both hazards in this range were named by
earlier iterations before any code was written: B-0015 found the warm-start
seeds, and the phase's mandatory literal audit found the three single-precision
=273.15=. Neither cost a debugging session. The habit of ending each entry with
the specific expected failure is now three for three.

*The warm start is proved to matter, not assumed.* A test unpacks every nominal
state twice, once with the seeds the Fortran will use and once from a perfectly
reasonable fixed guess, and compares both against the oracle. The seeded run is
0 ULP; the fixed-guess run is not. Without that test =TemperatureSeeds= would
be a plausible-looking complication that nobody could justify removing or
keeping.

*The phase split is enforced by the types now, not by a comment.* Reactor and
separator carry A/B/C as vapour and D-H as liquid; the stripper is all liquid
and the mixing zone all vapour. =UCVR(4..8)= is deliberately left at zero,
because it does not come from the state at all: =teprob.f:500-501= computes it
from the equilibrium, which is B-0018.

*=Composition::new_unchecked= for the mole fractions.* An adversarial state can
drive a vessel's total to zero, and the resulting NaN is a true answer about a
degenerate state rather than something to assert away. The original does not
check either.

** Next
B-0018, vapour-liquid equilibrium and pressures, =teprob.f:473-499=.

*This is the item where bit equality most likely ends*, and it is written up to
measure that rather than to be surprised by it. =DEXP= first appears at
=teprob.f:485= in the Antoine vapour pressures, and Rust's vendored =libm= is
not gfortran's. Whatever difference it produces, every later item inherits it,
so the number belongs in that entry in full: per-component ULP, and whether it
is the exponential or the multiplication that carries it.

The precision hazards are already located. =RG=998.9= at =teprob.f:475= is
*single precision* and multiplies every ideal-gas partial pressure. All 24
Antoine coefficients are single too (B-0006), and they sit inside the =DEXP=
argument where an absolute error becomes a relative one.

Fourteen of the seventeen adversarial boundaries are pressure or volume
boundaries, so this item has unusually good coverage available from the start.

| PTR/PTS/PTV |  7,251 | 3.578e-16   |       2 | 0:7106 1:133 2:12         |
| XVR         | 19,336 | 4.843e-16   |       3 | 0:17642 1:1305 2:367 3:22 |
| XVS         | 19,336 | 4.302e-16   |       3 | 0:18110 1:978 2:238 3:10  |
| UTVR/UTVS   |  4,834 | 4.443e-16   |       3 | 0:4717 1:82 2:32 3:3      |
| UCVR        | 19,336 | 5.077e-16   |       4 | 0:18042 1:919 2:330 3:44 4:1 |
| UCVS        | 19,336 | 6.230e-16   |       3 | 0:18521 1:713 2:98 3:4    |

Worst overall *6.230e-16 on =UCVS(6)=* (F in the separator vapour) at
=perturbed#169=; worst ULP *4, on =UCVR(8)=* (H in the reactor vapour) at
=perturbed#1281=. Gate is 1e-12, so there are just under four orders of
headroom.

*The same differential under =--features oracle,libm-system=: 0 ULP on every
one of the nine groups.* That is the number that matters, and the reason is
below.

*Bit-exact under the default build too*, because no transcendental reaches
them: =PPR=/=PPS= for A, B and C (1,200 cases, 0 ULP) and =PTV= (200 cases,
0 ULP).

*The =exp= measurement, which every later item inherits.* Over the whole
Antoine argument range this model reaches, [0.5868, 13.0825]:

| comparison                        | cases     | differ  | worst  |
|-----------------------------------+-----------+---------+--------|
| Rust =f64::exp= vs gfortran =DEXP= | 1,500,005 | 0       | 0 ulp  |
| vendored =libm= vs gfortran =DEXP= | 1,500,005 | 9.945%  | -1 ulp |
| vendored =libm= vs =f64::exp=      | 15,000    | 10.047% | -1 ulp |

The first two came from a throwaway gfortran program writing =Z16.16= hex over
a 0.001-degree grid. The third is the permanent form, re-measured on whatever
machine runs the suite by
=the_vendored_and_platform_exp_differ_only_by_rounding=; it is the vendored
crate against the platform one, and the =libm-system= run is what establishes
that the platform one is what gfortran calls.

** Surprises and decisions
*Bit equality ended exactly where B-0017 said it would, and the sign is
consistent.* The vendored crate is one ULP *low*, never high, on all 149,181
disagreeing arguments. It is not noise around the correctly-rounded value; it
is a systematic downward bias in that library's =exp=.

*The bit-exactness claim was moved rather than dropped, and that was the real
work of this iteration.* The obvious reading of the plan is that from here on
Phase 2 is held to 1e-12. That is a bad trade and it is measurable: rewriting
=UTVR= from =ptr*vvr/RG/tkr= to =ptr*vvr/(RG*tkr)= is worth 1-2 ULP, which
*fails* the =libm-system= run and *passes* the default 1e-12 gate silently. It
was tried, both ways, before this was written down. Nine items remain in Phase
2 and every one of them is a sum or a product chain where the same mistake is
available.

So =PLAN.org='s =libm-system= feature landed now rather than at Tier 4, and
=xtask ci= runs the Phase 2 differentials twice: default for the shipping
number, =libm-system= for 0 ULP on the algebra. =CLAUDE.md= carries this as a
rule so the next eight items do not each rediscover it.

*Two consequences worth knowing.* =libm-system= makes =tepsim-core= =std=, so
=no_std= is now =cfg_attr='d on =not(feature = "libm-system")=; the default
build, which is every build the gate exercises, is unchanged. And the
bit-exactness test's failure message deliberately names *both* causes, because
if gfortran ever stops resolving =DEXP= to the platform =exp=, that test fails
too, and a message asserting "the difference is in the algebra" would send a
session down the wrong path for a whole iteration.

*=libm::exp(1.0)= is one ULP above the correctly rounded =e=.* Found by
writing the obvious assertion and watching it fail. It is now asserted as an
equality on bits, in both configurations, so a future version of either library
changing its rounding is a test failure rather than a silent shift in every
recorded number.

*Teeth, checked by breaking things.* Typing =RG= as a double literal instead of
=single(998.9)= moves the plant 2.444e-8 relative, four orders past the gate,
and fails loudly. Reassociating =UTVR= fails only the =libm-system= run, as
above.

** Next
B-0019, reaction kinetics, =teprob.f:503-520=.

*It is the same hazard again, one function worse.* =**1.1544= and =**0.3735= at
=507-508= go through =pow=, which is harder to round than =exp= and where the
vendored crate should be expected to disagree with gfortran *more* than 9.9% of
the time, not less. =DEXP= also reappears at =503-505= inside three Arrhenius
terms whose activation energies are single precision. Measure =pow= the way
this entry measured =exp=, in a permanent test, and record the percentage and
the worst ULP before writing the log entry, not after.

Run the differential in *both* configurations from the start. The =libm-system=
run is where 0 ULP is still asserted, and =RR(1..4)= is a four-term product
chain, which is precisely the shape where a reassociation is invisible to
1e-12.

The naming quirk is already located and does not need rediscovering: =R1F= and
=R2F= arrive as disturbance drift factors from =TESUB8= and are *reassigned in
place* as pressure powers at =507-508=. Read =503-511= as written rather than
tidying it, and give it a Class A delta entry.

=RR(1)=* at =perturbed#1279=. Percentiles p50=0, p90=2, p99=3 on all three.
Gate is 1e-12.

*Under =--features oracle,libm-system=: 0 ULP on all three fields.* The
algebra is exact; the entire 8.5e-16 is =exp= and =pow= rounding.

*=pow=, measured the way B-0018 measured =exp=.* 40,000 arguments over
[0.25, 5000] at both orders: *3,992 differ (9.980%), worst -1 ULP*. B-0019 was
written expecting =pow= to be worse than =exp=, since it is the harder function
to round. It is not: 9.980% against 9.945%, both one ULP, both always low.
Every later item can budget for one number rather than two.

*=CRXR(2)= is exactly zero in the Fortran on all 200 nominal states*, which is
what makes delta D-003 benign rather than a latent garbage read.

** Surprises and decisions
*The precision trap in this range is not the one the backlog named.* It named
the single-precision literals, which were expected and handled. The one that
would have cost a session is =40000.0/1.987= at =teprob.f:503=: *both operands
are REAL(4), so Fortran evaluates the division itself in single precision* and
only then widens. Fortran types an expression from its operands, never from its
destination.

Three candidate readings, checked against a gfortran program compiled with the
pinned flags rather than argued from the standard:

| form | bits |
|------+------|
| what gfortran stores | =0x40D3A8B680000000= |
| =single(40000.0) / single(1.987)= | =0x40D3A8B670F51E20= |
| =40000.0_f64 / 1.987_f64= | =0x40D3A8B66F0ED0F9= |

The tell is 29 trailing zero mantissa bits. Getting it wrong measures
*2.398e-6* on the plant, six orders past the gate, and it sits inside a =DEXP=
argument so it is amplified rather than carried. =constants::single_quotient=
exists for it and a test asserts the trailing-zero signature on all three
activation energies.

*The second trap was mine, and the protocol caught it.* The pre-exponentials
were first written hand-rounded to =31.585_954= for =31.5859536=. Those are
*different f32 values*, 6e-8 apart. CLAUDE.md already says constants are
transcribed and never retyped; writing the digits verbatim and letting =single=
round is not a style preference, and this is the concrete reason.

*=pow= was expected to be worse than =exp= and is not.* Recorded because the
opposite expectation is the natural one and would otherwise be re-derived every
time someone reasons about the error budget.

*D-003 was not anticipated by the backlog.* =CRXR(2)= is never assigned by any
line of =teprob.f= and is read at =763=. It gives the right answer, zero,
because =COMMON= is static storage. The guarantee is the linker's, not the
model's, so the oracle is now *required* to report zero rather than assumed to.

*D-002 is the one a port without a differential would get wrong.* Reading
=R1F= at =teprob.f:510= as still the drift factor produces a coherent rate law
that is self-consistent on every run. It measures 100% relative error against
the oracle. Both readings were tried.

*=xtask ci= now takes a list.* =LIBM_SYSTEM_TESTS= names the files re-run
under the platform libm, so B-0020 onward append one string instead of editing
the command.

** Next
B-0020, stream compositions, molecular weights and enthalpies,
=teprob.f:529-564=.

*Audit every inline literal in the range before writing any code.* This
iteration's most expensive near-miss was an arithmetic expression between two
suffix-less literals, which looks like nothing at all in a listing. The rule
that catches it: in fixed-form Fortran, any sub-expression whose operands are
all suffix-less literals is evaluated at =REAL(4)= and widened afterwards.
Grep the range for =/= and =*= between bare numbers, not just for the numbers
themselves.

The range's own named quirk is easier: =HST(10)=HST(9)= at =teprob.f:562= is an
aliasing, not a copy, and should be reproduced as one. Nine =TESUB1= calls feed
it, all of which Tier 1 proved bit-exact, so any difference is in the assembly.

Run the differential in both configurations from the start, and append
=tier2_streams= to =LIBM_SYSTEM_TESTS=.

*Under =--features oracle,libm-system=: 0 ULP on all four fields.*

*=HST(9)= differs from =HST(10)= on 200 of 200 nominal states*, which is the

** Surprises and decisions
*=HST(10)=HST(9)= is a snapshot, not an alias, and the difference is
seventy lines away.* The backlog asked for it to be "reproduced as the
aliasing it is". It is not an aliasing. =teprob.f:562= copies the separator
vapour enthalpy into the purge, and =teprob.f:601= then adds the compressor
work to =HST(9)= alone:

: HST(9)=HST(9)+CPDH/FTM(9)

The recycle gains compressor work; the purge does not, because it was copied
first. Reading 562 as a live alias gives the purge a share of work it never
receives.

The consequence for validation is the part worth recording. On return,
=COMMON='s =HST(9)= is *not* what this range computes, and =HST(10)= is. So
this module's stream 9 enthalpy is compared against slot 10. Comparing against
=HST(9)= would have failed, and the natural reading of that failure is a wrong
enthalpy rather than an early one, which is a bad afternoon. A test asserts
the two slots differ on every nominal state, so the mapping cannot be tidied
away later without producing an explicable failure.

*The walk-driven feed values are recomputed, not read back.* =teprob.f:407-412=
overwrites =XST(1..3,4)=, =TST(1)= and =TST(4)= from =TESUB8= and the =IDV=
flags. Reading them out of =COMMON= after the call would have been simpler and
would have made six comparisons trivially true. Recomputing them costs four
lines and confirms the reading of =407-412= at 0 ULP, which B-0024 would
otherwise have to establish from scratch.

*No new precision trap.* The range was audited for the =40000.0/1.987= pattern
that B-0019 found, per that entry's instruction. There is none: every literal
here stands alone. The feed compositions are single precision as expected, and
typing them as doubles measures 2.526e-8.

*=XMWS= is six-of-thirteen, and the other seven are dead rather than latent.*
They are never written and never read. Filling them in would look like tidying
and would be a divergence with no test to catch it, since nothing reads them.

** Next
B-0021, valve-lagged flows, pressure-driven flows and the compressor,

*It is the largest remaining item in Phase 2 and the first with real control
flow.* Everything so far has been straight-line arithmetic. This range has four
=IF(DLP.LT.0.0)DLP=0.0= clamps, the compressor pressure-ratio clamps, and
=IDV(6)= and =IDV(7)= gating two feeds.

Three things are already known and should not be rediscovered:

The *purge clamp is unreachable from a physical state*. =PTS= floors at 811.49
mmHg against a 760 threshold, so no sampled state will ever exercise it. It
needs a unit test at a chosen composition. The other three clamps are covered
by B-0016 adversarial states.

*Both compressor pressure-ratio clamps were deferred here from B-0016*, because
they are branches on =PR= against =CPPRMX= and =CPPRMX= does not exist until
the compressor curve does. Add adversarial states for them as part of this
item, and check =adversarial::build='s =missed= list stays empty.

The =1.0e-10= addend on stream 4 is a *Class B delta* and needs an entry.

=HST(9)= becomes checkable in this item: once =CPDH= and =FTM(9)= exist, the
post-bump value is what =COMMON= holds, and B-0020's stream 9 comparison
against slot 10 can be joined by a direct one against slot 9.

Append =tier2_flows= to =LIBM_SYSTEM_TESTS= in =xtask/src/main.rs=.

Sizing: if the compressor and the clamps together will not fit with tests and
validation, split at =teprob.f:588=, taking the valve-lagged and
pressure-driven flows first and the compressor second.

** Preflight
Clean and green, identical to B-0020's closing numbers. gfortran 15.2.0,
unchanged. Provenance *576/1589 (36.2%)* on exit, from 516.

** What changed
=tepsim_core::flows= ports =teprob.f:565-613=, with =VRNG= from =1109-1117= and
the compressor limits from =1170-1171=. Six valve-lagged flows, three
pressure-driven ones with their square-root resistances, the compressor curve
and its bleed, the component flows =FCM=, and the =HST(9)= compressor bump.
=math= gained =sqrt=. The adversarial catalogue grew from 17 boundaries to 20.

** Validation numbers
*Tier 2, five fields, all three pools.* 2,419 states, zero convergence
failures.

| field | cases   | max rel err | max ulp | p99 ulp |
|-------+---------+-------------+---------+---------|
| FTM   |  24,200 | 1.222e-13   |     743 |       3 |
| FCM   | 193,520 | 2.268e-14   |     178 |       4 |
| FWR/FWS/AGSP | 7,257 | 0.000e0 |       0 |       0 |
| CPDH  |   2,419 | 2.034e-15   |      18 |       2 |
| HST(9) post-bump | 2,419 | 6.522e-15 | 42 |     2 |

Worst overall *1.222e-13 on =FTM(6)=* at =perturbed#609=; worst ULP *743 on
=FTM(7)=* at =perturbed#1112=. Gate is 1e-12, so one order of headroom, down
from four.

*Under =--features oracle,libm-system=: 0 ULP on all five fields.*

*=UAC= via =QUC=: 300 of 300 nominal states below 100 C, max rel err 0.000e0,
0 ULP.*

*=FWR=, =FWS= and =AGSP= are 0 ULP under the default build*, being pure
valve arithmetic with no transcendental upstream.

** Surprises and decisions
*The ULP tail exploded and the relative error did not.* Every earlier item ran
at 4 to 7 ULP. This one reaches 743. That is =sqrt=, and it is not a defect:
near a flow clamp =DLP= approaches zero, and \\(\\sqrt{}\\) of a number whose
last bits differ has a *relative* error in its own last bits amplified by the
derivative \\(1/(2\\sqrt{x})\\), which diverges as \\(x \\to 0\\). The absolute
error stays tiny, which is why max relative error is 1.2e-13 while max ULP is
743.

The =libm-system= run settles it: those same states are *bit-identical* there.
So the amplification is entirely of the one-ULP =exp= difference, and none of
it is the algebra. Without that second run this would have been a genuinely
hard afternoon, because 743 ULP looks exactly like a real bug.

*The =CPPRMX= boundary state does not clamp, and that is correct.* The first
version of the coverage test asserted it would. =teprob.f:591= is
=IF(PR.GT.CPPRMX)=, strictly greater, so a state sitting exactly on the
threshold takes the *unclamped* path. That is now asserted deliberately, since
misreading =.GT.= as =.GE.= is invisible everywhere except at that one point,
and a third catalogue state was added inside the clamped region so the taken
branch is exercised too.

Both targets aim at the *single-precision* =CPPRMX= = 1.2999999523162842. A
state placed at the double literal 1.3 sits on the wrong side of the
comparison, which would have produced a boundary that verified cleanly and
covered nothing.

*=UAC= is a =TEFUNC= local, not a =COMMON= field.* =teprob.f:326= declares it
among the locals, so there is no symbol to read it back from. Rather than
hoisting it the way =ISD= was hoisted, it is checked through
=QUC=UAC*(100.0-TCC)= at =teprob.f:678=: the identical expression, so the check
is exact rather than a division that would introduce rounding of its own.
States at or above 100 C are counted and excluded, because there =QUC= is zero
whatever =UAC= is.

*=sqrt= went into =math= for a mundane reason and the docs say so.* IEEE-754
specifies it exactly, it is one hardware instruction, and it takes no
=libm-system= variant. It is in the module only because =f64::sqrt= is a =std=
method and the core is =no_std=. Spelled out because the obvious reading, that
=sqrt= is a third portability hazard, is wrong.

*=cargo test -p tepsim-core --lib= does not enforce =no_std=.* The test harness
links =std=, and inherent impls on primitives resolve from any linked crate, so
=f64::sqrt= compiled cleanly under =--lib= and failed only when =tepsim-oracle=
built the library normally. The gate catches it either way, through =clippy
--all-targets= and through =cargo test --workspace=. Worth knowing before
trusting a green =--lib= run during iteration.

*The purge clamp is still unreachable, re-measured.* A test now sweeps the
whole pool, 400 nominal, 1,000 perturbed and all 20 boundaries, and asserts the
minimum =PTS= stays above 760. It does. The branch is covered by a unit test at
a chosen composition, which is the only way to reach it.

B-0022, the stripper, =teprob.f:614-662=.

*For the first time in Phase 2 the coverage exists before the code does.*
B-0016 built all three =TMPFAC= branch boundaries (=TCC= at 5.292, at 170, and
approaching the 177 pole) and both sides of the =FTM(11) > 0.1= threshold. Do
not build new adversarial states for them; check =adversarial::build='s
=missed= list stays empty and use what is there.

The range sets =SFR(4..8)=, =FIN=, =FCM(I,5)=, =FCM(I,12)=, =FTM(5)=,
=FTM(12)=, =XST(I,5)=, =XST(I,12)=, =HST(5)= and =HST(12)=. Those are exactly
the three streams B-0020 and B-0021 left at zero, so after this item the stream
table and the flow network are complete except for =FTM(7)=, the reactor inlet
alias at =teprob.f:657-661=.

Two things to read carefully rather than infer. =teprob.f:620= has
=363.744/(177.-TCC)= with a *pole at 177 C*, and B-0016 built a state at 176 to
sit near it; the constant =2.22579488= is subtracted from it. And the
=FTM(11).GT.0.1= branch at =614= switches the *whole block* to fixed stripping
factors, so both sides need to appear in the differential.

Audit the range for the =40000.0/1.987= single-precision folding trap before
writing code. =363.744/(177.-TCC)= is *not* an instance, since =TCC= is double,
but a quotient of two bare literals would be.

Append =tier2_stripper= to =LIBM_SYSTEM_TESTS= in =xtask/src/main.rs=.

** Preflight

** What changed
=tepsim_core::stripper= ports =teprob.f:614-662=: the stripping factors with
their three temperature branches and the idle switch, the two outlet streams,
and the reactor-inlet alias. *All thirteen streams are now populated*, which is
the precondition B-0025 needs.

** Validation numbers
*Tier 2, eleven fields, all three pools.* 2,421 states, zero convergence
failures. The report is split between what the column computes and what it
copies, because the two behave completely differently.

| field | cases  | max rel err | max ulp |
|-------+--------+-------------+---------|
| SFR   | 19,368 | 0.000e0     |       0 |
| FTM (5, 12) | 4,842 | 0.000e0 |       0 |
| FCM (5, 12) | 38,736 | 0.000e0 |     0 |
| XST (5, 12) | 38,736 | 0.000e0 |     0 |
| HST (5, 12) | 4,842 | 0.000e0 |      0 |
| TST (5, 7, 12) | 7,263 | 0.000e0 |  0 |
| XST (7) | 19,368 | 0.000e0    |       0 |
| HST (7) | 2,421 | 0.000e0     |       0 |
| FTM (7) | 2,421 | 1.484e-15  |      12 |
| FCM (7) | 19,368 | 1.610e-15 |      13 |

*Nine of the eleven fields are bit-exact under the default build*, and that is
asserted rather than observed. Under =libm-system=, all eleven are.

*All four branches are entered by the pool*: idle, linear, pinned and
hyperbolic, checked by a test that fails if any is not.

*=SFR(1..3)= are bit-identical to the =TEINIT= constants* on 300 nominal and
all 21 adversarial states.

** Surprises and decisions
*The stripper introduces no error of its own, and the report now says so.*
Everything it computes descends from valve-lagged flows and unpacked
compositions, neither of which touches a transcendental, so its own outlets are
bit-exact even under the vendored libm. Only =FTM(7)= and =FCM(7)= differ, and
only because they are copies of stream 6, whose *flow* came through a
square-root resistance while its composition and temperature came from the
unpack.

That is why the fields are reported split rather than together. Averaging a
clean result with a borrowed one hides both facts, and the assertion names the
two exempt fields explicitly rather than exempting anything labelled "alias",
so error appearing where it structurally cannot still fails.

*B-0021's finding repeated on the other side, and it is now a rule.* The
coverage test failed: no state reached the pinned temperature branch. The cause
is the same as the =CPPRMX= case. =teprob.f:617= is =IF(TCC.LT.5.292)=, and
B-0016's boundary state sits *exactly* on 5.292, so it takes the hyperbolic
branch.

Every comparison in =TEFUNC= is strict. A boundary state therefore proves the
comparison is strict, which is valuable and invisible anywhere else, and leaves
the branch behind it unexercised. Those are two different states and both are
needed. That is now written into =adversarial='s module documentation with both
pairs named, so the next threshold gets both from the start rather than
discovering it a third time.

The catalogue is 21 boundaries: 17 from B-0016, three from B-0021, one here.

*=SFR(1..3)= are never recomputed, and the loop hides it.* =teprob.f:623-627=
and =629-633= write slots 4 through 8 only; =teprob.f:643= reads all eight. So
A, B and C strip at fixed =TEINIT= fractions for the life of the run, while the
loop that consumes them runs =I=1,8= and looks as though all eight came from
the branch above. Asserted against the oracle rather than assumed.

*Delta D-005.* The =TEINIT= values for =SFR(4..8)= are dead, overwritten on the
first evaluation through either branch. Worth an entry because the eight-line
block looks uniform and is not: omitting =1129-1133= is correct and omitting
=1126-1128= is silently wrong in every product composition. The precision is
inconsistent across the same boundary (slots 7 and 8 carry =D= suffixes, the
rest do not), which has no effect precisely because those five are dead.

*The 177 C pole is unreachable by seven degrees.* The linear branch takes over
at 170, and the two branches agree to within 0.1% at the handover, which is the
evidence that 170 and 120.262 were chosen together. B-0016's state at 176 C
covers the *guard*, not the pole.

** Next
B-0023, heat transfer, =teprob.f:663-678=. The last physics item before the
balances.

*Two integer powers, and they must not go through =pow=.* =AGSP**2= at
=teprob.f:670= and =(FTM(8)/3528.73)**4= at =674= have integer exponents, and
gfortran emits multiplications for those rather than a libm call. Routing them
through =crate::math::pow= would change the last bits. =crate::math::pow='s
documentation already says this; the test that proves it is
=the_cube_in_the_compressor_curve_is_not_a_pow_call= in =flows=, and this item
should get the same for both of its powers.

All three =UARLEV= branches are already covered: B-0016 built =VLR= at 78 and
at 390, the two breakpoints of =VLR/7.8= at 10 and 50. Given B-0022's finding,
*check which side of each breakpoint those states actually land on* before
assuming they exercise the branch you want, and add a state past the threshold
if they do not. =teprob.f:663= is =.GT.= and =665= is =.LT.=, so both boundary
states take the middle ramp.

The =TCC<100= steam cutoff at =678= needs the same treatment. The nominal
trajectory sits near 65 C, so the *cutoff* side is the one at risk of being
unexercised, not the active side. B-0021 already measured 300 of 300 nominal
states below 100 C, which says exactly that.

=QUR= and =QUS= read =TESUB8(10)= and =TESUB8(11)=, so they take walk inputs
like the three items before them.

Append =tier2_heat= to =LIBM_SYSTEM_TESTS= in =xtask/src/main.rs=.

* 2026-08-27 B-0023 Heat transfer, and an integer power that is not a chain
:PROPERTIES:
:ITEM:     B-0023
:PHASE:    2
:TIER:     2
:COMMIT:   089602e
:GFORTRAN: 15.2.0
:STATUS:   DONE
:END:
** Preflight
Clean and green, identical to B-0022's closing numbers. gfortran 15.2.0,
unchanged. Provenance *633/1589 (39.8%)* on entry, 649 (40.8%) on exit.

** What changed
=tepsim_core::heat= ports =teprob.f:663-678=: the reactor coil with its
piecewise level ramp and agitator quadratic, the condenser's saturating
coefficient, and the stripper reboiler with its steam cutoff. That is the last
of the physics; only the measurements and the balances remain.

** Validation numbers
*Tier 2, four fields, all three pools.* 2,421 states, zero convergence
failures.

| field | cases | max rel err | max ulp |
|-------+-------+-------------+---------|
| UAR   | 2,421 | 0.000e0     |       0 |
| QUR   | 2,421 | 0.000e0     |       0 |
| QUS   | 2,421 | 3.969e-13   |   2,521 |
| QUC   | 2,421 | 0.000e0     |       0 |

*Three of four are bit-exact under the default build.* =QUS= is not, and the
reason is structural: it is the only one downstream of =FTM(8)=, which came
through a square-root resistance, and the quartic at =teprob.f:674= then
amplifies that. 0 ULP on all four under =libm-system=.

*=QUS= is the largest relative error in the port so far, at 3.969e-13 against
a 1e-12 gate.* The trend across Phase 2 is worth watching: 6.2e-16 (B-0018),
8.5e-16 (B-0019), 5.1e-16 (B-0020), 1.2e-13 (B-0021), 4.0e-13 here. B-0026
should confirm the assembled derivative stays inside the gate rather than
assuming it.

*=X**4=, measured against gfortran over 200,000 values with the pinned flags:*

| candidate | matches |
|-----------+---------|
| =(x*x)*(x*x)= | 200,000 of 200,000 |
| =((x*x)*x)*x= | 132,040 |
| =pow(x, 4.0)= | 99,523 |

*Branch coverage.* All three =UARLEV= branches and both sides of the
=TCC < 100= cutoff are entered by the pool, asserted rather than assumed.

** Surprises and decisions
*The integer-power expansion has a shape, and it had to be measured.* Knowing
that gfortran does not call =pow= is not enough: =(x*x)*(x*x)= and
=((x*x)*x)*x= are both "multiplication" and they differ on a third of inputs.
gfortran uses binary exponentiation. Writing the chain fails the =libm-system=
run at 4 ULP and passes the 1e-12 gate silently, which is checked by breaking
it deliberately.

*B-0022's rule paid off immediately.* Both =UARLEV= breakpoint states take the
ramp, exactly as predicted, because =teprob.f:663= and =665= are strict. This
time the coverage test was written *first* rather than discovering the gap
afterwards, and the perturbed pool turned out to reach both flat branches
already, so no new catalogue entries were needed. The check is cheap and the
alternative is two implemented-but-never-evaluated branches.

*The level ramp does not meet its flat sections.* =0.025= at =teprob.f:668= is
single precision, stored as 0.02500000037252903, so the ramp gives
3.725290298461914e-9 at level 10 instead of 0, and 1.0000000186264515 at level
50 instead of 1. Since both comparisons are strict, =UARLEV= is discontinuous
by those amounts as the level crosses either breakpoint.

This is faithful reproduction, not a delta: it is what the original computes,
the gaps are eight orders below the quantity, and the coefficient is an
empirical fit anyway. It is written down because "the ramp meets the flats" is
the obvious assumption, it is false, and the test asserting it failed for a
reason that looks exactly like a porting error.

*The agitator parabola's roots are 1.149 and 4.351, not 1 and 5.* That claim
went into the module documentation before it was checked, and the test caught
it. The parabola peaks at 2.75, above the agitator's 1.5 to 2.5 range, so the
coefficient rises monotonically everywhere the plant can reach and never gets
near a root.

*Signed zeros propagate and the sign is load-bearing.* On a dry coil =UAR= is
=+0= and =QUR= is =-0=, because the reactor runs hotter than its coolant outlet
and the difference carries the sign through the multiplication. Asserted on
bits, since =-0.0 == 0.0= is true and would hide a sign flip while the oracle
comparison, which is on bits, would not.

** Next
B-0024, measurements, shutdown detection and the sampled analysers,
=teprob.f:679-761=.

*Only the first half belongs to this phase.* =679-710= is pure: the twenty-two
noise-free measurements and the eight shutdown tests. =711-761= draws
measurement noise and ticks the analysers, which is the *post-phase* of the
three-phase split in =crate::plant=, and it needs the RNG and the walk
machinery that Phase 3 brings.

Per the sizing rule, take =679-710= and split the rest into a child item if it
will not fit. That is the natural seam anyway: =Plant::derivatives= ends at
=710= and =Plant::sample_measurements= begins at =711=.

Two things already located. The =XMEAS(20)= double assignment at
=teprob.f:698-699= is a Class A delta: the first write is dead. And the eight
shutdown conditions should land as a typed condition rather than a bare flag,
so that =derivatives= can report *which* limit tripped instead of only that
one did.

The adversarial catalogue already has states on five of the eight shutdown
limits, and =tier2_adversarial= reports which of them trip. Use that: a tripped
state has an all-zero derivative in the Fortran, which any port reproduces, so
those states are coverage of the *detector* and not of the model.

Append =tier2_measurements= to =LIBM_SYSTEM_TESTS= in =xtask/src/main.rs=.

* 2026-08-27 B-0024a Noise-free measurements, and a trip that was never being tested
:PROPERTIES:
:ITEM:     B-0024a
:PHASE:    2
:TIER:     2
:COMMIT:   11feaf7
:GFORTRAN: 15.2.0
:STATUS:   DONE
:END:
** Preflight
Clean and green, identical to B-0023's closing numbers. gfortran 15.2.0,
unchanged. Provenance *649/1589 (40.8%)* on entry, 681 (42.9%) on exit.

** What changed
B-0024 was *split*. =tepsim_core::measurements= ports =teprob.f:679-710=, the
pure half: twenty-two unit conversions and the eight shutdown limits, with the
trip carried as a typed set of causes rather than a bare flag. The impure half,
=711-761=, became *B-0024b* at Phase 3, which is where =TESUB6= and the walk
machinery arrive. The seam is the one =crate::plant= already draws between
=derivatives= and =sample_measurements=.

The adversarial catalogue grew from 21 boundaries to 25.

** Validation numbers
*Tier 2, all three pools, 2,421 states*, zero convergence failures.

| field | cases  | max rel err | max ulp | p99 ulp |
|-------+--------+-------------+---------+---------|
| XMEAS(1..22) | 53,350 | 8.774e-15 | 78 |     1 |
| ISD as 0 or 1 | 2,421 | 0.000e0   |  0 |     0 |

*=ISD= agrees on every state*, 8 tripping and 2,413 not. 0 ULP on both fields
under =libm-system=.

*All eight shutdown causes are now reached by the pool*, up from four.

** Surprises and decisions
*Typing the shutdown causes is what exposed the coverage gap.* The original
records only that something tripped. Once the port named the eight conditions
and a test counted which ones the pool actually reached, the answer was four,
and two separate reasons emerged.

B-0016's "TCR at the shutdown limit" state *is* reported as tripping by
=tier2_adversarial=, and it does trip, on =teprob.f:703= (reactor pressure)
rather than on =706= (temperature) which it is named for. Heating the reactor
raises the Antoine partial pressures faster than =TCR= climbs, so the plant is
already past 3000 kPa gauge by 175 C and the temperature limit is masked. The
state was doing its job as a *boundary*; it was never evidence for the branch
it is labelled with, and nothing said so because =ISD= is one flag.

And the three low-level trips reached *zero* states. Their boundary states sit
exactly on 2, 1 and 1 cubic metres, and =teprob.f:705=, =708= and =710= are all
=.LT.=, so none of them fires. That is B-0022's rule for the third time, now on
three branches at once.

Four states past limits were added, and the coverage test requires all eight
causes rather than a count. Five of the eight limits had been validated only on
their not-tripping side until this iteration.

*A wrong shutdown decision is the one error the derivative comparison cannot
catch.* =teprob.f:807-811= zeroes all fifty derivatives on a trip, so a port
that tripped when the Fortran did not would return zeros that look like a
perfectly valid frozen plant. That is why =ISD= is compared directly on every
state rather than left to Tier 2's derivative check, and why the coverage
matters more here than elsewhere.

*Forcing at =TIME=0= to get a noise-free =XMEAS=, and what it costs.*
=teprob.f:711= guards the noise block with =TIME.GT.0.0=, so time zero skips
the draw entirely. The alternative, subtracting the noise, would mean
reimplementing =TESUB6= and its draw order inside the test for this item, which
is B-0024b's job.

The cost is that =teprob.f:397-406= resets the walk state at time zero, so all
disturbance channels sit at =SZERO=. =tier2/mod.rs= warns about pools that lose
walk coverage silently, so the argument is made explicitly rather than assumed:
this range is a pure conversion layer over quantities the earlier items already
validated across the full walk range, and none of the eight limits reads a
walk-driven quantity. State diversity, which is what this item needs, is
untouched at 2,417.

A test checks the guard directly rather than trusting it, since every number in
the file rests on it: 22 of 22 measurements change once the clock runs, and two
evaluations at time zero agree bit for bit.

*Delta D-006.* =XMEAS(20)= is assigned twice, with 392.7 and then 293.07: a
third apart, both plausible as unit conversions, and the first dead. A port
taking the first line reports compressor work 34% high forever while every
other measurement stays correct.

** Next
B-0025, balance assembly and valve dynamics, =teprob.f:762-816=. The last item
in Phase 2, and the one everything else has been feeding.

*The Class C decision is the part to get right, not the arithmetic.*
=teprob.f:807-811= zeroes all fifty derivatives whenever =ISD= is set. That is
behaviour-defining and benchmark-relevant, so per =CLAUDE.md= it lands *behind
a flag, never as the default*, with a =BLOCKED= item carrying the question and
the measured Tier 5 delta. =derivatives= should return the trip instead, and
B-0024a already gives it a typed cause to return rather than a bare bool.

Do not let the faithful path zero the derivatives by default just because the
oracle does. The Tier 2 differential will then disagree on the 8 tripping
states out of 2,421, and that disagreement is *expected and correct*: compare
the un-zeroed derivative against the oracle only on the 2,413 healthy states,
and assert separately that the port reports a trip on exactly the 8 the Fortran
trips on. B-0024a already proves that second half.

The valve latch is hoisted into the pre-phase (B-0014), and
=crates/tepsim-oracle/tests/hoist_valve_latch.rs= proved it changes no number.
=YP(I+38)= at =805= stays in the pure phase; only =799-804= moved.

After this, B-0026 is Tier 2 acceptance. Note the error trend across the phase:
6.2e-16, 8.5e-16, 5.1e-16, 1.2e-13, 4.0e-13, 8.8e-15. The two large ones are
=sqrt= near clamps and the quartic downstream of it. B-0026 should confirm the
assembled derivative stays inside 1e-12 rather than assume it does.

Append =tier2_balances= to =LIBM_SYSTEM_TESTS= in =xtask/src/main.rs=.

* 2026-08-27 B-0025 The fifty balances, and a tolerance that cannot be met
:PROPERTIES:
:ITEM:     B-0025
:PHASE:    2
:TIER:     2
:COMMIT:   b391c57
:GFORTRAN: 15.2.0
:STATUS:   DONE
:END:
** Preflight
Clean and green, identical to B-0024a's closing numbers. gfortran 15.2.0,
unchanged. Provenance *681/1589 (42.9%)* on entry, *808 (50.8%)* on exit: the
first iteration to pass half the file.

** What changed
=tepsim_core::balances= ports =teprob.f:762-811=, and =Plant::derivatives= now
runs the *whole* right-hand side, from unpacking the state to the fifty
balances. =Plant::advance_discrete= gained the hoisted valve latch.
=constants= gained the nominal initial state. =PlantError::NotImplemented= is
gone.

** Validation numbers
*The whole right-hand side, all three pools.* 2,425 states, 2,412 running and
13 frozen, zero convergence failures.

| configuration | components | cases   | max rel err | max ulp |
|---------------+------------+---------+-------------+---------|
| =libm-system= | all 50     |  35,850 | 0.000e0     |       0 |
| default       | the 22 non-cancelling | 53,064 | 0.000e0 | 0 |
| default       | all 50     | 120,600 | 1.393e-4    | 7.4e11  |
| either        | frozen states | 650  | 0.000e0     |       0 |

*All fifty components are bit-identical to gfortran under =libm-system=.* That
is the correctness result, and it is stronger than any tolerance.

*Per-component relative error in the default build*, the 28 over 1e-12:

: YP(1) 1.7e-7   YP(2) 1.4e-4   YP(3) 1.4e-7   YP(4) 4.2e-8   YP(5) 1.7e-7
: YP(6) 4.5e-7   YP(7) 7.8e-7   YP(8) 4.6e-7   YP(9) 2.6e-8   YP(10) 7.5e-8
: YP(11) 5.5e-7  YP(12) 7.8e-8  YP(13) 6.0e-8  YP(14) 2.6e-6  YP(15) 3.2e-7
: YP(16) 3.6e-7  YP(17) 1.3e-8  YP(18) 3.5e-8  YP(28) 1.1e-7  YP(29) 3.9e-7
: YP(30) 2.5e-6  YP(31) 6.7e-9  YP(32) 5.2e-8  YP(33) 1.1e-7  YP(34) 5.4e-8
: YP(35) 1.1e-8  YP(36) 2.3e-7  YP(38) 1.2e-6

The 22 that are *not* listed are 0 ULP, not merely inside the gate.

*All fifty slots move somewhere in the pool*, so no comparison is zero against
zero. The twelve valve lags are still on the *nominal* trajectory, since =VCV=
equals =VPOS= there; only the perturbed pool exercises them.

** Surprises and decisions
*Tier 2's stated gate is not achievable, and the port is not why.* This is the
finding of the iteration and it is now *B-0026a*, BLOCKED.

=PLAN.org= sets Tier 2 at "rel err < 1e-12". Measured relative to the result,
28 of the 50 fail. Measured under =libm-system=, all 50 are bit-identical. Both
are true, and the reconciliation is cancellation: a balance is inflow minus
outflow, near steady state those nearly agree, and =YP(2)= is a difference of
two flows around 660 whose result is a few parts in ten thousand of either. One
ULP in each term, which is exactly what the vendored =exp= costs, is 1e-16 of
the terms and 1e-4 of the result.

The 22 components that pass are exactly the ones that do not cancel: the nine
stripper balances, whose inlet and outlet are unrelated in magnitude, the
reactor coolant wall, and the twelve valve lags.

Per =CLAUDE.md= the tolerance was *not* touched. The gate applies 1e-12 where
it is a meaningful question and reports the rest, with the excluded components
named individually rather than covered by a widened margin, and the module
documentation carries the numbers and the three candidate answers.

*Two real bugs, both caught within seconds of the differential existing.*

=HST(9)= in the energy balances is the *post-compressor* value.
=teprob.f:601= adds the compressor work after the stream table is built, and
both balances that read stream 9 come after it. Reading the stream table gave
=YP(36)= wrong by a factor of *6e8*. B-0020 documented this trap in detail and
documenting it was not enough; the note now sits at the point of use rather
than in the module that creates the hazard.

=1.8= at =teprob.f:790= and =792= is *single* precision. Every other =1.8= in
the file, at =1396=, =1404=, =1464= and =1471=, is =1.8D0=. Same shape as the
=273.15= against =273.15D0= split, and the same lesson: the original is not
consistent, so the precision has to be read off the line.

*No hand-built fixture is a running plant.* Four were tried and every one
tripped, most on *level low*: the vessels need tens of lbmol of liquid before
=VLR/35.3145= clears 2 cubic metres, and a plausible-looking small state is a
plant that has already emptied itself. A tripped plant returns fifty zeros,
which is exactly the answer these tests exist to distinguish from a bug. So the
nominal state from =TEINIT= went in, all fifty values, reparsed from the Fortran
and checked including the three that carry a =D= suffix. Nothing downstream can
start a simulation without it either.

*The backlog contradicted the plan on the Class C default, and the plan wins.*
B-0025's entry said the shutdown freeze should not be the default. =PLAN.org=
says each Class C entry gets "an explicit sign-off *before it becomes the
default*", of the **fix**, so the faithful freeze is what ships. It is also the
only reading Tier 2 can live with: 13 pool states trip, and a port that did not
freeze would disagree with the oracle on all fifty components for each. The
backlog entry is corrected in place with the reasoning, and D-007 records it.

** Next
*Answer B-0026a.* It is one line and it blocks the close of Phase 2. The
question, the evidence and three candidate answers are in the backlog entry;
the numbers are in this entry and in
=crates/tepsim-oracle/tests/tier2_balances.rs=.

With that answered, B-0026 states the criterion, wires =xtask validate --tiers
2=, and turns on the golden-trace diff in the fidelity preflight, which has
been reporting "0 of 100 steps diffed" since B-0004c and can now be real:
=Plant::euler_step= exists and the nominal state is in the crate.

If the answer is slow in coming, Phase 3 is unblocked and B-0024b is its
natural first item: =teprob.f:711-761= is already split out, and the noise
guard it needs (=TIME.GT.0.0.AND.ISD.EQ.0=) is exactly the =Signals::shutdown=
field B-0025 added.

One loose end worth an early look in Phase 3: =Signals::compositions= is still
zeros. =XCMP= at =teprob.f:717-735= needs nothing the pure phase does not
already have, but it belongs with the analysers that read it, so it went to
B-0024b rather than being half-done here.

* 2026-08-27 B-0027 Decompose Phase 3, and count the draws before porting any of them
:PROPERTIES:
:ITEM:     B-0027
:PHASE:    3
:TIER:     none
:COMMIT:   37fb224
:GFORTRAN: 15.2.0
:STATUS:   DONE
:END:
** Preflight
Clean and green, identical to B-0025's closing numbers. gfortran 15.2.0,
unchanged. Provenance *808/1589 (50.8%)*, unchanged: this item produces backlog
items and a measurement harness, not ported code.

*B-0026 remains BLOCKED on B-0026a*, so this took the next unblocked item per
=CLAUDE.md=. Phase 2's model is complete; only its acceptance criterion is open.

** What changed
Phase 3 decomposed into *B-0028* through *B-0034*, joining *B-0024b* which
B-0024a had already split out. Eight items, each naming its =teprob.f= range.

=crates/tepsim-oracle/tests/rng_call_order.rs= is new: a census of what draws
from the generator, when, and how many times.

** Validation numbers
*Draws per =TEFUNC= evaluation, measured on the nominal trajectory:*

| condition | draws | decomposition |
|-----------+-------+---------------|
| =TIME=0= | 0 | noise skipped, walks reset |
| running, nothing due | 264 | 22 measurements x 12 |
| walk due | 294 | 264 + 30 |
| walk + gas analysers | 462 | 264 + 30 + 168 |
| walk + both analysers | 522 | 264 + 30 + 168 + 60 |
| *tripped* | 258 | 30 + 168 + 60, no noise |

Over 400 nominal states the distribution is =0:1, 264:359, 294:1, 462:39=.

*The walk advance costs 30 draws*, isolated by freezing =TNEXT=: 9 channels
through =TESUB5= at 3 each, plus 1 each from the 3 spike channels.

** Surprises and decisions
*The counts are measured, not read off the listing, and it cost nothing.*
=TESUB7= is a multiplicative congruential generator whose entire state is =G=
(=teprob.f:1551=), so stepping a port-side =TepRng= from the word before a call
until it matches the word after gives the draw count exactly. No edit to the
Fortran, no counter to keep in sync, and the method itself is checked against
the oracle first. Every number above came out of that.

*The walk advance and the gas analyser are permanently in step.* =TNEXT= starts
at 0.1 for all twelve channels (=teprob.f:1362=) and =TGAS= starts at 0.1
(=teprob.f:741=), so the first gas sample and the first walk advance come due on
the same tick and never separate.

The tell was arithmetic: the gas step measures *198* draws, and 198 is not a
multiple of twelve. Fourteen compositions at twelve draws each could only ever
give 168. The residue is the walk's 30. Reading the two line ranges separately
would never have suggested it, and B-0031 would have been debugged against a
number that was wrong by 18%.

*A trip does not silence the generator.* Only =teprob.f:711-716= is guarded by
=ISD=; the three analyser blocks at =744-761= have no guard at all. So a tripped
plant still draws 258, and a port that skipped all noise on a trip would
desynchronise the stream and differ on every subsequent draw, forever, while
still producing a perfectly plausible distribution.

That is a second reason B-0024a's per-state shutdown check mattered: it was
justified there by the derivative freeze, and it turns out to gate the RNG
stream position too.

*The walk's draw count is data-dependent, which is why Tier 3 needs a trace.*
Its 30 draws are 27 + 3, and the 3 are conditional: =teprob.f:380-393= branches
on the interpolated value against 0.1, and a spike channel above that threshold
draws *nothing*. So the number of draws in an evaluation depends on the walk's
own value, which depends on earlier draws. A port cannot get the stream right by
counting; it has to match the sequence.

*Incremental verification is only partly available in this phase.* Phase 2 was
verifiable item by item because =COMMON/TEPROC/= exposes every intermediate.
The walk *state* is in =COMMON/WLK/= and is readable the same way, so values
stay checkable per item. Draw *order* is not a value in any =COMMON= block and
cannot be recovered after the fact. That is why B-0029 builds the trace
differ *before* B-0031 ports the advance, rather than after, which is the
opposite of the order the phase description implies.

** Next
B-0028: port =TESUB5= and =TESUB6=, =teprob.f:1506-1546=, and finish Tier 1.

Both *consume the generator*, unlike =TESUB1= through =TESUB4=, so Tier 1 for
them is not an input sweep: pin =G=, call, compare the value *and the number of
draws*. The counts this iteration measured are what the later items depend on,
so assert them at the source: 3 for =TESUB5= (=teprob.f:1528-1530=), 12 for
=TESUB6= (=1542-1543=).

The oracle has no =tesub5_= binding. It takes fourteen arguments, eight of them
in/out, so the shim is more work than the other seven put together; budget for
it.

=TESUB6='s twelve-uniform sum is the one place the port's arithmetic could
differ without a transcendental being involved: =X=X+TESUB7(I)= accumulates in
call order and =(X-6.D0)*STD= scales afterwards. Reassociating that sum is the
same class of mistake B-0021's =UTVR= was, and the =libm-system= run will not
help here because no transcendental is involved. It should be bit-exact under
*both* configurations.

*B-0026a is still the one to answer*, and it is one line. Phase 2's model is
complete and proved bit-exact; only its acceptance criterion is open.

* 2026-08-27 B-0028 TESUB5 and TESUB6, and a bug that changes no value at all
:PROPERTIES:
:ITEM:     B-0028
:PHASE:    3
:TIER:     1
:COMMIT:   6fa4001
:GFORTRAN: 15.2.0
:STATUS:   DONE
:END:
** Preflight
Clean and green, identical to B-0027's closing numbers. gfortran 15.2.0,
unchanged. Provenance *808/1589 (50.8%)* on entry, *849 (53.4%)* on exit.
B-0026 still BLOCKED on B-0026a; this is the next unblocked item.

** What changed
=tepsim_core::disturbance= ports =teprob.f:1506-1546=. The oracle gained a
=tesub5_= binding, which it lacked: fourteen arguments, six written back.
*Tier 1 is now complete* -- all eight =TESUBn= routines are ported and
differentially tested.

** Validation numbers
*Tier 1, 20,000 pinned generator words each.*

| field | cases  | max rel err | max ulp |
|-------+--------+-------------+---------|
| =TESUB6= sample | 20,000 | 0.000e0 | 0 |
| =TESUB5= =ADIST= | 20,000 | 0.000e0 | 0 |
| =TESUB5= =BDIST= | 20,000 | 0.000e0 | 0 |
| =TESUB5= =CDIST= | 20,000 | 0.000e0 | 0 |
| =TESUB5= =DDIST= | 20,000 | 0.000e0 | 0 |
| =TESUB5= =TNEXT= | 20,000 | 0.000e0 | 0 |

Zero non-finite cases. Neither routine touches a transcendental, so bit
equality is the only acceptable answer and the =libm-system= run offers no
cover: both are exact in *both* configurations.

*Draw counts*, recovered by stepping a port-side generator from the word before
each call to the word after: *3* for =TESUB5=, *12* for =TESUB6=, on every case
and for both flag values.

** Surprises and decisions
*The bug worth finding here changes no value at all.* =IDVWLK= multiplies the
two *endpoint* draws at =teprob.f:1529-1530= but not the *duration* draw at
=1528=. So an inactive channel still consumes all three.

The obvious optimisation is to skip the two endpoint draws when the flag is
zero, since they are multiplied by zero anyway. Doing that produces *identical
segment values*, because an inactive channel lands on =SZERO= with zero slope
either way, and leaves the generator two steps behind. Every subsequent draw in
the run then differs: the noise, the other channels, everything.

It was implemented deliberately to check the test has teeth, and the Tier 1
test caught it, on the *stream position* rather than on any value. A test that
compared only outputs would have passed. That is the entire argument for Tier 3
demonstrated at Tier 1, and it is why the ending generator word is compared on
every case rather than just the draw count: a port can make the right number of
draws in the wrong order.

*A claim of mine was wrong and the measurement corrected it.* The module first
said =TESUB6='s twelve-term sum was order-sensitive, like every balance in
=crate::balances=. It is not. Over 500,000 consecutive samples from the real
stream, summing the twelve draws forwards and backwards gives *bit-identical*
results every time.

The reason is conditioning: all twelve addends lie in [0, 1) and the running
total never exceeds 12, so every addition is between operands within a factor of
about twelve and nothing is lost to shift out. The loop is still written in call
order, because that is what the listing says, but the module now distinguishes
what is load-bearing (the draw order) from what is not (the arithmetic). A test
asserts the conditioning premise directly, so a change to =unit()='s range fails
there rather than somewhere subtler.

*Half the sweep was vacuous on the first run.* =oracle.wlk()= reads
=COMMON/WLK/=, which is all zeros until =TEINIT= runs, so the "real channel"
half of the sweep had zero spans, =H= = 0, and non-finite cubic coefficients on
both sides: 10,000 cases agreeing perfectly and proving nothing. The comparison
harness reported it as =non-finite: 10000 seen, 0 mismatched=, which is exactly
the line that made it visible. Both sweeps now call =oracle.init()= first and
assert the spans are populated.

** Next
B-0029: the Tier 3 harness, RNG call-trace instrumentation on both sides and
the differ.

*Build it before B-0031 ports the walk advance, not after.* B-0027 established
why: the walk state is readable from =COMMON/WLK/=, so values stay checkable per
item as Phase 2's were, but draw *order* is not a value in any =COMMON= block
and cannot be recovered after the fact.

The instrumentation goes in =crates/tepsim-oracle/instrument.rs=, which already
hoists =ISD= and asserts its own pre-image. Adding a counter to =TESUB7= is a
smaller edit than that one. Record =(index, sign flag, result)= per draw; the
sign flag matters because =TESUB7= returns different scalings for it and a port
could call the wrong one with the right count.

On the port side it belongs behind a feature, so the shipped core carries no
counter. =TepRng= is =Copy= and holds one =f64=; a trace cannot live in it
without changing that, so the recorder should wrap rather than extend it.

One thing B-0028 makes cheap: the draw-count method needs no instrumentation at
all, and it already works. If the traced differ turns out to be more work than
expected, counting per phase gets most of the value, and
=tests/rng_call_order.rs= is the working example.

*B-0026a is still open* and still one line. It is the only thing between Phase 2
and closed.

* 2026-08-27 B-0029 The Tier 3 trace, built before the thing it will measure
:PROPERTIES:
:ITEM:     B-0029
:PHASE:    3
:TIER:     3
:COMMIT:   7a7bbdc
:GFORTRAN: 15.2.0
:STATUS:   DONE
:END:
** Preflight
Clean and green, identical to B-0028's closing numbers. gfortran 15.2.0,
unchanged. Provenance *849/1589 (53.4%)*, unchanged: this item builds a
measuring instrument, not ported model code.

** What changed
=instrument.rs= gained one edit: =TESUB7= now records every draw into a new
=COMMON/RNGTRC/=. =tepsim_oracle::tier3= reads it and diffs two traces.
=tepsim_core::disturbance= gained =Draw= and =TracingRng=.

** Validation numbers
*The instrument checked against what it claims to explain.* Replaying a trace's
draws from the generator word before an evaluation must land exactly on the word
after:

: 113,088 draws traced and replayed across 400 evaluations, all exact.

*Trace lengths against B-0027's uninstrumented census*, which measured the same
quantity by a completely different method:

| time | draws | what |
|------+-------+------|
| 0 | 0 | noise skipped, walks reset |
| 1e-6 | 264 | noise only |
| 0.15 | 462 | noise, walk advance, gas analysers |
| 0.30 | 522 | and the product analyser |

Exact agreement at every point.

*Scalings in one real evaluation*: 30 signed, 432 unit. The 30 is
9 x 3 + 3, the walk advance; the 432 is 36 compositions at twelve each.

*Capacity headroom*: worst evaluation 462 draws against a capacity of 4096.

** Surprises and decisions
*One edit covers the whole stream.* Every stochastic quantity in =teprob.f=
comes through =TESUB7=, so instrumenting that one function records everything:
the walk segments, the spike dwells, the measurement noise and all three
analysers. The edit is appended *after* both assignments to the function result,
so nothing it does can reach the returned value, and the pristine-probe test
that already exists checks that rather than leaving it as an argument.

*The trace is per-step, and that is not an optimisation.* A 48-hour run makes
tens of millions of draws. Clearing before each evaluation and comparing one
step at a time reports the first divergence *at the step it happened*, which is
what a person debugging needs; a run-length trace would report an index into
something enormous. It also bounds the buffer at 4096 against a worst case of
462.

*The instrument is tested against the generator word, which is the strongest
check available.* A trace that dropped a draw, recorded one twice, or reordered
any of them would fail to land on the word the evaluation actually left behind.
That single property covers completeness, ordering and fidelity at once, and it
holds over 113,088 draws.

*Two independent methods now agree on the counts.* B-0027 measured them with no
instrumentation at all, by stepping a port-side generator from the word before
to the word after. The trace lengths match that census exactly. Either method
alone would be a claim; the two agreeing is evidence.

*The port side is a separate type, not a feature.* The item said to put
recording behind a cargo feature. =TracingRng= wraps =TepRng= instead, so the
shipped plant carries no buffer *by construction* rather than by configuration,
and there is no feature combination in which the wrong one ships. =TepRng= also
stays =Copy= and one =f64= wide, which it could not if a trace lived inside it.

*The differ reports a kind, not just an index.* Four things can go wrong and
they have different causes: a wrong value means the generator is already
displaced and the real fault is earlier; a wrong scaling means =TESUB7= was
called with the wrong sign flag; too few or too many draws means a consumer was
skipped or added. B-0028's bug was the "too few" case, and it produced
*identical values*, so a differ that only reported values would have said
nothing useful.

** Next
B-0030: the walk spans and the noise magnitudes, =teprob.f:1256-1367=.

Constants only, 149 of them: =XNS(1..41)=, five span arrays of twelve, and the
initial walk state. No behaviour, so it is the =tests/constants.rs= and
=tests/nominal_state.rs= treatment: reparse the Fortran, derive each value
independently, and assert against =COMMON/WLK/= from a =TEINIT='d oracle.
