One thing to check rather than assume: B-0028's Tier 1 sweep read these spans
and found them *all =D=-suffixed* in the range it sampled, which is unusual for
this file. Confirm it across all 149 rather than inferring it from twelve; the
=273.15= and =1.8= findings both came from the original being inconsistent
about exactly this.

B-0030 also removes a wart. =tier1_disturbance.rs= currently reads =XNS= and
the spans out of the oracle to avoid duplicating them before they are
transcribed; once they exist in the core, that test should read them from there
and the comment saying so can go.

*B-0026a is still open.* Phase 2's model is complete and proved bit-exact; only
its acceptance criterion waits.

* 2026-08-27 B-0030 The walk spans and noise magnitudes, checked twice
:PROPERTIES:
:ITEM:     B-0030
:PHASE:    3
:TIER:     2
:COMMIT:   e14cba3
:GFORTRAN: 15.2.0
:STATUS:   DONE
:END:
** Preflight
Clean and green, identical to B-0029's closing numbers. gfortran 15.2.0,
unchanged. Provenance *849/1589 (53.4%)* on entry, *950 (59.8%)* on exit.

** What changed
=constants::MEASUREMENT_NOISE= and =disturbance::CHANNEL_SPANS= transcribe
=teprob.f:1256-1359=: 41 noise magnitudes and five span arrays of twelve.
=tier1_disturbance.rs= now reads both from the port rather than out of the
oracle, which it was doing only because they did not exist.

** Validation numbers
*101 constants, verified two independent ways.*

| check | what it does |
|-------+--------------|
| =tepsim-core/tests/walk_constants.rs= | reparses the Fortran, derives each value from the literal and its suffix |
| =tepsim-oracle/tests/walk_constants_vs_oracle.rs= | compares against =COMMON/WLK/= and =COMMON/TEPROC/= as gfortran filled them |

Both exact on all 101. The second is the decisive one, because it does not
share the first's reading of Fortran literal semantics: if both readings were
wrong in the same way, only the oracle would notice.

*All 101 carry a =D= suffix*, asserted one by one. This is the only uniformly
double-precision block in the file.

*The initial walk state* at =teprob.f:1360-1367= is pinned against the oracle:
every channel starts at its own =SZERO=, flat, with =TNEXT= at 0.1, and
=IDVWLK= all zero.

** Surprises and decisions
*Checking the precision one by one was not paranoia.* The block turns out to be
uniformly double, which is unusual here and would have been safe to assume. But
the =273.15= against =273.15D0= split and the =1.8= at =teprob.f:790= both came
from blocks that looked uniform and were not, and each cost a measurement to
find. The assertion is three lines and it removes the question permanently.

*Three properties of the table matter more than the numbers, and are now tests.*

=SPSPAN= is zero for *every* channel. No walk in this model ends a segment with
a nonzero slope, so =teprob.f:1530= always computes zero and each segment is a
cubic through two points with flat ends. That is why B-0028's draw-skipping bug
was so invisible: the third draw affects the *stream* and never the *answer*.

Channels 10 to 12 have no value or slope span at all. They are not walks: the
spike rule at =teprob.f:372-396= reads only their durations, so =SSPAN= and
=SZERO= are dead storage for them, in the same way =XMWS='s seven unused slots
were in B-0020.

Every duration centre clears its span, so a draw of -1 cannot produce a
zero-length segment. =H= divides the cubic coefficients at
=teprob.f:1533-1534= and the original has no guard; it is the *table* that
keeps it safe, not the code. B-0028's Tier 1 sweep had to construct its random
spans with the same property for the same reason, and now the constraint is
written down where it is enforced.

*A fresh plant has every channel flat at its centre.* =IDVWLK= starts all-zero,
so no disturbance is active and every walk is a constant. That is what makes a
=d00= run a nominal run rather than a run with quiet noise on it, and it is
asserted rather than assumed.

** Next
B-0031: the walk advance, =teprob.f:340-406=. The first Phase 3 item with
behaviour rather than constants, and the one everything so far has been built
for.

Four pieces: the =IDV= clamp at =340-346=, the =IDVWLK= mapping at =347-358=,
the =DO 900= advance for channels 1-9 through =TESUB5=, and the =DO 910= spike
rule for 10-12. Then the =TIME=0= reset at =397-406=.

*The spike rule is where the work is.* =teprob.f:380-393= branches on the
interpolated value against 0.1: above it, the channel is given a 0.1-hour pulse
built from the current value and slope; at or below, it is given a flat dwell
whose curvature is =IDVWLK(I)/HWLK**2= and whose length is a fresh draw. Both
branches must be exercised, and B-0027 measured that all three spike channels
take the *dwell* branch at the nominal operating point, so the pulse branch
needs a state that reaches it.

Everything needed to verify it exists now. Tier 3 traces the draws, B-0030
pinned the initial state and the spans, and =COMMON/WLK/= exposes the walk
state after every call so the segments themselves are directly comparable.
Compare both: the state *and* the trace. B-0028 showed they are different
claims.

One thing to read carefully rather than infer: =teprob.f:387= borrows =ISD= as
the =TESUB7= sign flag, and the oracle's instrumentation already splits that
into =IRAND=. The split is behaviour-preserving, and the pristine probe proves
it, but the port should use a plainly-named local and not reproduce the borrow.

*B-0026a is still open*, and still one line.

* 2026-08-27 B-0031 The walk advance, compared as state and as stream
:PROPERTIES:
:ITEM:     B-0031
:PHASE:    3
:TIER:     3
:COMMIT:   95b8d57
:GFORTRAN: 15.2.0
:STATUS:   DONE
:END:
** Preflight
Clean and green, identical to B-0030's closing numbers. gfortran 15.2.0,
unchanged. Provenance *950/1589 (59.8%)* on entry, *1043 (65.6%)* on exit.

** What changed
=tepsim_core::walk= ports =teprob.f:340-406=. =disturbance= gained a =Draws=
trait, and =walk_segment=, =noise= and =advance= are generic over it.

** Validation numbers
*Every comparison checks two things*: the walk state, bit for bit against
=COMMON/WLK/= (four cubic coefficients and both times, per channel), and the
draw trace, in order and with the same scaling.

| sweep | coverage |
|-------+----------|
| nominal trajectory | 400 evaluations: *40* advanced a channel, 360 did not |
| each walk disturbance | 10 faults x 600 steps x 0.05 h = 30 simulated hours each |
| spike coverage | =IDV(17)=: *66 fired* segments, *1,432 dwells* over 75 hours |
| =TIME=0= reset | 30 draws made and discarded, matching |

All exact, in state and in trace.

*Draws per fault over 30 hours*, which differ and should:

: IDV(8,9,10,11,12,13,16): 1572 each
: IDV(17): 1563   IDV(18): 1543   IDV(20): 1588

The seven walk faults are identical because their channels re-segment on
duration draws alone. The three spike faults differ because their draw count is
*data-dependent*: a firing segment costs no draw and a dwell costs one. That is
B-0027's prediction showing up in practice, and it is the concrete reason Tier
3 needs a trace rather than a counter.

** Surprises and decisions
*Channels 10 to 12 are not walks, and the shared array names hide it.* They
alternate between a flat dwell whose curvature is =IDVWLK/h^2= and a 0.1-hour
cubic pulse. With the disturbance off the curvature is zero, so the parabola
never reaches the 0.1 threshold and the channel dwells forever, drawing a fresh
interval each time. With it on, the channel climbs, fires briefly, and falls
back.

So =IDV(17)=, =IDV(18)= and =IDV(20)= are *intermittent* faults rather than
sustained drifts, which is why the literature reports them as the hardest of the
twenty to detect. 66 fires against 1,432 dwells is what that looks like.

*=teprob.f:388= draws directly rather than through =TESUB5=.* It is the same
expression as =TESUB5='s first line, and it is one draw rather than three. That
is where B-0027's =9 * 3 + 3 = 30= comes from, and it only became obvious with
both routines side by side.

*The handover is at =TNEXT=, not at the current time.* =teprob.f:361-366=
evaluates the outgoing segment at its own scheduled end. Using =t= instead is
the natural implementation and it is wrong: it lets a step arriving late shift
the whole chain forward. Tried deliberately, it shows up as
=TNEXT(1) = 0.46658434048295055= against =...017=, at the fourteenth digit,
which no tolerance would catch and the bit comparison does.

*Model code is generic over the generator, not duplicated.* Tracing needed
=advance= to accept a recording generator as well as a plain one. Making that a
second code path would mean Tier 3 validated a program that never ships, and
the one thing Tier 3 exists to catch is a divergence between what is drawn and
what is believed to be drawn. A =Draws= trait costs nothing at runtime and
keeps one path.

*The =TIME=0= reset runs after the advance, not instead of it.* =teprob.f:397=
comes after both loops, so a t=0 evaluation with channels already due *draws*
and then throws the result away. Returning early is the obvious implementation
and produces the right state with the generator two dozen draws behind. Same
shape as B-0028's bug, and there is now a test for it on both sides.

** Next
B-0032: wire the walk outputs into =Plant::advance_discrete=,
=teprob.f:407-416=.

Mostly deletion. Six Tier 2 test files each reimplement that block to supply
the port's inputs, and each comes out 0 ULP, so the reading is already known to
be right. This item moves it into the plant and removes the six copies.

The pieces all exist now: =walk::advance= produces the channel state,
=Channel::at= is =TESUB8=, and =WalkInputs= is the struct the pure phase
already reads. What is missing is only that =Plant= holds no generator and no
=Walks= yet.

Watch one thing. =Plant::advance_discrete= currently takes =&mut self= and does
the valve latch; adding the walk advance makes it the *only* place the
generator moves in a step, which is what the three-phase split promised. Assert
it: an RK4 driver calling =derivatives= four times must still advance the walks
once, and =assert_derivatives_are_pure= should keep passing with a generator in
the plant.

*B-0026a is still open.*

* 2026-08-27 B-0032 Wire the walks into the plant, and find what six copies agreed on
:PROPERTIES:
:ITEM:     B-0032
:PHASE:    3
:TIER:     2
:COMMIT:   d35ef97
:GFORTRAN: 15.2.0
:STATUS:   DONE
:END:
** Preflight
Clean and green, identical to B-0031's closing numbers. gfortran 15.2.0,
unchanged. Provenance *1043/1589 (65.6%)* on entry, *1050 (66.1%)* on exit.

** What changed
=Plant= now holds the generator and the twelve channels, and
=Plant::advance_discrete= ports =teprob.f:340-416= as well as the valve latch
it already had. Six Tier 2 harnesses gained a term they were missing.

** Validation numbers
*All twenty disturbances*, one at a time, over 10 simulated hours each (200
steps of 0.05 h), against the oracle. Compared per step: =XST(1..3,4)=,
=TST(1)=, =TST(4)=, =TCWR=, =TCWS=, all twelve channels' four coefficients and
both times, and all twelve latched valve commands. All exact.

*The nominal trajectory*: 400 evaluations, same comparison, all exact.

*The two composition faults, measured:*

: nominal  A 0.485        B 0.005   C 0.51
: IDV(1)   A 0.45499999   B 0.005   C 0.54
: IDV(2)   A 0.48256281   B 0.01    C 0.50743719

So =IDV(1)= moves A by 0.03 and leaves B alone; =IDV(2)= moves A by 2.43719e-3
*and* B by 0.005, and C absorbs both.

** Surprises and decisions
*Six harnesses agreed with each other and all six were wrong.*
=teprob.f:407-408= subtracts two terms from the mixed feed's A fraction, on two
source lines:

: XST(1,4)=TESUB8(1,TIME)-IDV(1)*0.03D0
: .-IDV(2)*2.43719D-3

Every Tier 2 file from B-0020 onward reimplemented this block to supply the
port's inputs, and every one carried the first term and dropped the second. Six
files, all 0 ULP, which reads as six independent confirmations of the
reading. It was *one reading copied six times*.

It never showed because =Pools::collect= starts from =TEINIT=, where every
=IDV= is zero, and neither the perturbed nor the adversarial pool switches one
on. The term was multiplied by zero in roughly 2,400 states per file, about
14,000 comparisons in total, every one of them exact and none of them evidence.

*The lesson generalises past this term.* A comparison can be exact over
thousands of states and say nothing whatever about a branch none of them enter.
B-0021, B-0022 and B-0024a each found the same shape in the adversarial
catalogue, where boundary states sat on thresholds and never crossed them. This
is the same failure one level up: the *pool* had a blind spot, not the
catalogue. Sweeping with disturbances active was the missing move, and nothing
before this item did it.

And copying a harness copies its blind spots. Six files looked like redundancy
and were a single point of failure.

*The six copies are fixed, not deleted, which is a change from the item as
written.* B-0032's acceptance said to delete them and have each Tier 2 file get
its inputs from =Plant::advance_discrete=. That is worse: a bug in
=advance_discrete= would then feed *both sides* of every Tier 2 comparison, and
they would all pass on wrong-against-wrong. The whole value of those files is
that they reach the oracle by a path the port does not control.

So they keep their own reading, now correct, and the plant's version is checked
separately in =tier3_walk_inputs.rs= against the oracle with every fault on.
Two independent readings that must agree is the arrangement B-0030 used for the
constants, for the same reason.

*The generator moves once per step, and it is now checkable.* With the
generator living in =Plant= rather than in a test harness, the promise the
three-phase split has been making since B-0014 can be asserted directly: four
=derivatives= calls between two =advance_discrete= calls leave the word
untouched. That is what lets an RK4 driver exist.

** Next
B-0024b: measurement noise and the sampled analysers, =teprob.f:711-761=. The
last piece of the model, and the one B-0024a split out because it needed
exactly what Phase 3 has now built.

Everything it depends on exists. =disturbance::noise= is =TESUB6=,
=constants::MEASUREMENT_NOISE= is =XNS=, =Signals::shutdown= carries the =ISD=
the noise guard reads, and Tier 3 can compare the draws.

Three things to get right, all of them about *when* rather than what.
=teprob.f:711= skips the noise entirely at =TIME=0= or on a tripped plant,
which B-0027 measured: a tripped evaluation still draws 258 times for the
analysers, so a port that silenced everything on a trip would desynchronise the
stream. The analysers latch the *previous* sample and then store the current
one (=teprob.f:746-749=), which is what makes the dead time; reading them in
the other order gives a plant with no dead time and plausible numbers. And
=TGAS= and =TPROD= advance by 0.1 and 0.25 *from their previous value*, not
from the current time, so a step that arrives late does not shift the schedule,
exactly as the walk handover does not.

=XCMP= at =teprob.f:717-735= is still zeros in =Signals::compositions=; it is
pure and could have landed in Phase 2, but it belongs with the analysers that
read it.

*B-0026a remains open*, and Phase 2 stays open with it.

* 2026-08-27 B-0024b Noise and the analysers, and the model runs end to end
:PROPERTIES:
:ITEM:     B-0024b
:PHASE:    3
:TIER:     3
:COMMIT:   c692fa6
:GFORTRAN: 15.2.0
:STATUS:   DONE
:END:
** Preflight
Clean and green, identical to B-0032's closing numbers. gfortran 15.2.0,
unchanged. Provenance *1050/1589 (66.1%)* on entry, *1101 (69.3%)* on exit.

** What changed
=tepsim_core::analysers= ports =teprob.f:711-761=, and
=Plant::sample_measurements= is no longer a stub. With
=advance_discrete= and =derivatives= that is all of =teprob.f:340-816=:
*the port is a complete simulator*.

=Scenario= gained a =measurements= field, and =Scenario::force= restores it.

** Validation numbers
*A whole step, all three phases, against a whole =TEFUNC= call.*

| sweep | result |
|-------+--------|
| 400 nominal steps | worst measurement *8.008e-15*, 113,088 draws |
| all 20 disturbances, 6 h each | *0 ULP* on every measurement |
| 25 adversarial boundaries | tripped *258* draws, healthy *522* |
| *2,000 consecutive carried steps* | generators in lockstep throughout |

The last row is the one that matters. Every other test in the project restores
the port from =COMMON= before each comparison; this one lets both sides carry
their own state for 2,000 steps and compares as it goes.

** Surprises and decisions
*An analyser has two stored values, and I carried one.* =XDEL(i)= holds the
composition it will report next time, noise-free. =XMEAS(i)= holds what it
reported *last* time, noise included, and =teprob.f:744= writes it only inside
the schedule check, so between samples the previous reported value simply
persists.

They differ by exactly one noise draw. Carrying only =XDEL= and reporting it
between samples gives the right composition with the noise stripped off: a
reading that is plausible, within a fraction of a percent of correct, and
wrong. Nothing caught it until step *362* of the 2,000-step carried run, which
is the only test in the project that could.

*That exposed a gap in the Tier 2 harness.* =Scenario='s documentation says it
carries everything =TEFUNC= reads, and =force= restores =COMMON/TEPROC/=,
=COMMON/WLK/=, the generator, =XMV= and =IDV=. It did not restore =XMEAS=,
because =XMEAS= is in =COMMON/PV/= and the enumeration was of =TEPROC=.

It never mattered: the held readings do not reach the derivative, so every Tier
2 number stands. It matters the moment a whole step is compared. B-0015 chose
to restore the whole =TEPROC= block rather than a chosen subset, on the grounds
that "enumerating which fields carry over is exactly the kind of judgement that
was wrong the first time". The judgement was wrong again, one =COMMON= block
over.

*=TGAS=0.1= is single precision.* =teprob.f:741= and =751= both write =0.1=
with no =D= suffix, so the gas schedule is 0.10000000149011612 and a step
landing on exactly 0.1 does *not* sample. =TPROD=0.25= is exactly
representable, so the product analyser is unaffected. Two schedules on adjacent
lines, one of which has the quirk. Found by a unit test that used a plain
=0.1_f64= and failed.

*The trip guard, confirmed against the oracle.* A tripped evaluation draws 258
times and a healthy one 522, the difference being exactly the 264-draw
continuous noise block. The analysers at =744-761= have no =ISD= guard and keep
drawing. B-0027 predicted this from the source and it is now measured through
the port.

** Next
B-0033: the twenty disturbances as =InjectionPoint= plus =Profile= pairs.

A refactor with a differential behind it, not new behaviour: the faithful path
must stay bit-identical, and =tier3_analysers.rs= already sweeps all twenty
against the oracle, so the check exists before the change.

Three kinds are now visible from the work rather than from the plan, and the
types should follow them. Four are *step* faults on a feed composition or
temperature (=IDV(1)= through =IDV(5)=), acting at =teprob.f:407-414=. Ten are
*walk magnitude* flags, entering through =IDVWLK= at =347-358=, of which three
drive spike trains rather than walks. Six are neither: =IDV(6)= and =IDV(7)=
gate feed flows directly at =teprob.f:567-568=, and =IDV(14)=, =IDV(15)= and
=IDV(19)= are valve-*sticking* flags that never touch the model at all, only
the latch at =793-798=.

That last group is worth a type of its own. A valve-sticking flag is not a
disturbance to the plant; it is a disturbance to the *controller's authority*
over it, which is why it produces nothing at all in an open-loop run.

After B-0033, B-0034 closes Phase 3.

*B-0026a is still open* and still one line. It is now the only thing standing
between a complete, end-to-end-validated port and a closed Phase 2.

* 2026-08-27 B-0026 Tier 2 acceptance, and the preflight goes live
:PROPERTIES:
:ITEM:     B-0026
:PHASE:    2
:TIER:     2
:COMMIT:   831c60f
:GFORTRAN: 15.2.0
:STATUS:   DONE
:END:
** Preflight
Clean and green. gfortran 15.2.0, unchanged. Provenance *1101/1589 (69.3%)*,
unchanged: this item implements a metric and wires two commands.

B-0026a was answered by the user: *measure against the scale of the terms*.
=PLAN.org= updated, decision entry recorded, blocked list empty.

** What changed
=Balances= now carries a =scale=, the magnitude of the largest term entering
each balance. =Comparison::observe_against= divides by a supplied scale rather
than by the reference. =xtask validate --tiers 2= exists. And =xtask fidelity=
diffs the port against the golden trace, which it has not done since B-0004c
created it.

** Validation numbers
*Tier 2 acceptance. All fifty components, 2,412 running states, all three
pools.*

: worst 6.093e-14 at YP(7), perturbed#300, against a 1e-12 gate.

Per-component, both measures, worst over the sweep:

| YP | err/scale | err/value | ratio |
|----+-----------+-----------+-------|
|  2 | 3.811e-14 | 1.393e-4 | 3.7e9 |
| 30 | 4.552e-15 | 2.487e-6 | 5.5e8 |
| 38 | 8.696e-15 | 1.187e-6 | 1.4e8 |
| 14 | 3.113e-14 | 2.618e-6 | 8.4e7 |
|  7 | 6.093e-14 | 7.835e-7 | 1.3e7 |
| 19-27, 37, 39-50 | 0.000e0 | 0.000e0 | 1x |

*28 of 50 components cancel by more than 100x.* The 22 that do not are exactly
0.000e0: bit-identical, not merely inside the gate.

*The fidelity preflight, now real:*

: 100 of 100 steps diffed, worst 3.521e-14 at YP(12) step 77, gate 1e-12.

It compares states, derivatives, measurements *and* the generator word, needs
no gfortran, and takes about a second.

** Surprises and decisions
*The scale belongs in the model, not in the harness.* Only =balances()= knows
what the terms of a balance are. Computing the budget anywhere else would mean
a second implementation of all fifty equations, which is exactly the thing
B-0016 refused to do for the adversarial states and for the same reason: a
wrong second implementation looks like coverage.

*The acceptance table asserts the contrast, not just the numbers.* The decision
of 2026-08-27 rests on the claim that these balances cancel badly. If the model
ever stopped cancelling, relative-to-result would be the better metric and the
decision would be stale. So the table counts how many components cancel by more
than 100x and fails if it drops below twenty. The reason for a decision should
expire loudly, not quietly.

*The preflight earned its keep on its first run.* The first version hardcoded
=XMV= from the listing, and transposed two digits of =XMV(1)=: 63.053630
against 63.052630. The preflight caught it at *step 79*, as a 1.6e-5 divergence
in =XMEAS(2)=, and nothing else in the project would have. Every Tier 2 and
Tier 3 test forces =XMV= from a scenario, so none of them touches a
hand-written value.

=TEINIT= sets =XMV(I) = YY(I+38)= at =teprob.f:1104=, so the values now come
from =NOMINAL_STATE=. =CLAUDE.md= already says constants are transcribed and
never retyped; this was a fresh instance of exactly that, eight iterations
after B-0019 found the same thing in the pre-exponentials.

*Eight iterations of "0 of 100 steps diffed".* The preflight has printed that
line every session since B-0004c, and it was honest each time. It is worth
noting how long a placeholder can sit in a checklist while the checklist keeps
passing: the line was read, and understood, and skipped over, on every one of
those runs.

** Next
B-0033: the twenty disturbances as =InjectionPoint= plus =Profile= pairs, then
B-0034 closes Phase 3.

B-0033 is a refactor with a differential already behind it:
=tier3_walk_inputs.rs= and =tier3_analysers.rs= both sweep all twenty faults
against the oracle, so the check exists before the change. The faithful path
must stay bit-identical.

Three kinds are visible from the work rather than from the plan, and the types
should follow them: four *step* faults on a feed composition or temperature
(=IDV(1)= to =IDV(5)=, at =teprob.f:407-414=); ten *walk magnitude* flags
entering through =IDVWLK= at =347-358=, three of which drive spike trains
rather than walks; and six that are neither. =IDV(6)= and =IDV(7)= gate feed
flows directly at =567-568=, and =IDV(14)=, =IDV(15)= and =IDV(19)= are valve
*sticking* flags that never touch the model at all, only the latch at
=793-798=.

That last group deserves its own type. A valve-sticking flag is not a
disturbance to the plant; it is a disturbance to the controller's authority
over it, which is why it produces nothing whatever in an open-loop run. A
scenario engine that treated it as a plant disturbance would report a fault
that does nothing and look broken.

* 2026-08-27 B-0033 The twenty disturbances as what they actually are
:PROPERTIES:
:ITEM:     B-0033
:PHASE:    3
:TIER:     none
:COMMIT:   18f8558
:GFORTRAN: 15.2.0
:STATUS:   DONE
:END:
** Preflight
Clean and green. gfortran 15.2.0, unchanged. =xtask fidelity= now diffs for
real: *100 of 100 steps, worst 3.521e-14*. Provenance *1101/1589 (69.3%)* on
entry, *1123 (70.7%)* on exit.

** What changed
=tepsim_core::fault= describes all twenty disturbances: the published name
verbatim, what the source actually does, which shape it has, and the
=teprob.f= line it acts on. =tests/fault_table.rs= checks every claim against
the Fortran.

** Validation numbers
*Every claim, against the oracle.* For all twenty faults: the channels the
table claims are exactly the channels =IDVWLK= enables, and the valves it
claims are exactly the valves =IVST= sticks.

*The sticking faults change nothing.* =IDV(14)=, =IDV(15)= and =IDV(19)= leave
all fifty derivatives *bit-identical* in an open-loop run. Every other fault
moves something within 20 simulated hours, so the distinction is tested in both
directions.

*Step against random, by observation.* At t=0.05, before any channel is due,
all seven step faults have already moved the derivative and none of the ten
random ones has.

** Surprises and decisions
*The five "Unknown" faults are not unknown, and they are not one kind.* The
header at =teprob.f:187-191= gives no description for =IDV(16)= through
=IDV(20)=, and the literature repeats it. The *source* is entirely explicit;
only the physical interpretation was withheld:

| fault | what =teprob.f= does |
|-------+----------------------|
| =IDV(16)= | walk channel 9, stripper steam valve capacity |
| =IDV(17)= | spike channel 10, reactor coolant duty |
| =IDV(18)= | spike channel 11, condenser coolant duty |
| =IDV(19)= | sticks valves 5, 7, 8 and 9 |
| =IDV(20)= | spike channel 12, reactor outlet flow |

So one of the five does not reach the plant at all, and three of the remaining
four are *spike* channels rather than walks. That is why the literature reports
17, 18 and 20 as the hardest of the twenty to detect: they are intermittent, and
B-0031 measured the shape (66 fired segments against 1,432 dwells over 75 hours).

*A sticking fault is not a plant disturbance.* It is a disturbance to the
controller's *authority over* the plant: =IVST= widens the dead band a command
must cross before the valve follows it (=teprob.f:801=). With the command held
still, as in every open-loop run, it does nothing whatever.

That is worth a type rather than a comment. A scenario engine that treated all
twenty alike would inject =IDV(14)=, observe no effect, and look broken; or
worse, someone would "fix" it. The three are marked
=Fault::affects_the_plant() == false= and the fact is measured, not asserted.

*This item added no code path.* It describes what B-0031 and B-0032 already
implement, so the faithful path is untouched and their differentials still hold
without modification. The value is entirely in the table being *checkable*: two
statements of the same twenty facts, one in prose next to the code and one
read out of =COMMON= after forcing each fault, which must agree.

** Next
B-0034 closes Phase 3: Tier 3 acceptance and the Tier 4 diagnostic.

Most of Tier 3 already exists and passes. =tier3_walk.rs= compares the walk's
draws as a trace prefix; =tier3_analysers.rs= runs 2,000 carried steps with the
generators in lockstep and 20 faults for 6 hours each. What B-0034 owes is
=xtask validate --tiers 3= wiring those up, and a run long enough to be called
acceptance: PLAN.org says all 22 scenarios.

Tier 4 is a *diagnostic and not a gate*, which =CLAUDE.md= is emphatic about.
Record error against =XNS(i)= for the first hours and the divergence point, and
attribute it: the =libm-system= feature exists precisely so the attribution can
be demonstrated rather than asserted. Flipping it should move the divergence
point, and if it does not, the explanation is wrong.

Expect divergence to be *fast*. The plant is open-loop unstable in pressure, and
B-0025 measured 8.5e-16 per step on the derivative; over a 48-hour run at one
second that is 172,800 steps of amplification. The number to record is where it
crosses =XNS(i)=, not whether it eventually does.

* 2026-08-27 B-0034 Tier 3 and Tier 4, and Phase 3 closes
:PROPERTIES:
:ITEM:     B-0034
:PHASE:    3
:TIER:     3
:COMMIT:   8f82168
:GFORTRAN: 15.2.0
:STATUS:   DONE
:END:
** Preflight
Clean and green. gfortran 15.2.0, unchanged. =xtask fidelity= 100 of 100 steps,
worst 3.521e-14. Provenance *1123/1589 (70.7%)*, unchanged.

** What changed
=xtask validate --tiers 3= and =--tiers 4= exist.
=crates/tepsim-oracle/tests/tier4_trajectory.rs= is new.

** Validation numbers
*Tier 4, nominal, 8 simulated hours (28,800 steps):*

| libm | worst error, as a fraction of =XNS(i)= | ever outside =XNS= |
|------+----------------------------------------+--------------------|
| vendored | 5.149e-5 | never |
| platform | *0.000e0* | never |

*All 21 scenarios, 4 hours each*, both configurations: every one stays within
=XNS= for the whole run, and every one is exactly 0.00e0 on the platform libm.

Worst error by scenario on the vendored libm, at 4 hours: nominal 2.45e-5,
=IDV(10)= 3.66e-8, =IDV(8)= 1.42e-8, =IDV(13)= 1.05e-8, the rest below 1e-8.

** Surprises and decisions
*The attribution is exact, not merely demonstrated.* =PLAN.org= asks that the
divergence "be explained, by showing that switching between the vendored libm
and the system libm *moves* the divergence point". It does better than move it:
with identical transcendentals the two trajectories are *bit-identical for
28,800 steps*, on all 41 measurements. Zero, not small.

So the divergence is transcendental rounding and nothing else, and the residual
under the vendored libm is 20,000 times below what the instruments could
resolve after eight hours.

*Getting to zero took finding one thing, and it was not rounding.* The first
run showed 7.285e-6 of =XNS= under =libm-system= that would not go away, which
should have been impossible: Tier 2 says the derivative is 0 ULP there.

=TEINIT= calls =TEFUNC= once itself at =teprob.f:1369=. By the time it returns,
the four Newton warm-start temperatures have already converged away from the
nominal literals that =TemperatureSeeds::default()= carries. A trajectory
started from the defaults is a *different trajectory*, not a rounding of the
same one.

B-0017 measured that seeding differently moves up to 21 of the 50 derivatives
in the last bits. This is what that looks like eight hours later, and it is the
third time in this project that the warm start has turned out to be
load-bearing where it looked like an implementation detail.

*Tier 4 confirmed B-0033 from a direction B-0033 could not.* =IDV(14)=,
=IDV(15)= and =IDV(19)= report worst errors identical to nominal, to every
digit, over a four-hour run. They are the sticking faults: with the command
held still their trajectory *is* the nominal trajectory. B-0033 established
that from the source and from single forced evaluations; here it falls out of
an integration nobody wrote to test it.

*Tier 4 runs both configurations, by construction.* The contrast is the result,
so =xtask validate --tiers 4= runs the file twice rather than leaving the second
half to whoever remembers. A single run of either half proves nothing on its
own: the vendored figure alone is unexplained, and the platform figure alone
describes a configuration that does not ship.

** Next
*Phase 4: the control layer.* Decompose it first, as its own item, on the
B-0012 and B-0027 precedent. Both of those found something in the decomposition
that reshaped the phase, and both took a full iteration to do it.

What is already known. =PLAN.org= describes nineteen near-identical =CONTRLn=
subroutines in =temain_mod.f=, each with its own =COMMON= block, on three
periods: 3 s for flow and level loops, 360 s for composition, 900 s for product
quality. All of them are velocity-form PI.

Two things to settle in the decomposition rather than during the work. First,
=temain_mod.f= is a *different file* from =teprob.f=, and everything the
provenance scanner knows about is =teprob.f=; the control layer will need its
own coverage accounting or it will be invisible to =xtask provenance=. Second,
=CLAUDE.md= lists "hard-coded =IDV(12)= in the driver" as a Class C quirk, which
is in =temain_mod.f=, so the driver comes with a delta before any of it is
ported.

The =d00= through =d21= datasets are closed-loop, so Tier 7 cannot start until
this phase lands. That makes Phase 4 the gate on Phases 5 and 7 both.

* 2026-08-27 B-0035 Decompose Phase 4: a cascade, an override, and a dead controller
:PROPERTIES:
:ITEM:     B-0035
:PHASE:    4
:TIER:     none
:COMMIT:   8cd17c9
:GFORTRAN: 15.2.0
:STATUS:   DONE
:END:
** Preflight
Clean and green. gfortran 15.2.0, unchanged. =xtask fidelity= 100 of 100 steps,
worst 3.521e-14. =validate --tiers 1,2,3,4= all green. Provenance
*1123/1589 (70.7%)*, unchanged: this item produces backlog items.

** What changed
Phase 4 decomposed into *B-0036* through *B-0041*, plus *B-0037b* which the
decomposition itself created.

** Validation numbers
None. What this item produced is four facts, extracted from
=reference/fortran/temain_mod.f= mechanically rather than read.

** Surprises and decisions
*The scheme is a cascade, and =PLAN.org= describes it as nineteen flat loops.*
Extracting each =CONTRLn='s output line showed eight subroutines with *no*
=XMV= assignment at all. They write another loop's setpoint:

: SETPT(3) = SETPT(3) + DXMV * 1.017 / 100.

The outer loop works in percent-of-span and multiplies back into the inner
loop's engineering units. The pairs are 13 to 3, 14 to 1, 15 to 2, 16 to 9, 17
to 4, 18 to 10, 19 to 6, and 20 to 16. That last one drives an *outer* loop, so
there are three layers, not two.

This is the finding that justifies the item. A port written from the plan's
description would have nineteen loops all writing valves, would run, and would
control nothing correctly.

*=CONTRL6= is not a PI controller.* =temain_mod.f:710-753= is a latching
override on the purge valve, keyed on separator pressure: wide open above 2950
kPa, shut below 2300, each latched by =FLAG= until pressure returns through
2633.7, at which point the valve is reset to 40.060 and =SETPT(6)= and
=ERROLD6= are reset with it. The PI runs only in the final =ELSE=, so while an
override is latched the controller does not run and its error history does not
advance.

=FLAG= lives in =COMMON/FLAG6/=, so it is genuine carried state. Worth checking
rather than assuming: the file contains no =SAVE= statement anywhere, and a
local without one has no guaranteed persistence, so a state machine on a bare
local would have been a real bug resting on gfortran's storage choices.

*=CONTRL22= is dead code, and the override explains it.* Twenty controllers are
defined and nineteen are called. =CONTRL22= is a separator-pressure controller
writing =XMV(6)=, which is the valve =CONTRL6= already owns, and its setpoint
is initialised to *2633.7* -- exactly the override's reset threshold. So it
looks like the pressure loop that =CONTRL6='s override replaced, left in place
with its tuning intact.

It is also the only controller whose error is *not* normalised by a span. Every
other loop computes =(SETPT - XMEAS) * 100. / span=; =CONTRL22= computes
=SETPT(12) - XMEAS(13)= raw. Consistent with it predating the others.

*The integral multiplier is the loop's own period.* =DELTAT * 3.= in the
3-second loops, =* 360.= in the 360-second ones, =* 900.= in =CONTRL20=, and it
agrees with the scheduler loop for loop. A port that wrote =3.= everywhere,
which is what the first reading of one subroutine suggests, would scale the
integral action of the slow loops by 120 and 300.

*The provenance question needs answering before any of this is ported, not
after.* =xtask provenance= scans =teprob.f= alone and the =@port= claim syntax
names no file, so 1,408 lines of control layer would land with no coverage
accounting whatever. That is B-0036, and it comes first.

** Next
B-0036: teach =xtask provenance= and the oracle about a second file.

Two pieces. The scanner needs =@port temain_mod.f:477-514= to parse and to
report per file, while a bare =teprob.f:= keeps working unchanged -- there are
about ninety existing claims and none of them should need editing.

The oracle needs to compile =temain_mod.f= and expose each =CONTRLn=. That is
*easier* than =TESUB5= was, not harder: they take no arguments and communicate
entirely through =COMMON=, so the binding is a bare =fn contrl1_()= and the
state is set through blocks the oracle already mirrors, plus =CTRLALL= and the
nineteen per-loop ones.

One thing to watch. =temain_mod.f= is a =PROGRAM=, not a library: it has a main
entry point, it opens fifteen files under =~/=, and it runs 172,800 steps on
load. Compiling it whole into the test binary would be wrong. Either extract
the subroutines, which means editing vendored source and is forbidden, or use
the instrumentation path that already exists for exactly this reason and rename
=PROGRAM= to a subroutine nothing calls. The second is in keeping: =ISD= was
hoisted the same way, and the pristine probe already proves such edits change
no numbers.

* 2026-08-27 B-0036 Provenance and the oracle over two files
:PROPERTIES:
:ITEM:     B-0036
:PHASE:    4
:TIER:     none
:COMMIT:   855430b
:GFORTRAN: 15.2.0
:STATUS:   DONE
:END:
** Preflight
Clean and green. gfortran 15.2.0. =xtask fidelity= 100 of 100, worst 3.521e-14.
Provenance *1123/1589 (70.7%)* on entry.

** What changed
=xtask provenance= is two-file aware. The oracle compiles =temain_mod.f= and
binds all twenty =CONTRLn= with their =COMMON= blocks.

** Validation numbers
Provenance now reports per file:

: reference/fortran/teprob.f       1123 of 1589 (70.7%)
: reference/fortran/temain_mod.f      0 of 1408  (0.0%)
: total                            1123 of 2997 (37.5%)

The =teprob.f= figure is unchanged, which is the point: ninety existing claims
parse identically.

** Surprises and decisions
*The coverage number nearly halved, and that is the correct answer.* 70.7%
became 37.5% the moment a second vendored file was counted. Nothing regressed;
1,408 lines of control layer had simply been invisible. Doing this *before*
porting any of it means the number goes up from an honest floor rather than
appearing to fall later.

*An unknown file name is an error, not a shrug.* The first draft filtered
unrecognised claims out, which is the obvious thing. It is also the worst
possible behaviour: a mistyped =teprob.for:= would stop counting, coverage
would drop by however many lines that claim covered, and the report would give
no hint why. It now fails and quotes what it saw.

*=temain_mod.f= is an unnamed main program.* No =PROGRAM= statement, so
gfortran compiles it to =MAIN__= and emits a =main=. Linked into a Rust test
binary that collides with the harness, and had it run it would open fifteen
files under =~/= and simulate 48 hours on load.

One instrumentation edit turns it into =SUBROUTINE TEMAIN_UNUSED=, which
nothing calls. The anchor is the one comment in the file with *two* spaces
before "MEASUREMENT"; the twenty-three copies inside the subroutines have
three. The uniqueness assertion that =instrument.rs= applies to every edit is
what makes an anchor that near-miss safe to use.

*Parameterless subroutines are the easiest binding in the project.* Each
=CONTRLn= takes no arguments and communicates entirely through =COMMON=, so the
binding is a bare =fn contrl1_()= and everything else is a struct mirroring a
block. =TESUB5=, with fourteen arguments and six written back, was far harder.

*Three of the seven tests are findings, not plumbing.*

The velocity form is confirmed *incremental*: a proportional loop makes no
further move on an unchanged error. A positional implementation would ramp its
valve without limit at a constant offset, and it is an easy thing to write by
accident.

The purge override latches in *both* directions, and while latched =ERROLD6=
does not advance. That confirms B-0035's reading of the branch structure: the
PI really is inside the final =ELSE= and really does not run.

A cascade controller moves a setpoint and leaves every valve untouched, checked
by comparing all twelve.

*=XMV(6)=40.060= and =SETPT(6)=0.33712= are single precision*, and the override
thresholds are mixed: 2950 and 2300 are exactly representable, 2633.7 is not.
The same trap as =teprob.f= throughout, in a file this project had not read
until today. The first version of the override test used =40.060_f64= and
failed, which is the third time this session that writing the double literal
has been the mistake.

*The checksums needed nothing.* =reference_integrity.rs= walks =reference/= and
requires every file present to be listed in the manifest, so =temain_mod.f= was
already covered. Checked rather than assumed.

** Next
B-0037: the velocity-form PI controller itself.

One type with two shapes, and a Tier 1-style differential against all twenty
=CONTRLn= bodies over a sweep of measurement and setpoint values. The oracle
side exists now, so this is a straight port-and-compare.

Three things the source settles that a summary would not. The error is
normalised by a per-loop span before the gain, so every gain is in
percent-of-span. The integral multiplier is the loop's own period, =3.=,
=360.= or =900.=, and B-0035 confirmed it matches the scheduler loop for loop.
And =CONTRL22= has no normalisation at all, which is consistent with it being
the older loop the override replaced.

Bind the sweep to the *published* tuning as well as to random values: B-0038
transcribes it, but a differential that only ever sees random gains would miss
a constant that is wrong in the fourth digit.

* 2026-08-28 B-0037 The velocity-form PI controller, one implementation for twenty
:PROPERTIES:
:ITEM:     B-0037
:PHASE:    4
:TIER:     1
:COMMIT:   b0f7995
:GFORTRAN: 15.2.0
:STATUS:   DONE
:END:
** Preflight
Clean and green. gfortran 15.2.0. =xtask fidelity= 100 of 100, worst
3.521e-14. Provenance unchanged.

** What changed
=tepsim-control= holds =Loop::increment=, =Tuning=, =Output= and =Period=.
=tier1_control.rs= compares it against nineteen =CONTRLn= bodies.

** Validation numbers
*9,500 cases*, 500 random tunings for each of nineteen loops:

| field | cases | max rel err | max ulp |
|-------+-------+-------------+---------|
| the increment applied | 9,500 | 0.000e0 | 0 |
| the error stored | 9,500 | 0.000e0 | 0 |

=CONTRL6= is excluded: it is an override state machine, and B-0037b covers it.

** Surprises and decisions
*Two of the eighteen spans are not exactly representable.* Every span is a
single-precision literal, and sixteen of them are integers or exact binary
fractions so it makes no difference. =1.017= is stored as 1.0169999599456787
and =1.6= as 1.600000023841858. Writing them as =f64= costs *5.4e-8* on
=CONTRL3=, five orders past the Tier 1 gate, and the first version of the test
did exactly that.

*The shape is an =Option=, not a flag.* Eight loops have no =TAUI= in their
=COMMON= block at all. Representing that as =taui: 0.0= with a boolean beside
it would put a division by zero one mistake away; =reset: Option<f64>= makes
the proportional form unable to reach the integral term.

*The integral multiplier is asserted equal to the schedule.* =Period= carries
both =steps()= and =seconds()=, and a test requires them to be the same number.
They are two independent facts in the original -- one in the scheduler at
=temain_mod.f:369-394= and one inside each subroutine -- and nothing there
connects them, so a port can get one right and the other wrong.

*The cascade rescaling uses the inner loop's span.* An outer loop converts
percent back into engineering units, and the plausible mistake is to use its
own span rather than the one it is writing into. A test walks the wiring: for
each cascade output it finds the loop that *owns* that setpoint and requires
the scale factor to be that loop's span. =CONTRL13= scales by 1.017, which is
=CONTRL3='s and not its own 100.

*One failure was my measurement, not the port.* The differential recovers the
increment as the output's change, and starting the output at 50 loses most of
the precision when the increment is small. That showed as 1.2e-11 with 24 cases
past 16 ULP, which looks exactly like a real error. Zeroing the output first
makes the recovery exact and the number became zero. Worth remembering: a
differential's own arithmetic is part of what it measures.

** Next
B-0037b: the cascade as a type, and the purge override.

The override is the harder half and B-0036 already pinned its behaviour against
the oracle: it latches in both directions, and while latched =ERROLD6= does not
advance. What remains is to port it, with =FLAG= as carried state, and to
record =CONTRL22= as a delta.

=XMV(6)=40.060= and =SETPT(6)=0.33712= are single precision, and the
thresholds are mixed: 2950 and 2300 exact, 2633.7 not. That is now three
separate places in this file where the precision has to be read off the line.

* 2026-08-28 B-0037b The cascade and the purge override, as types
:PROPERTIES:
:ITEM:     B-0037b
:PHASE:    4
:TIER:     1
:COMMIT:   f468d5d
:GFORTRAN: 15.2.0
:STATUS:   DONE
:END:
** Preflight
Clean and green. gfortran 15.2.0. =xtask fidelity= 100 of 100. Provenance
unchanged.

** Validation numbers
*A 108-step pressure walk*, crossing 2950, 2633.7 and 2300 repeatedly in both
directions and landing exactly on each. Compared at every step: =XMV(6)=,
=SETPT(6)=, =FLAG= and =ERROLD6=. All exact.

Plus =CONTRL22= in the sweep from B-0037: 500 tunings, 0 ULP.

** Surprises and decisions
*A single-step comparison would have proved nothing.* The override's answer
depends on where it has been, so the *sequence* is the claim. The walk is built
to re-enter each latch several times from different directions rather than to
visit each branch once.

*The error history is what makes it worth a differential.* The PI sits inside
the final =ELSE=, so while an override is latched =ERROLD6= does not advance. A
port that ran the loop anyway would produce entirely plausible valve positions
and a controller whose integral state had drifted. Comparing the valve alone
would not catch it; comparing =ERROLD6= does.

*The branch order is load-bearing.* The =>= 2950= test comes first, so a
pressure above 2950 re-latches *open* even from the shut state. Reordering the
arms to group the two latch cases together reads better and is wrong.

*Separator pressure is not under continuous control in this scheme.* That falls
out of D-008 rather than from anything in the plan. =CONTRL22= is the
separator-pressure PI loop, it is never called, and =CONTRL6='s override does
the job instead with a 650 kPa deadband. Anyone reasoning about the plant's
behaviour near the pressure limits needs that, and "nineteen near-identical PI
loops" actively obscures it.

=CONTRL22= is ported anyway and covered by the differential at 0 ULP. It costs
nothing, and it is the only evidence that will ever exist for that subroutine.

** Next
B-0038: the nineteen-loop preset, =temain_mod.f:246-317=.

Constants, verified two ways as B-0030's were: reparse the source, and assert
against =COMMON/CTRLALL/= and the twenty per-loop blocks.

Several gains are written as arithmetic rather than as a number:
=GAIN10 = -0.156 * 10.=, =GAIN19 = -83.2 / 5. / 3.=, =GAIN20 = -16.3 / 5.=,
=GAIN22 = -1.0 * 5.=. Transcribe the expression. B-0019 measured what happens
when a quotient of single-precision literals is folded in double, and
=-83.2 / 5. / 3.= is exactly that shape.

This is also the first item that will claim lines in =temain_mod.f=, so
provenance on that file stops being zero.

* 2026-08-28 B-0038 The Braatz preset, and twelve reset times that are all inexact
:PROPERTIES:
:ITEM:     B-0038
:PHASE:    4
:TIER:     none
:COMMIT:   9050513
:GFORTRAN: 15.2.0
:STATUS:   DONE
:END:
** Preflight
Clean and green. gfortran 15.2.0. =xtask fidelity= 100 of 100.

** What changed
=tepsim_control::PRESET= transcribes =temain_mod.f:246-317=. Provenance on that
file goes from *0* to *107 lines (7.6%)*; combined coverage is 41.0%.

** Validation numbers
Every setpoint, gain and reset time for all twenty loops, bit-identical to the
Fortran expressions evaluated the way Fortran evaluates them.

*Reset times: 12 of 12 differ between single and double folding.*

*Arithmetic gains: 4, of which at least 2 discriminate*, asserted so the test
cannot become vacuous.

** Surprises and decisions
*Every one of the twelve reset times is inexact.* They are all =N./3600.=, and
that form reads like a plain number rather than like arithmetic, which is
exactly why it is dangerous. B-0019's =40000.0/1.987= was visibly an
expression; =1452./3600.= looks like someone writing "1452 seconds" and it is a
single-precision division whose result differs from the double one in every
single case.

*The verification is weaker than B-0030's, and the log says so.* The walk spans
could be asserted against =COMMON/WLK/= because =TEINIT= runs and fills it. The
preset lives in the main program, which =instrument.rs= turned into a
subroutine nothing calls, so =COMMON/CTRLALL/= never receives it and there is
nothing to read back.

What is checked instead is that two independent transcriptions of the same
Fortran expressions agree bit for bit: one in the constant table and one in the
test, written from the listing separately. That still catches a hand-folded
expression, a missing =D= suffix or a transposed digit, which is the whole
class of error this project keeps finding. It does not catch both
transcriptions being wrong the same way, and that limitation is real.

*The tests assert their own discriminating power.* Requiring "single folding
matches" is worthless if single and double happen to agree, so both tests also
require that they *differ*, on at least half the cases. A test that cannot fail
is not evidence, and this one would have quietly become vacuous if the tuning
ever changed to round numbers.

*Three structural facts fall out of having the table at all.* Every setpoint
slot is owned by exactly one loop; every cascade target is a slot some loop
actually reads; and the eight proportional-only loops are exactly the eight
whose =COMMON= blocks declare no =TAUI=. None of those is checkable from a
single subroutine, and all three would be broken by a plausible transcription
slip.

** Next
B-0039: the multi-rate scheduler and the closed-loop driver,
=temain_mod.f:365-412=.

Three things to reproduce rather than tidy, all found in B-0035. The loops run
in *source order* within a period and share =XMV= through =COMMON=, so the
order is load-bearing wherever two touch the same valve. =CONSHAND= clamps
=XMV(1..11)= and *not* =XMV(12)=. And the driver overrides =XMV(1..11)= after
=TEINIT= with *rounded* values, =63.053= against =TEINIT='s =63.05263039=,
leaving =XMV(12)= alone.

That last one deserves a delta. A closed-loop run does not start where an
open-loop one does, and the difference is in the fourth decimal place of eleven
valve positions.

Also pin the *phase* of each period: =MOD(I,3)= with =I= starting at 1 fires
first on step 3, not step 1, and an off-by-one there shifts every controller
action by two seconds for the whole run.

* 2026-08-28 B-0039 The scheduler, the driver, and the ordering that was wrong
:PROPERTIES:
:ITEM:     B-0039
:PHASE:    4
:TIER:     4
:COMMIT:   bdd9813
:GFORTRAN: 15.2.0
:STATUS:   DONE
:END:
** Preflight
Baseline clean and green. =xtask fidelity=: 100 of 100 steps diffed, worst
3.521e-14 at =YP(12)= step 77, against a 1e-12 gate. Unchanged from B-0038,
to the last digit. Local gfortran 15.2.0 matches the one that recorded the
golden trace. Provenance found no new unclaimed ranges.

** What changed
=Scheme::step= dispatches the three periods, =Driver= places them in the
plant's loop, and =DRIVER_INITIAL_VALVES= starts a closed-loop run where the
Fortran driver starts one. A closed-loop differential against the oracle now
exists and runs in both libm configurations.

The iteration's real content is an ordering. =temain_mod.f:369-394= calls the
controllers and *then*, at line 409, calls =INTGTR=, which is what writes
=XMEAS=. So on iteration =I= every controller reads the measurements iteration
=I-1= produced. Every loop in the scheme carries one plant step of dead time
that appears in no controller. =CONSHAND= likewise runs after the integration,
so the clamp moved out of =Scheme::step= into =Driver::settle=.

** Validation numbers
Closed loop against the Fortran driver, nominal, 4 h at dt = 1 s, 14,400 steps:

| libm     | worst XMEAS      | worst XMV (%range) | first valve split |
|----------+------------------+--------------------+-------------------|
| platform | 0                | 0                  | never             |
| vendored | 8.746e-14 @ (1)  | 8.782e-12 @ (10)   | step 39           |

The platform-libm row is the result worth keeping: with identical =exp= and
=pow=, plant *and* controllers are bit-identical for the whole run. Nothing in
the control path introduces any error of its own.

The vendored row is amplification, and much smaller than the open-loop Tier 4
figures, which is what closing the loop should do: the controllers pull the
plant back toward a setpoint, so a one-ULP =exp= difference no longer
compounds freely. Compare B-0034's open-loop 8 h run, which left the noise
band. Here 8.7e-14 after four hours is within a few ULP.

Trip check: 8 simulated hours closed-loop, =ISD= zero at every one of 28,800
steps. Open loop the same plant trips.

The ordering, measured directly. First =CONTRL7= fire, step 3:

| driven from            | XMV(7)       |
|------------------------+--------------|
| previous step's XMEAS  | 34.147823842 |
| current step's XMEAS   | 35.623679189 |
| the Fortran            | 34.147823842 |

Before this was found, the closed loop diverged to 2.352e-1 at =XMEAS(14)= in
four hours. It is now 8.746e-14, a factor of 2.7e12.

D-009's rounding gaps, driver against =TEINIT=, per valve:

| 1       | 2       | 3       | 4       | 5 | 6       |
| 3.70e-4 | 2.94e-4 | 4.41e-4 | 7.63e-5 | 0 | 2.52e-4 |

| 7       | 8       | 9       | 10      | 11      | 12 |
| 3.43e-4 | 1.56e-4 | 2.63e-4 | 1.87e-4 | 5.09e-4 |  0 |

Tests: 277 default, 439 with =oracle=. Provenance 1123/1589 on =teprob.f=
(unchanged), 183/1408 on =temain_mod.f= (up from 107), 43.6% combined.

** Surprises and decisions
*Two orderings are both well-formed, and only one is right.* Nothing about
=Scheme= is wrong in the version that diverged. The scheduler fired on the
right steps, the loops ran in the right order, the clamp clamped eleven
valves. Every unit test passed. The bug was entirely in *when* the driver
handed the scheme its measurements, which is a property no test of the scheme
alone can see. That is why the differential is the acceptance criterion and
the unit tests are not, and why =Driver= exists as a type rather than as a
loop written inline in each test.

*=CONSHAND= is not =f64::clamp=.* It tests =.LE. 0.0=, so it replaces a
negative zero with a positive one. =f64::clamp= tests =<= and leaves negative
zero alone. =teprob.f:803-804= does the same job with *strict* comparisons, so
there =clamp= is exact, and =Plant= uses it. A comment there claimed the code
was written as two guards when it was a =clamp=; it now says which of the two
Fortran sites it is talking about and why the other one differs.

*The clamp's placement is observable only under a sticking fault.*
=teprob.f:801= moves =VCV= toward =XMV= only when they differ by more than the
stick threshold, and with =IDV(14)=, =IDV(15)= or =IDV(19)= active that
threshold is non-zero. An unclamped =XMV= of 105 crosses it at a different
moment than a clamped 100 does. So the placement is faithful even though today
every test would pass with it in either position. That is a case where a later
item, not this one, is what would have caught the error.

** Next
B-0040, the driver's hard-coded =IDV(12)=. =temain_mod.f:366-368= turns on
disturbance 12 unconditionally once =I >= SSPTS=, which is step 28,800, eight
hours in. It is Class C: it is not a modelling error, it is a scenario baked
into the driver, and it is the reason published closed-loop runs show a
disturbance nobody asked for at the eight-hour mark. Per =CLAUDE.md= it goes
behind a flag, gets its delta measured with the Tier 5 battery, and is marked
=BLOCKED= on a decision rather than made the default. The Tier 5 battery does
not exist yet, so the honest form of this item is: implement the flag,
reproduce the default behaviour exactly, measure what can be measured with
Tier 4, and record what Tier 5 will have to answer.

Note that the closed-loop differential above runs four hours, which is
deliberately *short of* step 28,800. Once =IDV(12)= is in, that run can be
extended past eight hours and the disturbance's arrival becomes the thing it
checks.

* 2026-08-28 B-0040 The driver's hard-coded IDV(12), and a warm start nobody resets

** Preflight
Clean and green. =xtask fidelity=: 100 of 100 steps, worst 3.521e-14 at
=YP(12)= step 77, identical to B-0039's. Provenance 1123/1589 and 183/1408, no
new unclaimed ranges. gfortran 15.2.0, unchanged.

** What changed
=temain_mod.f:366-368= is reproduced: =Driver= forces =IDV(12)= on at
=STEADY_STATE_STEPS= (3600*8), and
=DriverQuirks::only_the_requested_disturbances= turns that off. Default is the
faithful path, as =CLAUDE.md= requires for Class C.
=Driver::scenario_is_overridden= reports when the driver has gone past the
requested scenario.

The closed-loop differential now runs ten hours rather than four, so it covers
the mark instead of stopping short of it.

** Validation numbers
Closed loop against the Fortran driver, 10 h, 36,000 steps, crossing the
=IDV(12)= mark:

| libm     | worst XMEAS       | worst XMV (%range) | first split |
|----------+-------------------+--------------------+-------------|
| platform | 0                 | 0                  | never       |
| vendored | 2.694e-13 @ (37)  | 1.074e-11 @ (10)   | step 39     |

Bit-identical for 36,000 closed-loop steps under the platform libm, forced
disturbance included.

D-011, the Class C delta. Faithful against fixed, everything else identical:

| first difference       | step 29,390, =XMEAS(22)=            |
| identical before that  | all 41, all 29,389 steps, every bit |
| worst over the run     | 1.092e-1 at =XMEAS(37)=             |
| worst at hour ten      | 3.262e-2 at =XMEAS(38)=             |

Tests: 277 default, 442 with =oracle=. Provenance unchanged.

** Surprises and decisions
*The quirk does not take effect when it is switched on.* =IDV(12)= reaches the
plant through =IDVWLK(6)= (=teprob.f:351=), and =teprob.f:359= only redraws
that walk when =TIME= passes =TNEXT(6)=. So the flag flips at step 28,800 and
nothing moves until step 29,390, ten minutes later. My first version of the
test asserted the split was at the mark and failed. The fix was not to relax
the assertion but to check the step against the *Fortran*: two oracle runs
differing only in the forcing also part at 29,390. The number is now ground
truth rather than a recorded coincidence, and the onset moves with the seed, so
anyone hunting the quirk in a dataset by eye should not look at hour eight.

*=TEINIT= does not reset the Newton warm starts, and that made Tier 4
order-dependent.* =TCR=, =TCS=, =TCC=, =TCV= are read as guesses and written as
answers by =TESUB2= (=teprob.f:460=). Nothing in =teprob.f= ever assigns them
otherwise. They are process-global mutable state that survives =TEINIT=, so two
identical runs in one process disagree in the last bits and which answer you
get depends on the order the tests ran in.

This surfaced as the D-011 test reporting that two Fortran runs of the *same
scenario* differed at step 1. Repeated =TEFUNC(0)= does not converge to a fixed
point (=TESUB2= chatters in the last bits; see D-001), so the fix is
=Oracle::init_cold=, which zeroes the four before =TEINIT=. That reproduces a
freshly loaded process exactly, from any history: verified after runs of 1,
137, 5,000 and 20,000 steps. Both Tier 4 files now use it, and
=teinit_does_not_reset_the_newton_warm_starts= in =drive_fortran.rs= pins both
halves, that the problem is real and that the fix removes it.

Tier 2 was never exposed: =Scenario::force= restores the whole of
=COMMON/TEPROC/=, so the warm start is part of the scenario. Tier 1 is not
exposed either, since it calls the subroutines directly. So the exposure was
Tier 4 only, and the previously recorded Tier 4 numbers should be read as
having a last-bit dependence on test order. They are all diagnostic, so nothing
recorded needs revising, but B-0034's and B-0039's figures were produced under
the old behaviour.

*=CONSHAND= and the negative zero, again.* The new =scenario_is_overridden=
compares two flags that are exactly 0.0 or exactly 1.0. clippy wants an epsilon
and is wrong here, so it compares bits.

** Next
B-0041, Phase 4 acceptance. The remaining criterion in =BACKLOG.org= is a
48-hour closed-loop run that does not trip. That is 172,800 steps, which is
=NPTS= exactly, so it is the run the original driver was written to do.

Two things to be careful of. It crosses the =IDV(12)= mark at step 28,800 and
spends 40 of its 48 hours under a disturbance, so "does not trip" is a claim
about the *disturbed* plant, not the nominal one, and the log entry should say
so. And at 36,000 steps the closed-loop differential already takes six seconds
under the platform libm; 172,800 steps against the oracle will take roughly
half a minute, which is fine for =validate= but too slow for =ci=. Put the long
run behind =validate --tiers 4= and leave =ci= on the ten-hour version.

After that, Phase 5, which needs its decomposition written as the first action
of its first iteration. Everything in it (TOST, KS, ACF, Welch) has to be
implemented in Rust: no numpy, no scipy.

** What changed
The closed-loop differential runs =NPTS= steps: 172,800 at one second, the run
=temain_mod.f= was written to do. The horizon follows =TEP_TIER4_HOURS=, the
idiom =TEP_TIER1_SWEEP= already established, so =ci= runs ten hours and
=validate --tiers 4= runs all 48. The differential also reports error over
=XNS(i)=, which is the criterion =PLAN.org= states, instead of relative error
alone.

** Validation numbers
Closed loop, 48 h, 172,800 steps, nominal scenario with the driver's forced
=IDV(12)= from hour eight:

| libm     | worst XMEAS      | worst XMV (%range) | within XNS | at the end          |
|----------+------------------+--------------------+------------+---------------------|
| platform | 0                | 0                  | 48.000 h   | 0                   |
| vendored | 1.705e-10 @ (1)  | 1.246e-11 @ (10)   | 48.000 h   | 7.882e-11 x XNS(13) |

Bit-identical for all 172,800 closed-loop steps under the platform libm. Not
one measurement, not one valve, not one step.

Under the vendored libm the error never reaches a tenth of a billionth of any
instrument's noise, over two simulated days. Compare the *open*-loop figures
from B-0034: nominal 8 h ends at 5.149e-5 of =XNS=, six orders of magnitude
worse. Closing the loop suppresses the amplification, which is what a
controller pulling toward a setpoint should do and is worth having measured
rather than assumed.

Open loop from the same start, same forced =IDV(12)=, valves held: *trips at
step 11,017 (3.060 h) on =ReactorPressureHigh=*. So "48 hours without tripping"
is a statement about the control layer, not about a placid plant.

Tier 4 open loop, unchanged from B-0034: all 21 faults plus nominal stay within
=XNS= for the full 4 h, worst 2.45e-5 (nominal, and =IDV(14)=, =IDV(15)=,
=IDV(19)=, which are the valve-sticking faults and so are the nominal
trajectory until a valve sticks).

Tests: 277 default, 442 with =oracle=. Provenance unchanged at 43.6%.

** Surprises and decisions
*The open-loop trip is the interesting number.* The backlog asserted that the
plant runs 48 hours closed-loop "which open-loop it cannot", and it would have
been easy to record that as a verdict. Measuring it gives a much better fact:
three hours and four minutes, on reactor pressure. That is the actual size of
the claim the control layer is making, and it is now printed by the test rather
than asserted in prose.

*The D-011 delta test stays at ten hours* even under =TEP_TIER4_HOURS=48=. It
holds four complete measurement traces at once so it can compare them step by
step, and at 48 h that is 226 MB. Two hours past the mark is enough to measure
the delta. The sign-off item B-0040a is what runs it long, with Tier 5
statistics instead of stored traces.

*=cargo clippy --features oracle= is not clean*, and =ci= does not run it: the
gate runs =clippy --workspace --all-targets= with default features, so
=tepsim-oracle='s feature-gated modules are never linted. The failures are all
=suboptimal_flops= on expressions that must round exactly as the Fortran does,
so they are the lint being wrong rather than the code, but the gap itself is
real and nobody chose it. Not fixed here; it is a =ci= change, not a Phase 4
one.

** Next
*Phase 4 is closed.* Phase 5 is next, and =BACKLOG.org= deliberately leaves it
coarse: the first action of its first iteration is to decompose it, which is
that iteration's whole job.

The decomposition has one hard constraint that is not in =BACKLOG.org= and must
not be lost: *every statistic ships in Rust*. No numpy, no scipy, no calling
out to Python for a KS test. That means implementing, and testing against known
values, at minimum: Welch's t and the TOST wrapper around it, an F or Levene
test for variances, the two-sample Kolmogorov-Smirnov statistic and its
asymptotic p-value, the autocorrelation function, Welch's method for power
spectra, and the Pearson correlation matrix. Each of those is small; the risk
is that six small numerical routines with no reference to check against is
exactly the situation that produces a subtly wrong validation suite that passes
everything.

So the decomposition should put a *statistics kernel with its own known-answer
tests* first, before any of it is pointed at the plant. Check the routines
against published worked examples and against the oracle where one exists, not
against the plant data they are meant to judge.

Note also that Tier 5 needs long runs of both sources, and B-0041 just
established what that costs: 172,800 steps is about six seconds per source per
libm configuration in release. A Tier 5 battery over 21 faults and two sources
is therefore minutes, not hours, which means it can be a real gate rather than
a nightly.

* 2026-08-28 B-0042 Decompose Phase 5, and the statistics kernel
:PROPERTIES:
:ITEM:     B-0042
:PHASE:    5
:TIER:     5
:COMMIT:   451233f
:GFORTRAN: 15.2.0
:STATUS:   DONE
:END:
** Preflight
Clean and green. =xtask fidelity= 100 of 100 steps, worst 3.521e-14 at =YP(12)=
step 77, unchanged for a fourth iteration. Provenance 1306/2997, nothing new
unclaimed. gfortran 15.2.0.

** The split
Phase 5 becomes ten items, B-0042 through B-0051, ordered so that every
statistic exists and is checked before anything points it at plant data:
B-0042 the moment kernel and TOST, B-0043 Kolmogorov-Smirnov and energy
distance, B-0044 an FFT, B-0045 autocorrelation and Welch spectra, B-0046 the
correlation matrix and the physics invariants, B-0047 the battery, B-0048
=xtask validate --tiers 5= and the report, B-0049 the Tier 6 detectors, B-0050
the cross-source experiment, B-0051 Tier 7.

The ordering is deliberate. The temptation with a statistical validation suite
is to write the harness first and fill in the statistics as they are needed,
which produces routines whose only check is that the plant data they judge
looked plausible. Everything numerical here lands with a known-answer test
before it has anything to judge.

** What changed
=crates/tepsim-stats=, validation-only. =xtask ci='s isolation check now covers
both development-only crates rather than just the oracle, and it fails if a
crate it names does not exist, so a typo cannot silently turn it into a loop
over nothing.

Adding the crate departs from =PLAN.org='s workspace layout, so it is a
recorded decision (2026-08-28 in =BACKLOG.org=) with the plan updated. The
argument is short: the Tier 5 differentials are *tests* in =tepsim-oracle=, and
a test crate cannot sensibly depend on =xtask=, so the statistics need to be a
library.

Contents: =Summary= (Welford 1962 for the moments, Chan-Golub-LeVeque 1979 for
merging), =ln_gamma= (Lanczos, g = 7), the regularised incomplete beta (Lentz's
continued fraction), Student's t CDF and quantile, Welch's t-test, and TOST.

** Validation numbers
26 known-answer tests, 0 failures. Not one of them checks against a number this
project produced:

- =ln_gamma=: Gamma(n) = (n-1)! for n = 1..20; the half-integers from
  Gamma(1/2) = sqrt(pi) by recurrence; the reflection formula at six points.
- Incomplete beta: I_x(1,1) = x, I_x(a,1) = x^a, I_x(1,b) = 1-(1-x)^b over 7 x
  5 points; I_{1/2}(a,a) = 1/2 for a up to 500; the symmetry identity; NaN
  rather than a panic on five out-of-domain inputs.
- Student's t: the Cauchy closed form at df = 1 and the algebraic one at
  df = 2, ten points each; *thirteen published table entries* to better than
  5e-5; quantile inverts CDF to 1e-12 over 6 x 7 points.
- =Summary=: exact mean and variance of 1..=n for five n; merge equals
  summarising the concatenation to 1e-12.
- Welch: a fully hand-worked case, se = sqrt(2.5), t = -3/sqrt(2.5),
  df = 6.25/1.0625, each to 1e-13; df reduces to n_a+n_b-2 in the balanced
  equal-variance case.
- TOST: interval and p-value agree at all 400 margins tested, with both
  verdicts seen.

Accuracy, measured rather than assumed. The incomplete beta's error is
=eps * (16 + |ln_gamma(2a)|)= relative: 8 eps at a = 1, where the log-gamma
term vanishes, and 5.2e-13 at a = 500 against a bound of 1.3e-12. That is not a
tuned tolerance, it is the cancellation in the prefactor's exponent, and no
arrangement of this algorithm avoids it.

Mutation testing, since "26 tests pass" says nothing on its own:

| mutation                                      | tests failed |
|-----------------------------------------------+--------------|
| transposed digit in a Lanczos coefficient     |            6 |
| Welford using =delta= twice (the classic slip) |            6 |
| Welch df with =n= instead of =n-1=              |            2 |
| =merge= dropping the between-group term        |            1 |

A one-ULP change to a Lanczos coefficient passes everything, which is correct:
that is below the routine's own accuracy, and a test that failed on it would be
asserting noise.

Tests: 303 default (up from 277), 468 with =oracle= (up from 442).

** Surprises and decisions
*The obvious incomplete beta does not terminate.* The natural way to use the
symmetry =I_x(a,b) = 1 - I_{1-x}(b,a)= is to recurse. At =a == b= the
switchover point is exactly 1/2, so =I_{1/2}(a,a)= swaps to itself forever.
That is not a corner case: it is Student's t with one degree of freedom at
=t = 1=. It blew the stack on the first run. Fixed by applying the symmetry to
the continued fraction rather than by recursing, which is what Numerical
Recipes does and now the code says why.

*Two of the three test failures were the tests, not the code.* Worth writing
down because both are general:

The symmetry test as first written computed =left - (1 - right)=. With
=I_0.05(7.5,1.5) = 5.5e-10=, its partner is =1 - 5.5e-10=, and forming
=1 - that= in =f64= throws away nine significant digits *before* the
comparison. It reported 6.9e-8 error for a routine correct to 4e-17. Stated as
=I_x(a,b) + I_{1-x}(b,a) = 1= with an absolute tolerance, it passes at 4e-17.

The t-approaches-normal test ran at df = 1e6 and 1e7 and reported a 4.9%
discrepancy. The cause was resolution: at df = 1e7 the log-gammas are 7.7e7, so
the prefactor carries 1.7e-8 relative error, and the gap being measured is
7.6e-8 relative. The experiment was asking for four digits it did not have.
Moved to df = 1e4 and 1e5, where there are six orders of margin, and the test
became *stronger*: the measured coefficient of =phi(z)/n= agrees with the
expansion's =-(z^3+z)/4= to five significant figures at all four z, so it is
now asserted rather than printed.

Both are the same lesson in different clothes: before believing a numerical
test's verdict, check that the experiment can resolve what it is measuring.

** Next
B-0043, the two-sample Kolmogorov-Smirnov statistic and the energy distance.

Three things worth knowing before starting it. The KS statistic needs sorted
samples, which is where =alloc= finally enters this crate. The asymptotic
p-value comes from the Kolmogorov distribution =Q(x) = 2 sum (-1)^(k-1)
exp(-2 k^2 x^2)=, which converges fast for moderate x and badly for small x, so
the small-x branch needs the theta-function transformation and both branches
need pinning against published critical values (D_crit at alpha = 0.05 for
n = m = 100 is a number that can be looked up). And the energy distance is
O(n*m) in the naive form, which for 100 seeds by 172,800 samples is not
affordable; the item should measure the cost and say what it does about it
rather than discovering the problem in B-0047.

* 2026-08-28 B-0043 Kolmogorov-Smirnov and the energy distance
