** Preflight
Clean and green. Fidelity 100 of 100 steps, worst 3.521e-14 at =YP(12)= step 77,
a fifth iteration at the same number. Provenance 1306/2997 unchanged. gfortran
15.2.0.

** What changed
=tepsim-stats= gains =ks= and =energy=, and =alloc= (both sort). The KS
statistic is a merge walk; its p-value goes through Kolmogorov's distribution,
which needs two series because neither works everywhere; the energy distance
uses the one-dimensional closed form rather than the definition.

** Validation numbers
23 new tests, 0 failures. 326 tests default (up from 303), 491 with =oracle=.

Kolmogorov's distribution against the series the published tables are derived
from, three terms, to 1e-14 absolute at every alpha:

| alpha | c       | Q(c)           | 2nd term | 3rd term |
|-------+---------+----------------+----------+----------|
| 0.2   | 1.07298 | 0.199800002000 | 2.00e-4  | 2.00e-9  |
| 0.1   | 1.22387 | 0.099987500004 | 1.25e-5  | 3.91e-12 |
| 0.05  | 1.35810 | 0.049999218750 | 7.81e-7  | 7.63e-15 |
| 0.025 | 1.48021 | 0.024999951172 | 4.88e-8  | 1.49e-17 |
| 0.01  | 1.62762 | 0.009999998750 | 1.25e-9  | 3.91e-21 |
| 0.005 | 1.73082 | 0.004999999922 | 7.81e-11 | 7.63e-24 |
| 0.001 | 1.94947 | 0.001000000000 | 1.25e-13 | 3.91e-30 |

The two series agree to 1e-13 across the whole overlap 0.75 to 1.25.

Energy distance, fast form against the definition: worst relative 3.4e-14 over
100 random pairs; 4.8e-12 on plant-shaped data.

*Cost, which B-0047 needs.* 172,800 samples per side:

| form           | time                       |
|----------------+----------------------------|
| O(n log n)     | 281 ms                     |
| the definition | 1186 s (projected from 1k) |

A factor of about 4,200. Tier 5 is 22 scenarios by 53 variables by two sources;
the definition would be roughly a fortnight of compute per battery, the closed
form about seven minutes.

Stephens' correction shifts lambda by 1.92% at n = m = 100, and p at D = 0.2 by
more than 1e-3.

Mutations, all caught: no Stephens correction (1 test), alternating series used
below its switchover (1), wrong weight =(2j-n)= in the pair sum (2), no centring
(1), naive tie walk (2, after the fix below).

** Surprises and decisions
*The fast energy distance needs its inputs centred, and the plant is exactly the
data that shows it.* =self_absolute_sum= accumulates =(2j - n - 1) * x_j=, whose
weights sum to zero. At 2705 kPa with a spread of tenths those terms reach 1e6
and the answer is 1.6e4: four digits lost before the outer =2A - B - C=
subtraction removes three more. Uncentred it disagreed with the definition in
the eighth significant figure. The statistic is translation-invariant, so
subtracting a common offset costs nothing and fixes it: 1.4e-8 becomes 4.8e-12.
Random uniform data on [0,1] did *not* show this. The plant-shaped fixture did.

*Two of my KS table values were transcription errors.* =alpha = 0.025= was
written as 1.48007 and =alpha = 0.005= as 1.73115. The routine says 1.48021 and
1.73082, and the relation those tables come from, =c = sqrt(-ln(alpha/2)/2)=,
confirms the routine. Both rows are dropped from the table test, which now
carries only the four values every table prints, and the derivation is checked
separately and much more sharply. This is the fourth time in this project that a
number written from memory has been the error rather than the code, which is why
the constants rule exists.

*The tie mutation passed at first, and the reason is worth keeping.* Every tie
case I had written was balanced, and for a balanced tie the intermediate points
of the naive walk lie between the endpoints, so the maximum is unaffected. The
distinguishing case is one tied value with very different sample sizes: two
point masses at 7, with n = 2 and m = 100, where the correct D is 0 and the
naive walk reports 0.98. That case is now in, along with a brute-force
supremum cross-check over 200 quantised random pairs that would have caught it
generically.

The lesson generalises past this test: a hand-written fixture tests the case its
author was thinking about. The mutation test is what finds the case they were
not.

** Next
B-0044, the FFT. Radix-2 Cooley-Tukey, deterministic: fixed summation order, no
threading, no runtime planning.

Known answers are easy to come by and should be taken: hand-computed DFTs at
n = 2, 4 and 8 (the n = 4 transform of =[1,0,0,0]= is all ones, of =[1,1,1,1]=
is =[4,0,0,0]=, and the transform of a pure bin-k sinusoid is two spikes);
agreement with a naive O(n^2) DFT over random inputs; Parseval's identity; and
the forward-inverse round trip. Note that the round trip and Parseval are *not*
sufficient on their own, since a transform with a permuted output satisfies
both.

One design note for B-0045 that belongs here: Welch's method needs the segment
length to be a power of two, and at dt = 1 s over 48 h there are 172,800
samples, so segments of 4096 with 50% overlap give 83 averages and a resolution
of 0.244 mHz. That is the shape to aim at, and it means the FFT never needs
Bluestein.

* 2026-08-28 B-0044 A deterministic radix-2 FFT
:PROPERTIES:
:ITEM:     B-0044
:PHASE:    5
:TIER:     5
:COMMIT:   7fc130e
:GFORTRAN: 15.2.0
:STATUS:   DONE
:END:
** Preflight
Clean and green. Fidelity 100 of 100 steps, worst 3.521e-14 at =YP(12)= step 77,
a sixth iteration unchanged. Provenance 1306/2997. gfortran 15.2.0.

** What changed
=tepsim_stats::fft=: a =Complex= type, an =Fft= that owns its twiddle table, an
in-place iterative forward transform, an inverse by conjugation, and
=dft_naive= as the reference. Powers of two only.

** Validation numbers
12 tests, 0 failures. 338 tests default (up from 326).

| comparison                           | worst relative |
|--------------------------------------+----------------|
| fast against the definition, n <=1024 | 4.469e-16      |
| plant-shaped n = 4096                | 6.123e-17      |
| forward-inverse round trip, n <=4096  | 7.459e-16      |
| Parseval, n = 4, 32, 256, 2048       | under 1e-14    |

At n = 4096 a two-tone plant-shaped signal recovers its DC level to 1e-9 and
both tone amplitudes to 1e-11.

Mutations, all caught: no bit-reversal (8 tests), twiddle sign flipped (4),
twiddle table indexed without the stage stride (6), sign error in the complex
multiply (6).

** Surprises and decisions
*The reference was wrong, and it was wrong in a way that looked exactly like the
code being wrong.* =dft_naive= computed =cos(-2 pi j k / n)= directly. At
n = 4096 with j = k = 4095 that argument is 2.6e4 radians, so the argument
*itself* carries =eps * 2.6e4 = 5.7e-12= of error before =cos= is even called.
The FFT, whose twiddles never leave one turn, disagreed with it by 1.7e-13, and
the obvious move at that point is to widen the tolerance to 1e-12 and move on.

Reducing =j * k= modulo =n= first is exact integer arithmetic, costs nothing,
and took the disagreement to 6.1e-17. The same bug was in the shifted-impulse
fixture, where the unreduced argument reaches 389 radians at size 64; it showed
up only after the tolerance had been tightened, which is the argument for
tightening.

So the tolerances went *down*, not up: 1e-13 to 1e-14 on the hand-computed
values and 1e-15 on the plant-shaped case, against 4.5e-16 measured. A tolerance
two orders above the truth is a place a regression can live.

This is the second iteration running where the reference, not the routine, was
the least accurate thing in the comparison (B-0043's energy distance needed
centring for the same reason). Worth stating as a rule: *before believing a
numerical disagreement, establish which side of it is less accurate.*

*Parseval and the round trip are the two tests everyone writes and both are
blind to a permuted output.* A permutation is unitary, so Parseval holds; a
permutation composed with its inverse is the identity, so the round trip holds.
Bit-reversal is the easiest part of an FFT to get wrong. The tests that actually
carry the case here are the hand-computed n = 4 transforms and the shifted
impulse, whose phase winds =delay= times and therefore names every bin
individually. The mutation run confirms it: removing the bit-reversal fails
eight tests, and the round trip is among them only because =inverse= is built on
=forward= rather than being independently wrong.

** Next
B-0045, the autocorrelation function and Welch spectra.

The ACF has an easy known answer: an AR(1) process with parameter =phi= has
theoretical ACF =phi^k=, so generating one with a fixed seed and checking the
sample ACF against =phi^k= to within the sampling error is a real test. The
sampling error for =n= samples is roughly =1/sqrt(n)=, so use enough samples
that the tolerance is meaningful, and state it rather than tuning it.

For Welch, decide and *write down* the window and the overlap rather than
inheriting a default. Hann with 50% overlap is the usual choice and its noise
bandwidth is 1.5 bins; the normalisation must divide by the window's sum of
squares or every spectrum is wrong by a constant that no test comparing two
spectra to each other would ever notice. That last point deserves a test that
checks an *absolute* level, not a ratio: a unit-amplitude sinusoid should come
back with the power it actually has.

Note also that Tier 5 compares spectra *band by band* rather than as one
scalar, per =PLAN.org=. The item's output should be per-band ratios with the
band edges stated.

* 2026-08-28 B-0045 Autocorrelation and Welch spectra
:PROPERTIES:
:ITEM:     B-0045
:PHASE:    5
:TIER:     5
:COMMIT:   646dafa
:GFORTRAN: 15.2.0
:STATUS:   DONE
:END:
** Preflight
Clean and green. Fidelity 100 of 100 steps, worst 3.521e-14 at =YP(12)= step 77,
a seventh iteration unchanged. Provenance 1306/2997. gfortran 15.2.0.

** What changed
=tepsim_stats::serial=: =autocorrelation= (through the FFT) with
=autocorrelation_direct= beside it, =bartlett_standard_error=, =Window=,
=Spectrum=, =welch=, =band_comparison= and =log_band_edges=.

These are the first Tier 5 statistics that see *time*. Shuffling a series leaves
its mean, variance, histogram and KS statistic untouched and destroys its
spectrum, so nothing before this could have detected the failure =PLAN.org=
names: "the Fortran limit-cycles, the port damps".

** Validation numbers
18 tests, 0 failures. 356 default (up from 338), 521 with =oracle=.

Autocorrelation, transform against the definition, worst absolute difference:

| fixture                            | difference |
|------------------------------------+------------|
| white noise                        | 1.561e-16  |
| a strong trend                     | 6.328e-15  |
| a slow sinusoid                    | 6.772e-15  |
| plant-shaped, offset plus a ripple | 4.330e-15  |

Cost, release, one 172,800-sample series to lag 200: transform 41 ms, direct
65 ms, a factor of 1.6. The scaling is the point, not the constant: at lag 1000
the transform is unchanged and the direct form is five times worse.

AR(1), n = 400,000, judged against Bartlett rather than white noise:

| phi  | worst deviation | at lag | Bartlett SE | white-noise SE |
|------+-----------------+--------+-------------+----------------|
| 0.5  |            1.20 |      5 |     0.00288 |        0.00158 |
| 0.8  |            1.25 |     16 |     0.00481 |        0.00158 |
| 0.95 |            0.53 |      4 |     0.00253 |        0.00158 |

Welch, absolute level. A sinusoid of amplitude A must integrate to A^2/2:

| A    | rectangular | Hann     |
|------+-------------+----------|
| 1    | 4.44e-16    | 2.22e-16 |
| 0.25 | 4.44e-16    | 2.22e-16 |
| 7.5  | 3.79e-16    | 5.05e-16 |

White noise: integrated density 0.082872 against a variance of 1/12 = 0.083333,
0.55% out, which is the sampling error of 200 segments.

Plant-shaped, offset 2705 with a 0.4-amplitude oscillation: total power to
1e-9 of 0.08, DC bin below 1e-6.

Hann leaks less than a hundredth of what rectangular does beyond five bins from
a between-bins tone.

48 h at one second with 4096-sample segments: 83 averages, 0.879 mHz
resolution.

Mutations, all caught: normalising by =(sum w)^2= (3 tests), no one-sided fold
(3), no per-segment mean removal (1), symmetric instead of periodic Hann (1),
ACF padded to =n= rather than =2n= (1).

** Surprises and decisions
*The white-noise error band is the wrong band, and using it fails a correct
implementation.* The AR(1) test at =phi = 0.8= came out at 3.8 white-noise
standard errors and I had asserted three. The temptation is to widen to four.
The actual answer is that =1/sqrt(n)= is Bartlett's formula *specialised to
white noise*, and a correlated series has a larger standard error: at
=phi = 0.95= it is about =4.4/sqrt(n)= at large lag, so the white-noise band
would reject a good estimate most of the time. With the general formula the
worst deviation across all three =phi= is 1.25.

=bartlett_standard_error= is now in the crate rather than the test, because
Tier 5 will judge plant measurements, and plant measurements are strongly
autocorrelated. It needs an ACF that reaches several times past the lag of
interest: at =phi = 0.95= the correlation is still 0.36 at lag 20, and
truncating the sum there understates the error badly. The test therefore
computes 400 lags to judge 20.

*The test that matters most is the one nothing else can do.* Tier 5 compares two
spectra to each other. A normalisation wrong by a constant factor cancels
exactly in every such comparison, so it is invisible to every test that
compares spectra. It is only visible against an absolute standard, which is why
=a_sinusoid_carries_the_power_it_actually_has= exists and why the mutation
dropping the one-sided fold fails three tests rather than none.

*Two mutations appeared uncaught and were not.* Both =sed= expressions failed to
match after =cargo fmt= had reflowed the code, so nothing was mutated and the
suite passed on unmodified source. Re-applied properly, each fails three tests.
Worth stating because a mutation test that silently fails to mutate reports
exactly what a passing test suite reports.

*Half-open bands exclude Nyquist.* =band_power= is =[low, high)= so adjacent
bands do not double-count their shared edge, which means a set of bands running
from the resolution to =sample_rate / 2= accounts for the total minus the DC
*and* Nyquist bins. Documented on =band_power= and asserted both ways, since
B-0047 would otherwise find every ratio slightly off and have to work out why.

** Next
B-0046, the correlation matrix and the physics invariants.

The correlation matrix is easy and the invariants are the interesting half.
They are the only test in the entire ladder that can catch an error the port
*faithfully inherited*: everything else compares against the Fortran, and an
error in the Fortran is invisible to all of it.

Concretely, from =teprob.f=: total mass in equals total mass out plus
accumulation; each of the eight components' atoms are conserved except where a
reaction consumes them, and the reaction stoichiometry at =teprob.f:512-527=
says by how much; and the energy balances at =teprob.f:762-811= should close
against the enthalpy differences.

Two cautions. First, run every invariant against the *oracle* as well as the
port and attribute any failure explicitly to one or the other. An invariant that
fails on both is a finding about the original, which is Class B and belongs in
=deltas.md=, not a bug to fix. Second, state each invariant as an equation with
its =teprob.f= line range before writing any code for it, because "mass should
balance" is not a testable statement until it says which streams and over what
control volume.

* 2026-08-28 B-0046 The correlation matrix and the physics invariants

** Preflight
Clean and green. Fidelity 100 of 100, worst 3.521e-14 at =YP(12)= step 77, an
eighth iteration unchanged. Provenance 1306/2997. gfortran 15.2.0.

** What changed
=tepsim_stats::correlation=, and =crates/tepsim-oracle/tests/tier5_invariants.rs=
with four conservation invariants.

The invariants are the only tests in the whole ladder that can catch an error
the port *faithfully inherited*. Every other tier compares against the Fortran,
so an error in the Fortran is invisible to all of them.

** Validation numbers
19 new tests (10 correlation, 9 invariants), 0 failures. 366 default (up from
356), 540 with =oracle= (up from 521).

| invariant                        | Fortran   | port      | gate  |
|----------------------------------+-----------+-----------+-------|
| I-1 reaction mass, 200 states    | 4.664e-16 | 3.498e-16 | 1e-14 |
| I-2 plant mass balance, nominal  | 2.079e-16 | 2.344e-16 | 1e-13 |
| I-2 along 2 h open loop          | 6.556e-16 | -         | 1e-13 |
| I-3 inert reaction term          | exactly 0 | exactly 0 | exact |
| I-4 per-component moles, 1 h     | 7.436e-15 | 2.892e-15 | 1e-13 |

All four reactions balance exactly with the published molecular weights:
=2+28+32=62=, =2+28+46=76=, =2+46=48=, =3*32=2*48=. That is why I-1 is an
equality and not a tolerance.

I-4's teeth are checked: the largest reaction term over its run is 457.5
lbmol/h, so it genuinely exercises the term it is there to see. I-2's are too:
the accumulation reaches 0.0613 lb/h against a residual of 2.1e-11 lb/h, a
factor of 2.9e9.

Mutations: D's coefficient 1.5 to 1 fails 2; the inert given a reaction term
fails 2; the purge dropped from the separator balance fails 1; the reaction
dropped from the reactor balance fails 1, *and only I-4 catches it*.

** Surprises and decisions
*I-2 structurally cannot see a missing reaction term, and I only learned that
from a mutation.* Deleting =CRXR= from the reactor's component balance left the
total mass balance passing at 2e-16. That is not a weak test, it is a true fact
about the invariant: I-2 is the =XMW=-weighted sum of I-4, and I-1 says the
reaction is mass-neutral, so the reaction term cancels out of I-2 exactly. Total
mass conservation is blind to stoichiometry by construction.

The fix was to add the unweighted per-component balance, I-4, which sees it
immediately. The general lesson: an invariant that is a *sum* of other
invariants is weaker than the set, and how much weaker is not obvious from
reading it.

*Two failures were my formula, not the plant, and both looked like the plant.*

=XMWS= is filled for streams 1, 2, 6, 8, 9 and 10 only (=teprob.f:529-548=).
Streams 3, 4, 11 and 13 keep whatever the slot held, and two of those are feeds
or products this invariant needs. Reading =XMWS(13)= gave a mass balance failing
by 95%, *identically on both implementations*. Identical failure on both is the
signature of a wrong formula rather than a wrong plant, and it is worth naming
as a diagnostic: a real inherited error would also fail on both, but it would
not fail at 95%.

Then, with that fixed, the port sat at 6.9e-9 against the Fortran's 2.1e-16.
Cause: I rebuilt the port's stream table with =WalkInputs::default()= instead of
the walk inputs the derivative actually used, so the A/C feed composition
differed between the two halves of the balance. Seven orders of magnitude from
one wrong argument.

*The gate went from 1e-11 to 1e-13.* Both implementations close to one or two
ulp of a 32,000 lb/h throughput. A term dropped from a balance equation is an
entire stream, of order 1e-5 relative at the very smallest, so asking for what
the code delivers costs nothing and leaves nowhere for a regression to hide.

*The correlation matrix needed one square root, not two.* =r(x, 2x)= came out
0.9999999999999998 because =sqrt(5) * sqrt(20)= is 10.000000000000002 while
=sqrt(5 * 20)= is exactly 10. Storing sums of squares and taking a single root
of their product is both cheaper and exactly right on the cases where the answer
is exactly plus or minus one.

** Next
B-0047, the Tier 5 battery. Every statistic now exists and every one has been
checked against something outside this project.

The remaining design questions, which the item should settle *before* running
anything:

*What is a sample?* =PLAN.org= says 100 seeds by 22 scenarios by 48 hours by two
sources. That is 4400 runs of 172,800 steps. B-0041 measured a closed-loop run
against the oracle at about 6 seconds; port-only will be faster, but 4400 runs
is still an hour or two. Decide whether the battery samples the trajectory (say
every 180 steps, as =temain_mod.f='s own output does) or keeps every step,
because the KS and energy statistics scale with the sample count and the
autocorrelation does not.

*What is the equivalence margin?* =PLAN.org= says one tenth of the Fortran
standard deviation for the means. It says nothing about the margins for
variances, KS, spectra or the Frobenius norm. Those have to be chosen and
written down *before* the numbers are seen, or they are not margins.

*How does a failure get reported?* Per =CLAUDE.md=, a statistic outside its
margin is a =BLOCKED= item with the numbers, never a widened margin. The battery
should make that easy: the natural output is a table of every statistic for
every scenario with its margin beside it.

** Preflight
Clean and green. Fidelity 100 of 100, worst 3.521e-14 at =YP(12)= step 77, a
ninth iteration unchanged. Provenance 1306/2997. gfortran 15.2.0.

** The split
B-0047 asked for the whole battery in one iteration, and the sizing rule says
not to start what cannot be finished. Measured cost decided it: 766 ms per
48-hour port run, 2100 runs per source, so about an hour of simulation before
any statistic is computed, and the margins for four of the six statistics have
to be *calibrated* rather than chosen.

Split into B-0047a, which produces runs, and B-0047b, which judges them.

** What changed
=tepsim_oracle::tier5=. A run is a scenario at a seed, closed loop, sampled
every 180 steps as =temain_mod.f:401= samples: 960 rows of 53 variables over
48 hours.

The closed-loop driving that lived inside =tier4_closed_loop.rs= moved into the
library. Both tiers now drive the Fortran through one transcription of
=temain_mod.f='s loop instead of two, which is one fewer place for the two to
drift apart.

** Validation numbers
9 new tests, 0 failures. 366 default (unchanged, the harness is oracle-gated),
549 with =oracle= (up from 540).

| check                                    | result             |
|------------------------------------------+--------------------|
| samples per 48 h run                     | 960 x 53 variables |
| port run, 48 h, release                  | 766 ms             |
| full battery, 2100 runs, per source      | about 27 min       |
| sources agree, platform libm, 3 scenarios | 0, bit-identical   |
| sources agree, vendored libm             | 2.056e-13          |
| five nominal seeds, worst spread         | 1.366e1            |

All twenty disturbances move the plant within two hours. Smallest departures:
=IDV(3)= 4.076e-3, =IDV(9)= 3.531e-3, =IDV(15)= 9.839e-3. Largest: =IDV(6)=
3.467e0, =IDV(1)= 2.456e0.

** Surprises and decisions
*Twenty-one scenarios, not twenty-two.* =PLAN.org= says twenty-two. The
vendored =teprob.f= has twenty disturbances: =teprob.f:340= is =DO 500 I=1,20=
and =tepsim_core::FAULTS= has twenty entries. The extra one comes from later
versions of the model, which add =IDV(21)=, and the number in the plan was
written from that literature rather than from this source. Settled against the
source, as the protocol requires, and asserted so it cannot drift back.

*The published seed table, and three things wrong with it.* =teprob.f:1187-1256=
carries fifty-four generator words in comments, one per published dataset, plus
the compiled-in one and two others. It is the only statement anywhere of how
=d00= through =d26= were made, so B-0051 depends on it entirely and it is now
transcribed and asserted.

Thirty-four of the fifty-four exceed 2^32. That is not a transcription error:
=TESUB7= computes =DMOD(G * 9228907, 2^32)=, so the first draw reduces any seed.

Twenty-seven of the fifty-four are *even*. A multiplicative generator modulo a
power of two keeps the factors of two its seed has, so those runs have a
shorter period and low bits that never move. That is what the original did, and
reproducing the published files means doing the same rather than fixing it.

And =d19_te= is written =G=9090909232.DO=, with the letter O in place of the
zero. Inside a comment, so nothing ever caught it. Recorded as =9090909232= on
the assumption the digit was meant, and flagged, so that a Tier 7 failure on
=d19= has an obvious first suspect rather than a mystery.

*The tests that matter here are about the harness, not the plant.* A harness
whose seed did nothing would produce a hundred identical runs, every statistic
would have zero variance, and every equivalence test in B-0047b would pass
trivially. So: seeds are asserted distinct and odd; runs are asserted
reproducible *with another run interleaved*, which is what catches =COMMON=
leaking between Fortran runs; and different seeds are asserted to give a spread
of 1.4e1 rather than a rounding difference.

** Next
B-0047b, the battery.

The design question it must answer first, before running anything: *what is the
margin?* =PLAN.org= gives one only for the means, a tenth of the Fortran
standard deviation. For the variance ratio, the KS statistic, the energy
distance and the Frobenius norm there is no absolute scale at all: those are
two-sample statistics whose value depends on the sample size and the process,
so "less than 0.05" is not a claim about equivalence.

The reference has to be the Fortran's own run-to-run variability. Split its
hundred seeds into two halves of fifty, compute each statistic
Fortran-against-Fortran, and require the cross-source value to sit inside that
distribution. That is Tier 6's argument applied one tier early, and it makes
every margin a measurement rather than a choice.

Write the margins down before looking at the cross-source numbers. And note
=CLAUDE.md=: a statistic outside its margin is a =BLOCKED= item with the
numbers, never a widened margin.

* 2026-08-28 B-0047b The Tier 5 battery, with calibrated margins
:PROPERTIES:
:ITEM:     B-0047b
:PHASE:    5
:TIER:     5
:COMMIT:   31d184b
:GFORTRAN: 15.2.0
:STATUS:   DONE
:END:
** Preflight
Clean and green. Fidelity 100 of 100, worst 3.521e-14 at =YP(12)= step 77,
unchanged for a tenth iteration. Provenance 1306/2997. gfortran 15.2.0.

** What changed
=tier5::battery=: TOST on per-run means and log variances, and four calibrated
statistics (Kolmogorov-Smirnov, energy distance, autocorrelation, Welch
spectra) plus the correlation matrix's Frobenius distance.

The design question this item existed to answer was *where the margins come
from*, and the answer is that four of the six are measured rather than chosen.
Split the reference's seeds in half, compute the statistic against itself over
twenty deterministic splits, and treat the cross-source value as one more draw
from that null. The p-value is =(1 + #{within >= cross}) / (K + 1)=, gated at
0.05, so with twenty splits the cross-source value fails exactly when it is the
strict maximum of the twenty-one.

** Validation numbers
Smoke battery in =ci=: 3 scenarios, 4 seeds, 2 h, 12 runs per source, 14 s.

Full battery, *partial*: 3 of 21 scenarios at 100 seeds by 48 hours, stopped by
direction after about 25 minutes to redirect effort to Phase 6.

| scenario | worst mean power | frobenius cross | within max | p      |
|----------+------------------+-----------------+------------+--------|
| nominal  |             1.59 |        1.186e-2 |      7.595 | 1.0000 |
| IDV(1)   |             1.61 |        7.080e-3 |      5.703 | 1.0000 |
| IDV(2)   |             0.23 |        2.827e-5 |      1.924 | 1.0000 |

Every calibrated statistic passed at p = 1.0000 on all three: the cross-source
value was never the maximum of its null. The Frobenius distance is three to
five orders of magnitude inside the within-source spread.

TOST power against battery size, measured:

| battery                  | worst power |
|--------------------------+-------------|
| 4 seeds, 2 h (smoke)     |       11.93 |
| 8 seeds, 12 h            |        3.38 |
| 8 seeds, 48 h            |        1.04 |
| 100 seeds, 48 h, nominal |        1.59 |
| 100 seeds, 48 h, IDV(2)  |        0.23 |

** Surprises and decisions
*The margin's achievability depends on the scenario, and nobody had noticed.*
=PLAN.org= sets the mean margin at a tenth of the standard deviation. Which
standard deviation? The pooled one, and a disturbed plant has a much larger
pooled spread than a quiet one. So the same absolute run-to-run wander in the
mean sits comfortably inside the margin under =IDV(2)= and outside it at the
nominal operating point. At the nominal plant the margin needs about *255
seeds*, not 100.

The variables that run out of power are the manipulated ones, =XMV(1..12)=.
That is what an integrating controller does: its output has a random-walk
component, so its mean over 48 hours varies between seeds by much more than a
tenth of its own within-run spread. This is a fact about the plant, not about
the port.

*An underpowered test has not failed.* It has declined to decide, and reporting
it as a difference would be exactly the error TOST exists to prevent, in the
other direction. =Report::undecided= separates them and =seeds_for_power= says
what the margin would cost. The battery still asserts on the *measured* gap for
undecided variables: a difference well outside the margin is a finding even
when the test lacks the power to say so formally.

*Two harness bugs, both found only by running long.*

=TRCN=, the Tier 3 trace counter, is a Fortran =INTEGER= that nothing clears
between evaluations. At about 264 draws per step over 172,800 steps it passes
2^31 after roughly fifty runs, goes negative, passes the =TRCN.LE.TRCCAP=
guard, and writes outside the array. The symptom was a SIGSEGV deep inside the
Fortran, tens of millions of steps into the battery, with nothing nearby to
suggest why: the same seed ran perfectly in isolation, and starting at seed 40
moved the crash to seed 90, which is what identified it as cumulative rather
than data-dependent. It now saturates at =TRCCAP+1=. Nothing in Tiers 1 to 4
was ever affected because no shorter run approached the overflow, and fidelity
plus all seven Tier 3 files pass unchanged.

=welch_t= computed =(n - 1)= on a =Summary= with no observations. In debug that
panics; in release it wraps to =usize::MAX= and returns a degrees-of-freedom
figure that looks like a number. The case is =XMV(12)=, the agitator, which is
never written by a controller: every run has zero variance, so the log-variance
ensemble is empty. Now =NaN=, with a known-answer test.

Both are the same lesson. A validation harness is code, it has bugs, and the
bugs it has are the ones only its longest runs reach.

** Next
*Redirected by the user to Phase 6*: stand up the =tepsim= facade, so that
there is something for the Python and wasm bindings to bind to and something to
ship.

That is the right call and the state of the tree says why. =tepsim-core= is
12,081 lines and validated bit-for-bit against the Fortran over a full 48-hour
closed-loop run. =tepsim= is fourteen lines of =pub use=. =tepsim-py= is eleven
lines of doc comment, =tepsim-wasm= thirteen. Phases 7 and 8 are not blocked on
PyO3 or wasm-bindgen; they are blocked on there being no API to bind.

B-0047c (the full battery run and the seed-count decision) and B-0048 (the
report chapter) stay in Phase 5 and block nothing.

:PROPERTIES:

** Preflight
Clean and green. Fidelity 100 of 100, worst 3.521e-14 at =YP(12)= step 77,
unchanged for an eleventh iteration. Provenance 1306/2997. gfortran 15.2.0.

** Why this, out of order
The user asked what the state of the wasm app, the Python bindings and the
documentation was. The answer was: =tepsim-wasm= is thirteen lines of doc
comment, =tepsim-py= is eleven, =book/src= holds one file, and =tepsim-cli=
prints "Nothing does." Meanwhile =tepsim-core= is twelve thousand validated
lines.

Phases 7 and 8 were never blocked on PyO3 or wasm-bindgen. They were blocked on
there being no API to bind. So Phase 5 is parked at seven of twelve and this is
Phase 6's first item.

** What changed
=tepsim= is a library. =Scenario= describes a run, =Simulation= performs one,
=Run= holds the output as columns, and =tep= drives it from a terminal.

The design decision worth recording is that the facade owns *no new physics*.
Every line of the loop already existed in =tier5::run_port=, and that function
now delegates here and its body is deleted. Two transcriptions of
=temain_mod.f='s main loop is one more than this project can keep honest.

** Validation numbers
387 tests default (up from 366), 577 with =oracle= (up from 549).

The acceptance criterion is the one that matters: =Simulation= reproduces
=tier5::run_port= *bit for bit*, over nominal and =IDV(1)=, =IDV(6)=, =IDV(13)=,
=IDV(20)=, all 60 samples, all 53 channels, in both libm configurations. Since
=run_port= is bit-identical to the Fortran over 172,800 closed-loop steps, every
claim Tiers 4 and 5 established now attaches to the public API.

Regression check after the delegation: =tier5_runs= 9 passed, =tier4_closed_loop=
5, =tier5_invariants= 9, =tier5_battery= 3, all unchanged.

The warm start, asserted against the oracle bit for bit:

| vessel    | after TEINIT      | nominal literal |
|-----------+-------------------+-----------------|
| reactor   | 120.3999996050374 |           120.4 |
| separator |  80.1094039945582 |          80.109 |
| stripper  |  65.7310297718018 |          65.731 |
| mixing    |  86.1201119771066 |          86.120 |

Open loop from the CLI trips at step 11,017 (3.060 h) on =ReactorPressureHigh=,
which is exactly what B-0041's Tier 4 test measured. That the two agree without
either being told about the other is the cheapest possible check that the facade
is driving the same plant.

** Surprises and decisions
*The priming measurements are never read, and that had to be checked rather
than reasoned.* =run_port= primes the controllers with =TEINIT='s =XMEAS=; the
facade cannot, because it has no oracle. The argument that it does not matter is
that the fastest loop is =MOD(I,3)= and so nothing fires until step 3, by which
time step 2 has produced real measurements. That is a load-bearing argument, so
it is now a test: a run primed from zeros is bit-identical to one primed from
=TEINIT='s 41 non-zero values.

*Two of my own claims were wrong and measurement caught both.*

I wrote that =Scenario::steps()= rounds because =48.0 / (1.0/3600.0)= is
172799.99999999997 and truncation would lose a step. It is not: that quotient is
exactly 172800, and truncation is fine for every whole hour up to 200. The
rounding is still right, but the case that discriminates is *4.1 hours*, where
the quotient is 14759.99999999999818. The test now uses it, so it has teeth
rather than a plausible story.

And a guard written =!(hours > 0.0)= to catch NaN reads better, and lints
cleaner, as an explicit =hours.is_nan() || hours <= 0.0=.

*A trip freezes rather than ends, and that is a deliberate default in the API
now rather than an implementation detail.* =Outcome::Tripped= is reported the
moment it happens and the run keeps producing samples, because
=teprob.f:807-811= zeroes the derivatives rather than stopping and those
constant rows are in every published dataset of a tripped run. The Class C fix
is one field away and still off.

*Ground truth is the thing the original cannot give.* A published TEP dataset is
a matrix and a filename, so every detection-delay number in the literature is
computed against an onset the author assumed. The facade records which
disturbances were active and how long each had been, which incidentally makes
delta D-011 visible in the data itself: a "fault-free" run is fault-free for
eight hours and then carries =IDV(12)=, and now the labels say so.

** Next
The facade unblocks both remaining directions, and the user should choose.

*Phase 7, PyO3.* The API sketch in =PLAN.org= maps onto what now exists almost
one to one: =tep.Simulation(scenario=tep.Scenario.baseline(seed=42, hours=48))=
is =Simulation::new(Scenario::baseline().with_seed(42.0).with_hours(48.0))=. The
columnar =Run= is already the right shape for a zero-copy numpy view. What is
missing is the =Recorder= trait (B-0055), which matters for streaming but not
for =run()=.

*B-0053, the integrators.* RK4 and Dormand-Prince, and this is where the
three-phase split finally pays. B-0012 established that =TEFUNC= is not a pure
function of =(t, y)=; a multi-stage integrator calling it four times per step
would advance the disturbance walks four times and draw noise four times. The
split makes RK4 correct, and nothing has yet demonstrated it.

*What is still missing entirely*: the mdBook (=book/src= has one file and no
=book.toml=), the scenario engine, recorder sinks beyond CSV, and the browser
app, which additionally needs =trunk= installed.

Phase 5's B-0047c (the full battery, about 90 minutes of compute, plus the
seed-count decision the power finding forces) and B-0048 (the report chapter)
are unfinished and block nothing.

** Preflight
Clean and green throughout. Fidelity 100 of 100, worst 3.521e-14 at =YP(12)=
step 77, unchanged. gfortran 15.2.0.

** How this round was run
The user asked for a team of agents on parallel activities. Four ran in git
worktrees on disjoint crates: PyO3 bindings, the mdBook, the Tier 6 detectors,
and the wasm bindings. I took the integrators and the recorder sinks in the
main tree and did every merge and all the bookkeeping centrally, so that
=BACKLOG.org=, =LOG.org= and =book/src/deltas.md= had exactly one writer.

*The one thing that nearly went badly wrong.* Three of the four worktrees were
created at =8fd135c=, one hundred and thirty-three commits behind. At that
commit =crates/tepsim= is fourteen lines of =pub use=, =tepsim-stats= does not
exist, and =book/= does not exist. The documentation agent noticed on its own
and fast-forwarded. The statistics agent noticed and fast-forwarded. The wasm
agent did not, and had written a complete implementation against a tree with no
facade in it before I caught it and sent it back; it discarded everything and
rewrote. Two of its files were a hand-transcribed =IDV= table and a placeholder
sample engine, both of which already existed in validated form on =main=.

The lesson is cheap to state and was expensive to learn: *check the base commit
of every worktree before the agents start*, not after.

** What landed
| item   | what                                    | where |
|--------+-----------------------------------------+-------|
| B-0053 | Euler, RK4, Dormand-Prince 5(4)         | me    |
| B-0055 | Recorder, five sinks, and =run_into=      | me    |
| B-0058 | PyO3 bindings and an abi3 wheel          | agent |
| B-0059 | The mdBook, thirteen chapters            | agent |
| B-0049 | Jacobi, PCA, T-squared, SPE, DPCA        | agent |
| B-0060 | wasm-bindgen bindings and a worker       | agent |

** Validation numbers
Tests: *411 default, 601 with =oracle=*, up from 387 and 577. Plus 47 Python
tests and 105 Node checks that =xtask ci= does not yet run (B-0058a).

*Integrators.* Orders against =y' = y=: Euler 0.993, RK4 3.970, dopri5 4.937.
The walks advance once per outer step whichever method runs, and all three
update the gas analyser exactly 9 times over an hour.

*The result about the original.* Worst relative difference over all 53
channels, at one hour: euler-rk4 1.113e-2, rk4-dopri5 1.496e-6, euler-dopri5
1.112e-2. Two independent high-order methods agree with each other four orders
of magnitude better than either agrees with Euler, so Euler is the outlier and
the published TEP data carries about one percent of integration error against
an accurate solution of the same equations. It saturates rather than
accumulating, because the controllers hold the setpoints.

*Python.* Wheel 328,137 bytes, =cp39-abi3=, imports in 1.4 ms. 2 h run 32.0 ms,
48 h run 851 ms, and eight 48-hour runs across eight threads in 0.997 s against
5.912 s serial, a 5.93x speedup from releasing the GIL. One copy at
construction then views: channel accessors share memory with the matrix and
every array is read-only. *Verified independently of the agent*: the open-loop
plant trips at step 11,017 (3.060 h) on reactor pressure high, identical to the
Rust CLI and to the Tier 4 test.

*wasm.* Deployable total *45,015 bytes gzipped* against a 1.5 MB budget. The
raw cargo artifact is 450,564 bytes and gzips to 113,888; wasm-bindgen strips
the describe scaffolding to 87 KB raw, 35 KB gzipped. Determinism digest
=c8a26889992f1719=, identical on aarch64 and wasm32 across three profiles, and
exported glue-free so any runtime can check it. The wasm half of Tier 9 is
automatable today.

*Detectors.* Eigensolver reconstructs to 8.2e-15 at n=30, recovers the 1-D
Laplacian's published spectrum to 8.0e-15, and gets Hilbert 8x8's smallest
eigenvalue as +1.111539e-10 at condition 1.53e10. F quantile matches all ten
published table entries. T-squared calibration: 4936 exceedances in 100,000 at
a nominal 0.05, 0.6 standard errors out.

*Book.* Thirteen chapters, 2,772 lines, =mdbook build= and =mdbook test= clean.

** Surprises and decisions
*Two wall-clock assertions failed, and both were measuring the machine.* The
autocorrelation test asserted the transform was faster than the definition;
with four agents compiling in parallel, the ordering flipped. The energy
distance test was worse: it discarded the naive call's result, so a release
build eliminated it as dead code, timed 0.0 s, and failed by concluding the
naive form was affordable. Both now assert *scaling* instead. Quadrupling the
data costs the definition x13.7 and the fast form x3.6; ten times the lags cost
the direct autocorrelation x9.65 and the transform x0.64.

A timing test that passes on an idle machine is measuring the machine. Where
the claim is about complexity, measure complexity.

*A citation block had been wrong since B-0011.* The documentation agent, told
to cite a line range for every factual claim, checked them instead of copying
them, and found every =teprob.f= line number in =stream.rs='s mapping
derivation off by one: =FTM(1)= is at 565 not 564, =XMEAS(10)= at 688 not 687.
=flows.rs= and =measurements.rs= had them right, which is how it surfaced. The
mapping was correct; the citations were not, which is worse than useless in the
one module whose entire purpose is to be the checkable record of it. Now fixed
and pinned: a test reads the vendored Fortran and checks each cited line says
what the prose claims, plus a second test that the neighbouring lines do *not*
match, so a block shifted wholesale cannot pass.

Prose is not compiled. Anything load-bearing written in a comment needs a test.

*Both binding agents found bugs by breaking their own work on purpose.* The
wasm run digest did not cover the packed row buffer, so a transposition would
have shipped wrong numbers under a matching digest. The statistics agent ran 32
mutations and two survived: a Jacobi rotation that evaluated the annihilated
pivot instead of assigning zero, and a correlation grouped as
=sqrt(a)*sqrt(b)= rather than =sqrt(a*b)=, whose difference is one ulp on
exactly collinear columns and zero elsewhere. It closed both, and noted that
one of the existing tests claimed "bit for bit" in its name while asserting
=< 1e-14=.

*A seam the facade does not have.* A mid-run fault toggle cannot reach a
running =Simulation=: the scenario is taken at construction and handed to the
driver there. The wasm agent declined to invent one, on the grounds that a run
reachable only through the browser is a run nobody can reproduce. That is the
right call and the seam belongs in B-0054.

** Next
B-0054, the scenario engine, is the last Phase 6 item not blocked on a
sign-off, and it now has a second reason to exist: the live-fault seam above.

What remains, honestly. Phase 5: B-0047c (the full battery, about 90 minutes,
plus the seed-count decision the power finding forces), B-0048 (the report
chapter, generated rather than hand-written), B-0049a (CVA and published
detection rates), B-0050 (the cross-source experiment), B-0051 (Tier 7).
Phase 6: B-0054, B-0057 (quirk deltas, needs B-0047c). Phase 7: B-0058a (the
wheel matrix, and =xtask= running the Python tests at all). Phase 8: the Leptos
app, which needs =trunk=, still not installed. Phase 9: B-0059a.

Two items remain blocked on the user: B-0025b (should a shutdown end the run)
and B-0040a (should the driver's IDV(12) stay the default).

** Preflight
Green throughout. Fidelity 100 of 100, worst 3.521e-14 at =YP(12)= step 77,

** What landed
Four agents in worktrees (Tier 7, Tier 6 cross-source, the wheel matrix and CI,
the browser app) and the scenario engine here. Every worktree was again created
48 commits stale; this time every agent was told to check and fast-forward
first, and every one of them did. That instruction is now the cheapest possible
insurance and belongs in any future agent brief.

** Validation numbers
505 tests default (up from 411), 713 with =oracle= (up from 601), 47 Python, 31
Node.

*Tier 6, the equivalence claim itself.* 20 faults by 20 test seeds by 48 hours,
860 runs, 47 minutes. All 328 comparisons passed while gating. Worst
permutation p-value *0.619* against a gate of 0.05 and a floor of 1/21.
Training source made no difference to any metric: both sources fit 14
components at 90% variance with identical limits.

| detector | fault   | metric        | Fortran-eval | Rust-eval | within-source | p     |
|----------+---------+---------------+--------------+-----------+---------------+-------|
| PCA      | IDV(14) | T2 rate       |       0.4342 |    0.4278 | +-0.097       | 0.952 |
| PCA      | IDV(14) | T2 delay      |        34.65 |     32.05 | +-24.1        | 0.762 |
| PCA      | IDV(19) | T2 delay      |       486.75 |    514.15 | +-220         | 0.619 |
| DPCA(2)  | IDV(19) | T2 rate       |       0.3102 |    0.3118 | +-0.111       | 1.000 |

The number that makes this more than a table: over 168 rate comparisons the
median half-split spread was 0.340 separation units, so two halves of the
Fortran's *own* twenty seeds typically differ by fifteen percentage points of
detection rate. The largest cross-source difference anywhere was 0.64 points.

*Tier 7.* 44 files inventoried from the bytes. Trajectory reproduction fails;
the operating point matches (=d00= median mean gap 0.037 published sd, median
sd ratio 1.082). Under the evidence-supported reading, 38 of 42 representable
files land at KS median 0.044 to 0.14.

*Browser app.* 74,010 bytes gzipped, 4.8% of the budget. 40,662x to 85,529x
real time against a 100x target.

*Scenario engine.* Half of =IDV(1)= puts mean =XMEAS(1)= at 0.503 of the way
from none to full. Magnitude exactly 1.0 is bit-identical to the faithful path.

** Surprises and decisions
*The published datasets were not made with the driver as shipped.* This is the
round's real finding and it is about the original, not the port.
=temain_mod.f:367= forces =IDV(12)= on at eight hours, and the published files
carry it only in =d12= and =d12_te=. Two independent predictions agree:
=d12_te='s spread jumps at row 160 by at least 5.3x what =d00_te='s does on
every channel =IDV(12)= drives, and keeping the line inflates the port's spread
against =d00= by up to 9.9x against 1.55x for replacing it. Removing it roughly
triples agreement across the whole table.

The agent did not change the port's default, and was right not to. The shipped
source *does* force it, and reproducing the source is a separate goal from
reproducing the files. But the only argument anyone had for the current default
was that every long published dataset carries it, and that argument is now
known to be false. B-0040a and the new B-0051a should be answered together.

*The recorded seeds carry no information.* Ranked first on 10 of 42 files
against a four-decoy null where chance gives 8.4. Whatever generated those
files, it was not this source at those seeds, and no protocol choice recovers
it. Seven other unknowns are enumerated in the test rather than hidden,
including that the training protocol is documented nowhere and that the output
format's five significant figures destroy bit-exactness at the source.

*Tier 7 cost 35 minutes of CPU in the debug gate.* Its full sweep is 42 files by
five seeds by 172,800 steps: 321 seconds in release, far worse unoptimised. It
now takes a =TEP_TIER7= selector, and the smoke set is chosen to span the
outcomes rather than to be the first few: =d00= the fault-free point, =d01= an
ordinary step, =d06= a shutdown freeze, and the =d12= pair that genuinely
carries =IDV(12)=. 6.9 s.

The general lesson, now learned three times: an expensive tier needs its size
selector *in the same commit that adds it*, not after it has been merged into
the gate.

*A third timing assertion had to go.* The energy distance's scaling check is now
release-only, because an unoptimised build's constants dominate to the point
where the O(n log n) form grows *faster* than the quadratic one over a fourfold
range: x24.5 against x19.1 in debug, x3.7 against x15.1 in release.

*Two agents corrected their own designs mid-flight, and said so.* The Tier 6
agent found that its half-split null was on the wrong scale (a 10-vs-10 split
against a 20-vs-20 comparison makes the null about sqrt(2) too wide, drifting
every verdict toward passing) and that six seeds admit only ten distinct splits,
so twenty repeats of ten is not a p-value. It also reported that its first
control test failed correctly and it re-specified rather than weakened it: a
0.5 sd shift in one variable does not move T-squared at all, because it lands in
the residual subspace, and 2 sd is the smallest single-variable shift this
detector notices.

*Tier 6 cannot see variance collapse, and says so.* =PLAN.org= names the Python
port's real failure as reactor pressure sd 8.10 against 61.48. Shrinking a
spread can only *lower* an alarm rate, and there is little room below a few
percent, so a false alarm rate is blind to it. That is a measured limitation
rather than a defect: Tier 5's TOST on log variances is the gate for
second-moment defects, and the test says so rather than putting a gate where it
cannot bite.

*trunk was installed and deliberately not used.* Its bundled binaryen 123
rejects the module outright, because rustc 1.97 emits =memory.copy= and that
build needs =--enable-bulk-memory-opt=. With a JavaScript front end everything
trunk does is =cargo build= plus =wasm-bindgen= plus =wasm-opt=, which
=build.sh= does in three commands with no build-time toolchain download.

*A green Python gate could have been testing the wrong package.* The CI agent
added an import-site probe asserting =tepsim.__file__= is inside the throwaway
venv, and reported that it caught a real bug in its own first draft.

** Next
Everything that remains is either a sign-off, a long run, or work that depends
on one of those.

Three questions are the user's: B-0025b (should a shutdown end the run rather
than freeze the plant), B-0040a (should the driver's forced =IDV(12)= stay the
default) and the new B-0051a (does Tier 7's evidence change the answer to

B-0047c is the full Tier 5 battery, about ninety minutes, plus the seed-count
decision its power finding forces: =PLAN.org='s margin of a tenth of a standard
deviation on the mean needs about 255 seeds at the nominal plant, not 100.

B-0048 generates the validation chapter from =xtask= rather than hand-writing

And nothing has yet run =.github/workflows/wheels.yml=. The free-threaded and

against a free-threaded interpreter, and the Emscripten job has the most moving
parts and none exercised.

** The headline: the full Tier 5 battery
21 scenarios by 100 seeds by 48 hours on both sources. 4200 runs, 171 minutes.

*Nineteen of twenty-one pass every gate.* Paired mean power 0.0000 to 0.0086,
so the confidence interval is under one percent of the equivalence margin.
Frobenius cross 2.8e-5 to 1.9e-2 against within-source maxima of 5 to 8. Every
calibrated statistic at p = 1.0000 on every scenario.

*Two do not, and they are exactly the ones that should be suspected.* =IDV(14)=
and =IDV(19)= are sticking-valve faults, and the channels that fail are
precisely the valves they stick.

| scenario | paired power | undecided                | worst gap | frobenius |
|----------+--------------+--------------------------+-----------+-----------|
| IDV(14)  |       1.4271 | XMV(10), XMV(11), XMV(5) | 1.114 mar |     3.002 |
| IDV(19)  |       1.2117 | XMV(5)                   | 0.702 mar |     2.176 |

=teprob.f:801= moves a valve only when the command differs from its position by
more than the stick threshold. That is a discontinuous branch on a
floating-point comparison, and with the vendored libm an ULP of difference
eventually lands the two implementations on opposite sides of it: one valve
moves and the other does not for a whole control period.

*Under =libm-system= both scenarios are bit-identical, 0.000e0.* So it is
transcendental rounding meeting a discrete decision, the same mechanism B-0021
and B-0022 found in the strict comparisons, and not an error in the algebra.
The margin was not widened, the port was not changed, and B-0047d is BLOCKED
with the numbers and three options.

** The seed count, resolved by pairing rather than by more compute
B-0047b measured the unpaired mean test needing 237 to 460 seeds. The right
answer was not more seeds: the two sources are run at the *same* seeds, so each
seed's difference is an observation and the wander both sources share cancels
out of it. Measured on a fixture with a shared offset of order one and a true
difference of 0.01, unpaired power is 7.75 and paired power 0.0000. The
unpaired figure is kept and reported, because it is the conservative statement
and the right one for a comparison that cannot be seed-matched.

** Validation numbers
D-007, over 10 h: the open-loop plant trips at step 11,017 and the fix discards
69.5% of the run; =IDV(6)= trips at 21,037 and loses 42.0%; five closed-loop
faults never trip and the fix is exactly a no-op. Up to the trip the two
settings are bit-identical.

D-011: worst paired mean shift 0.32 margins on =XMEAS(7)=, diluted about
fivefold because =IDV(12)= is active for under two of the ten hours. The
undiluted figure is B-0040's 1.092e-1.

Detection rates reproduce the literature's split. Faults 3, 9, 15 hard (T2
0.031, 0.036, 0.055); faults 1, 2, 6, 7, 8, 12, 13, 14 easy (0.95 to 1.00).
Every hard fault alarms at most 1.89x fault-free data, every easy one at least
30.6x. No published number is quoted as a target, because the agent did not
have the papers and declined to write down remembered rates.

CVA: =B = I= bit-for-bit against the standard solver, 1000 pencils against the
characteristic polynomial at 8.3e-16, a construction giving exactly 3/5 and
5/13.

** Surprises and decisions
*The backlog's plan for B-0057 was wrong, and finding out why was the item.* It
assumed both Class C deltas could be measured by running the Tier 5 battery
with the fix on and off. That works for D-011 and cannot work for D-007: every
battery statistic compares two ensembles of the same shape, and the fix makes a
tripped run *stop*, so a Kolmogorov-Smirnov statistic between a 200-sample run
and a 61-sample one is measuring the truncation. D-007's delta is measured in
the terms the fix actually changes.

*The delta register's two halves had disagreed since B-0008.* The generated
cross-check failed on its first run: D-008 was documented and no =@delta=
marker named it, although the doc comment above =const FAST= said so in prose.
Prose is not compiled. Marker added, and the check is a gate step from the same
commit, which is the only honest order.

*The oracle feature had never been linted.* =xtask ci= ran clippy on default
features only, so everything behind =#[cfg(feature = "oracle")]= was
unlinted: the Tier 2, 5, 6 and 7 machinery, every differential, the harness.
Ten real lints in the library, including a manual clamp that must stay one
because =CONSHAND= normalises negative zero and two Euler steps that must not
fuse. It is a gate step now.

*A fourth timing assertion was removed rather than revised.* The energy
distance's had already been through three revisions: optimised away as dead
code, then an ordering that flipped under load, then a scaling check that still
flaked with builds competing for cores. The claim is that one algorithm is
O(n log n) and the other O(n*m), which is true by inspection and which no
wall-clock measurement on a shared machine is evidence for. It records the
numbers now and asserts only correctness.

*A =debug_assert= test had been failing in release since it was written*, unseen
because =xtask ci= runs the workspace tests in debug.

The two tiers that had no harness now have one. Tier 8 fuzzes states against

** Validation numbers

*** Tier 8, differential fuzzing (B-0062)
5,000,000 tuples at seed =0x7E2_0062=: *2,551,618 compared (51.0%)*, 2,263,855
frozen by the shutdown, 184,526 lost to =TESUB2= falling through, *0* where the
port failed while the Fortran converged. 240,773,700 component comparisons, 0
non-finite on either side, worst agreeing error *4.674e-13 of scale*. Default
=TEP_TIER8= size 400 tuples, 0.74 s.

Sensitivity measured rather than assumed: an injected error of *1e-11* of a
balance's scale is found, *1e-13* is not, both asserted. A term behind
=teprob.f:615= (=TCC > 170 C=), reached by 25 of 10,222 compared tuples, shrinks
from 18 knobs to 1 in 21 evaluations.

*One counterexample in five million.* =fuzz#863105=, shrunk from 22 knobs to 3:
nominal state, =IDV(13)=, =G=985491900=, =TIME=973.308134169278=. =YP(9)= misses
by *4.607e-12 of the scale of its terms* against a 1e-12 gate. Attributed, not

IDV(13)'s kinetic walk leaves the drift at 3e7 and the rates at 1.1e10, so one
ULP of =exp= becomes 1.2e-6 against balance terms of 5.2e4. No Tier 2 pool state
has a drift factor above about one. The gate was not touched; =TEP_TIER8=full=

*** Regression
Fidelity, provenance and delta counts all unchanged end to end. The six Tier 9
digests are unchanged *after* both default flips, which is independent evidence
that the flips changed no arithmetic: they are pinned to the reproducing
configuration in =tier9::pinned=.

** Surprises and decisions

*The published files are not in Fortran =E13.5= form.* The driver writes
=FORMAT(1X,E13.5)= to a dozen separate units, four values a line. The
distributed files are one 52-column row per sample in =%16.7e=, so they were
reassembled and reformatted by something that is not in the repository. Five
significant digits of content padded to seven decimals is the fingerprint.

*And the layout was got wrong first.* Three spaces plus =%.7e= reproduces every
positive value exactly and is one byte too wide on every line containing a
negative. They are fixed sixteen-column fields; every published line is exactly
832 bytes. The test that settles it takes a shipped line apart into numbers and
requires the formatter to give the bytes back.

*The freeze's counter-argument does not survive contact.* The case for freezing
was continuity of the published record. The user's question was better: the
plant cannot be restarted either way, so the freeze preserves no option. It only
withholds the fact that the run is over, and it does so for three quarters of
=d06.dat=.

*Pinning the Tier 9 cases mattered more than it looked.* Written a day earlier,
the table inherited =Scenario::baseline()=. Both defaults then moved. Had they
not been pinned, two of the six digests would have shifted and the failure would
have read as a cross-platform disagreement rather than as the deliberate change
it was.

*A lint that nothing ran.* =clippy::suboptimal_flops= is on for the workspace
and fires only under =libm-system=, because =no_std= has no =f64::mul_add= to
suggest, and no gate linted that configuration. Eighteen findings in
=tepsim-core=. Taking the suggestion would have been wrong: =mul_add= is fused,
one rounding where the Fortran does two. Allowed with that reason, and the
missing clippy pass added to =xtask ci=.

*Three book tutorials were wrong within minutes of the flip, and nothing could
have told me.* They carried =rust,ignore= listings and transcripts pasted
beside them, so =scheduling-a-fault.md= went on showing =active [4, 12]= from a
forced =IDV(12)= that no longer happens, and =injecting-a-fault.md='s closing
block demonstrated a contrast that had stopped existing. Found by grep. They are
now compiled examples pinned to their pages, and compiling them immediately
turned up an =unwrap()= in the book that the workspace's =clippy::unwrap_used=
would always have rejected. =a-detector.md= was unaffected, which was verified
rather than assumed. B-0068.

*The plant's own default had to diverge from the scenario's, and the gate is
what found it.* Flipping =QuirkFixes::default= moved =Plant::default= with it,
and Tier 2's =all_fifty_derivatives_match_the_fortran_over_all_three_pools=
failed on the thirteen tripping states: un-zeroed derivatives against a frozen
Fortran. The fix is not to patch the harness. =trip_ends_the_run= is a promise
about what the *caller* does after a trip, and a bare =Plant= has no run to end,
so a caller who took the fix and ignored the flag would integrate straight
through a trip, which is neither behaviour. =Plant= now defaults to
=QuirkFixes::faithful= and =tepsim::Simulation= assigns the scenario's over it,
so the facade is unaffected. =the_plants_own_default_is_faithful= asserts the
divergence, because the next person to tidy that constructor would otherwise
reconverge them.

*The scenario text was re-based without a version bump*, deliberately. Every
field is written out explicitly, so a link written before the sign-off still
names the scenario it always named; what changed is only which scenario the
defaults render to. Asserted rather than assumed.

** Next
The remaining gaps are all things this machine cannot do rather than things
nobody has done.

- *Tier 9 has no x86-64 leg and no real browser.* Both are one command on the
  right machine: the table is committed constants, not a value computed twice,
  so =cargo xtask tier9= on an x86-64 host completes the claim with no code
  change. A headless-browser leg is what =PLAN.org= actually asks for and node
  is not a substitute for it, since a browser also exercises the wasm-bindgen
  glue and the Worker boundary.
- *=.github/workflows/wheels.yml= has never run.* Its free-threaded and Pyodide
  rows are the least certain thing in the repository.
- *Tier 8's open finding.* =fuzz#863105= is recorded and attributed, not fixed,
  because there is nothing to fix: it is an accepted one-ULP =exp= difference
  amplified by 973 hours of drift. Worth revisiting only if a second, unrelated
  counterexample appears.
- *=book/src/validation/delta-index.md= can never be stamped with its own
  commit.* =xtask deltas= writes the hash it ran at into the page, so the
  committed page is always one commit behind, and it had been carrying
  =3ba70c3-dirty= until this iteration restamped it at =c736d76=. Not wrong,
  but the stamp is the one field that cannot be right. Either drop the hash from
  the page or let the generator read =HEAD= before the bookkeeping commit; a
  small decision either way, and nobody has taken it.
- *Arrow and Parquet sinks were considered and not built.* They were on the
  previous entry's unfinished list. The right home is =tepsim-cli= behind a
  feature, never =tepsim=, which is =no_std= and compiles to wasm32 under a size
  budget; and =arrow= plus =parquet= is a large dependency tree for a project
  whose CSV path already streams and never holds a run. Worth doing when someone
  has a dataset large enough that CSV is the bottleneck, and not before. The
  same reasoning applies to =tep dataset --format=, which writes =.dat= and
  =.csv= and has room for a third arm.
- *A historian sink* was asked about and does not exist. Everything is wide
  tabular. Long-format =tag,timestamp,value,quality= needs a caller-supplied
  epoch (the core may not read a clock), a decision about what quality code a
  dead-time analyser and a frozen channel deserve, and units promoted from
  prose in =measurements.rs= to data on a channel.

* 2026-08-29 B-0074 The operating cost of a run
:PROPERTIES:
:ITEM:     B-0074
:PHASE:    10
:TIER:     11
:COMMIT:   9eaad83
:GFORTRAN: 15.2.0
:STATUS:   DONE
:END:
** Preflight
Clean and green. This is the first item of a new phase, so the preflight is the
usual one: fidelity 3.521323734565789e-14, provenance 1306 of 2997 (43.6%),
deltas 11 and 11. None of them move, because nothing in this iteration touches
the model.

** What changed
A new shipped crate, =tepsim-operations=, and the first of its three planned
pieces: what a run costs, per hour, computed from what the plant measures.

Nothing in =tepsim-core= or =tepsim-control= changed. The layer attaches through
=Action::Setpoint=, which already existed, which is the whole reason this is
additive rather than a redesign.

** Validation numbers

*** The physical rates, nominal 48 h, 960 samples, unit prices
| term       |       rate | unit  |
|------------+------------+-------|
| purge      |    386.483 | kg/h  |
| product    | 17,020.989 | kg/h  |
| compressor |    341.483 | kWh/h |
| steam      |    233.579 | kg/h  |

Raw channels at the last sample: =XMEAS(10)= 0.3392 kscmh, =XMEAS(17)= 23.8693
m3/h, =XMEAS(19)= 230.2244 kg/h, =XMEAS(20)= 340.9525 kW.

*The compressor number is the check that matters.* The canonical base case for
this process is about 341.4 kW, and 341.483 kWh/h came out of reading
=XMEAS(20)= through a conversion derived from =teprob.f:699=, with nothing
fitted to it. A channel wired to the wrong index would not land there.

*** The conversions
=the_purge_conversion_inverts_teprob_f_688= and its product twin read the
vendored Fortran, assert the line is exactly
=XMEAS(10)=FTM(10)*0.359/35.3145= (and =XMEAS(17)=FTM(13)/DLC/35.3145=), and
round-trip a molar flow to 1e-9 relative. If someone edits the conversion the
test says which Fortran line it stopped matching.

*** Structure
Nine tests. Zero prices cost exactly zero; zero flows cost exactly zero; each of
the four terms answers to its own price and to no other; the product mass rate
divided back through its own density returns =XMEAS(17)= to 1e-9; the raw
analyser readings are asserted *not* to sum to 1 before normalising, so the
normalisation is demonstrably load-bearing.

** Surprises and decisions

*The product density is not measured, and that turned out to be the interesting
part.* =XMEAS(17)= is a volumetric flow, so a mass rate needs =DLC=, which no
instrument reports. It is recoverable from the product analyser and the stripper
temperature through =thermo::liquid_density=, the plant's own correlation,
already validated against the oracle. That is a small instance of exactly what
this layer exists to do, and it arrived on the first function rather than in the
reconciliation item where it was expected.

*The analysers do not sum to 100 and must not be trusted to.*
=Composition::new= checks the sum to 1e-6 in debug builds, and noisy mole-percent
readings violate that immediately. Normalising is what an operations layer does;
the test asserts the raw sum really is out of tolerance so the normalisation
cannot quietly become decorative.

*=mul_add= is used here and is forbidden three crates away.* The workspace lint
=clippy::suboptimal_flops= is allowed in =tepsim-core= because a fused
multiply-add rounds once where the Fortran rounds twice. None of that applies to
a cost accumulation, so the suggestion is taken, and the contrast is commented at
the call site so the next person does not have to rediscover which rule applies
where.

*There is no price preset, deliberately.* The Downs and Vogel coefficients are in
the paper and not in =reference/=. Typing them here would be a constant nobody in
the repository could check, which is the precise failure mode
"constants are asserted, never retyped" exists to prevent. Recorded as an open
question in =PLAN.org=: vendoring the paper's cost table with a checksum would
resolve it.

** Next
*B-0075, data reconciliation.* It is the piece with the best evidence available
and it is now unblocked. Both halves come out of the source rather than out of a
modelling choice: the constraints are the balances =tepsim-core= already
computes and the Tier 2 invariants already check, and the weights are =XNS(i)=,
the noise standard deviations in =teprob.f=. And the scoring is better than the
real problem ever gets, because the simulator knows the true state, so
"reconciliation reduces error against the truth" is directly measurable rather
than argued from residuals.

** Preflight
Fidelity 100 of 100, worst 3.521323734565789e-14 at =YP(12)= step 77, unchanged
throughout. Provenance and the delta register unchanged: nothing here touches
the model.

** What changed
The validation chapters now *show* the similarity they used to only tabulate.
Seven SVGs, generated by a new =xtask/src/plot.rs= with =format!=, no plotting
dependency, inlined so they follow both mdBook themes.

** Validation numbers
| tier | comparisons | bit-identical | worst |
|------+-------------+---------------+-------|
| 1 | 14 | 13 | 1.597e-5, the deliberate f64 offset |
| 2 | 141 | 115 | platform libm 1,535,225 / 1,535,225 at 0 ULP |
| 3 | 6 | 6 | all zero, 120,000 draws at 0 ULP |
| 4 | 42 runs | 21 | 2.450e-5 of =XNS(i)= |
| 5 | smoke | n/a | Frobenius cross ~4e-12 vs within-source null ~1e1 |

Tier 5 at smoke size: 3 scenarios by 4 seeds by 2 h, 14 s. The full battery was
not re-run; the figure says so on its face.

** Surprises and decisions

*The calibration plot is the one to show someone who asks whether this works.*
Cross-source against the within-source null, log-log, diagonal is equality. The
port sits far above the diagonal, which says it differs from the Fortran by less
than the Fortran differs from itself across seeds. And the battery's own
ten-sigma positive control sits below the diagonal on the same axes, so the
picture carries its own proof that it can see a difference when there is one.

*A picture found something a table had hidden.* =tier1 heat QUS= is at
*9.572e-13* against a 1e-12 gate, four percent of the way from passing to
failing. A table of maxima per tier cannot show which single comparison is
nearest the line; a strip plot shows it immediately.

*The generated SVGs were not well-formed XML*, and the book did not reveal it.
An XML comment may not contain =--=, and every generating command has =--tiers=
in it, so the provenance comment was malformed. mdBook inlines the figure and an
HTML parser tolerates it, but mdBook also copies the =.svg= to the site, where
opening it directly was a parse error. Provenance now lives in an SVG
=<metadata>= element and there is a test.

*Markers are coloured by value, never by name.* A marker goes orange because it
is past a gate, not because the test was recognised, so a genuine regression and
a deliberate positive control are drawn identically and the caption names the
out-of-gate comparisons from the data. A figure that knew which points were
supposed to be bad would not be evidence.

** Next
The two figures worth adding next both need =crates/= changes, which is why they
are not here. A Tier 5 forest plot of each variable's paired mean difference over
its equivalence margin needs =battery.rs= to print the difference and not only
the TOST verdict, and an ECDF overlay needs some test to emit per-sample data.
The first is the more valuable: it would put all 21 scenarios by 53 variables on
one axis against the margin, which is the claim Tier 5 actually makes.

* 2026-08-29 B-0079 The Tier 5 forest plot: every variable against its margin
:PROPERTIES:
:ITEM:     B-0079
:PHASE:    9
:TIER:     5
:COMMIT:   f8a4419
:GFORTRAN: 15.2.0
:STATUS:   DONE
:END:
** Preflight
Fidelity 100 of 100, worst 3.521323734565789e-14 at =YP(12)= step 77. Unchanged;
nothing here touches the model.

** What changed
The figure B-0078 could not draw. =tier5_battery= now prints a machine-readable
line per variable with the paired difference, the margin, and why the moment
gate does or does not apply; =plot::forest= draws it.

The calibration figure says the two sources are closer to each other than the
reference is to itself. This one says *how much room is left*, which neither
that figure nor a TOST verdict can: a p-value reports that a test did not
reject, not by what distance.

** Validation numbers
Smoke battery, 3 scenarios by 4 seeds by 2 h, 159 variable-measurements.

| quantity | value |
|----------+-------|
| worst gated difference / margin | *5.590e-12* |
| gated variables | 156 of 159 |
| exempt (constant reference) | 3, all =XMV(12)=, the agitator |
| markers reaching the gate | 0 |

Eleven orders of magnitude of headroom on the worst variable.

** Surprises and decisions

*The figure shipped with the exact bug its backlog item warned against, and the
item is why it was caught.* A constant reference has a margin of zero, so
=ratio()= is undefined, and the first drawing skipped those points while the
legend still advertised a marker for them. Three of 159 silently absent, and a
denominator a reader would have counted wrongly. Written down as a requirement
before any code, checked afterwards, and it still took actually rendering the
figure and noticing the legend promised a marker that was nowhere on the canvas.

Constant variables are now drawn in the zero lane, with a tooltip saying they
have no ratio and why. =a_constant_variable_is_drawn_rather_than_dropped= pins
it, and was mutation-tested by restoring the skipping form: it fails with "a
marker went missing".

*The axis is set by the gated points alone.* A stuck valve sits outside its
margin by construction, and letting it into the scale would stretch the axis and
make every gated point look artificially close to zero. That is flattering the
result with layout. =the_forest_scale_ignores_the_variables_it_does_not_gate=
asserts the gated marker keeps its exact x position when a stuck one is added.

** Next
The figure is drawn at smoke size, because the full battery is about three
hours. Running it once at full size and committing that figure would make it the
strongest artifact in the book: 21 scenarios by 53 variables, 1,113 markers, all
of them against the margin they are actually gated on. Nothing else needs to
change to do it, only the time.

* 2026-08-29 B-0080 Rieth-shaped ensembles from the CLI
:PROPERTIES:
:ITEM:     B-0080
:PHASE:    6
:TIER:     none
:COMMIT:   pending
:GFORTRAN: 15.2.0
:STATUS:   DONE
:END:
** Preflight
Fidelity 100 of 100, 3.521323734565789e-14 at =YP(12)= step 77. Unchanged.

** What changed
=tep dataset --layout rieth --runs N=, which produces the shape of
=rieth-2017-addit=: many runs per fault, one long-format file per split with
=faultNumber=, =simulationRun= and =sample= beside the 52 variables.

That dataset, not the original 44 files, is what most current machine-learning
work on this process is trained against, and the CLI could not produce its shape
at all.

** Validation numbers
| | training | testing |
|---+---+---|
| hours | 25 | 48 |
| rows | 500 | 960 |
| onset | 1 h, row 20 | 8 h, row 160 |
| normal / faulty | 20 / 480 | 160 / 800 |

Onset verified in generated data rather than only in the scenario: =XMEAS(1)=
has sd *0.01202* over the 20 rows before the training onset and *0.24175* over
the 80 after it. 21,000 seeds asserted distinct and reproducible.

** Surprises and decisions

*Their training protocol is not ours, and the two are easy to confuse.* Both are
25 hours and both split twenty rows against four hundred and eighty. Ours runs
with the fault live from step zero and discards the first hour, which is the
only hypothesis that explains =d00.dat='s 500 rows against the others' 480.
Theirs schedules the fault at hour one and keeps all 500 rows, so the first
twenty are genuinely fault-free. There is a test whose whole purpose is to state
that they differ.

*I nearly asserted something about the generator's period that is false.* The
dataset describes its runs as using independent, non-overlapping seeds, and I
started to reason that at 500 runs by 21 faults this cannot hold, because a
multiplicative generator mod 2^32 has period at most 2^30 and a single 48-hour
run draws about 8e7 numbers. A cycle search settled it instead: *no return to
the starting state within 2e9 states*. The reason is already documented in
=rng.rs=, that =G * 9228907= exceeds 2^53 for most states, so the modulus is
inexact and this is not the clean generator the arithmetic assumed. The claim
was wrong and so was its negation; the code now promises only what it can
deliver, which is that the seeds are distinct, spread and reproducible.

*A doc link that resolved to the wrong scope.* =///= on =pub mod rieth= is
resolved in the *parent* module's scope, so =super::File= inside it meant
=tepsim::File= and not =tepsim::published::File=. Caught by =cargo doc -D
warnings= in the gate, which is exactly what that step is for.

** Next
The obvious follow-on is throughput. A full-scale run is 21,000 simulations and
about three and a half hours single-threaded. Runs are independent and a run is
a pure function of its scenario, so =std::thread= would take it to well under an
hour with no new dependency and no risk to determinism.
